The proof is almost the same as Lemma II.2 of
[
17
]
. We omit the details here for simplicity.
∎

Denote by

(2.7)

\displaystyle F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}L_{\alpha}(f)^{\sharp}(s,x,\xi)ds,\hskip 9.24994pt\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\},

\displaystyle F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}L_{\alpha}(f)^{\sharp}(s,x,\xi,I)ds,\hskip 9.24994pt\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}\,,

where the operator
L_{\alpha}(f)
is defined in (
1.37
).

Lemma 2.10
.

Assume that

\displaystyle L_{\alpha}(f)

\displaystyle\in L^{1}((0,T)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}),\quad\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\},

\displaystyle L_{\alpha}(f)

\displaystyle\in L^{1}((0,T)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)),\quad\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\},

for all
R,T<\infty
.
f
is a mild solution of (
1.1
) if and only if the following conclusions hold:

(1)

For all
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\},\ 0<s<t<\infty
, one has

(2.8)

\displaystyle f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi)-f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(s,x,\xi)\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(s)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}Q^{+}_{\alpha}(f,f)^{\sharp}(s,x,\xi)\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\sigma)))d\sigma\quad a.e.\ x,\xi;

(2)

For all
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\},\ 0<s<t<\infty
, one has

(2.9)

\displaystyle f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)-f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(s,x,\xi,I)\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(s)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}Q^{+}_{\alpha}(f,f)^{\sharp}(s,x,\xi,I)\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\sigma)))d\sigma\quad a.e.\ x,\xi,I.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we only consider the case
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
. Assume that
f
is a mild solution of (
1.1
). Then
Q^{\pm}_{\alpha}(f,f)^{\sharp}(t,x,\xi,I)\in L^{1}_{loc}(0,+\infty)
for almost all
(x,\xi,I)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
and

f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)-f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(s,x,\xi,I)=\int_{s}^{t}Q_{\alpha}(f,f)^{\sharp}(\sigma,x,\xi,I)d\sigma\quad(\forall 0<s<t<\infty).

Therefore, for such
(x,\xi,I)
, one has

(2.10)

\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t}f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)+f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}L_{\alpha}(f)^{\sharp}(t,x,\xi,I)=Q^{+}_{\alpha}(f,f)^{\sharp}(t,x,\xi,I)\quad a.e.\ t>0.

Note that for almost all
(x,\xi,I)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
,
F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)
is absolutely continuous with respect to
t
. From multiplying (
2.10
) by
\mathrm{exp}(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I))
, we then find

\displaystyle f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)-f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(s,x,\xi,I)\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(s)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}Q^{+}_{\alpha}(f,f)^{\sharp}(\sigma,x,\xi,I)\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\sigma)))d\sigma\quad a.e.\ x,\xi,I.

Conversely, by (
2.9
),
f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)
is absolutely continuous with respect to
t
for almost all
(x,\xi,I)
and

\displaystyle f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)\mathrm{exp}(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I))-f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(s,x,\xi,I)\mathrm{exp}(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(s,x,\xi,I))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}Q^{+}_{\alpha}(f,f)^{\sharp}\mathrm{exp}(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\sigma,x,\xi,I))d\sigma\quad a.e.\ x,\xi,I.

Fixing
s
and taking derivatives for
t
, one has

\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t}f^{\sharp}(t,x,\xi,I)+f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}L_{\alpha}(f)^{\sharp}(t,x,\xi,I)=Q^{+}_{\alpha}(f,f)^{\sharp}(t,x,\xi,I)\quad a.e.\ t,x,\xi,I.

According to the above proof procedure, we have that
f
is a mild solution of (
1.1
). The proof of Lemma
2.10
is finished.
∎

3.
Approximate problem and global uniform bounds

In this section, we mainly construct the following approximate problem:

(3.1)

\tfrac{\partial f^{n}}{\partial t}+\xi\cdot\nabla_{x}f^{n}=\tilde{Q}_{n}(f^{n},f^{n})\,,\ f^{n}|_{t=0}=f^{n}_{0}\,,

where
f^{n}_{0}=(f_{1,0}^{n},\cdots,f^{n}_{s,0})
and
\tilde{Q}_{n}=(\tilde{Q}_{1,n},\cdots,\tilde{Q}_{s,n})
are respectively the approximated initial and nonlinear collision operator that will be explicitly constructed later. Then we will prove the existence, positivity and uniqueness of the approximate problem. Moreover, the regularity of the solution to (
3.1
) will also be verified. At the end, we shall derive the conservation laws, entropy inequality and the uniform-in-
n
bounds of the approximate problem (
3.1
).

3.1.
Approximation of initial data
f_{0}

In this subsection, we focus on constructing the approximation of the initial data
f_{0}
given in (
1.40
)-(
1.43
).

Lemma 3.1
.

Assume that
f_{\alpha,0}\in L^{1}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})_{+}
for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
satisfy (
1.42
), hence,

\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi)\cdot(1+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+|\log f_{\alpha,0}|)dxd\xi<+\infty.

Then there exists a sequence
\{f^{n}_{\alpha,0}\}_{n\geq 1}\subset\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
(the Schwartz space over
\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
) and a constant
C>0
such that

(3.2)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(1+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2})|f^{n}_{\alpha,0}-f_{\alpha,0}|dxd\xi=0\,,\ f^{n}_{\alpha,0}\geq\tfrac{1}{n}\mathrm{exp}(-\tfrac{|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}}{2})\,,

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}(1+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+|\log f^{n}_{\alpha,0}|)dxd\xi\leq C\,,

\displaystyle\limsup\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log f^{n}_{\alpha,0}dxd\xi\leq\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{\alpha,0}\log f_{\alpha,0}dxd\xi\,.

Proof.

Let
h^{n}_{\alpha}(x,\xi)=f_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi)\chi_{n}(x,\xi)
for
n\geq 1
. Here
\chi_{n}=\mathbf{1}_{\{(x,\xi):|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}\leq n^{2}\}}
. Obviously, by the Dominated Convergence Theorem,

(3.3)

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(1+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2})|h^{n}_{\alpha}-f_{\alpha,0}|dxd\xi=0\,.

Assume that
j_{\delta_{n}}
is a standard mollification. Let

\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi)=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}j_{\delta_{n}}(x-y,\xi-\zeta)h^{n}_{\alpha}(y,\zeta)dyd\zeta.

As a result,
\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}\in\mathscr{S}(x,\xi)
.
For any
n\geq 1
, there exists
\delta_{n}^{*}\in(0,1)
such that for all
\delta_{n}\in(0,\delta_{n}^{*})
,

\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}-h^{n}_{\alpha}|dxd\xi\leq\tfrac{1}{n(n+2)^{2}}.

Due to
\mathrm{supp}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0},\mathrm{supp}h^{n}_{\alpha}\subseteq\{(x,\xi):|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}\leq(n+1)^{2}\}
, one then has

(3.4)

\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}-h^{n}_{\alpha}|(1+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2})dxd\xi\leq\tfrac{1}{n}\,.

Combining (
3.3
) and (
3.4
), we get

(3.5)

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}-f_{\alpha,0}|(1+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2})dxd\xi=0.

As a consequence, there exists a constant
C_{1}>0
such that

(3.6)

\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}(1+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2})dxd\xi\leq C_{1}.

Note that
\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}j_{\delta_{n}}dxd\xi=1
and
t\log t
is a continuous convex function over
\mathbb{R}_{+}
. The Jensen’s inequality then implies

\displaystyle\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}\leq\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}j_{\delta_{n}}(x-y,\xi-\zeta)(h^{n}_{\alpha}\log h^{n}_{\alpha}(y,\zeta))dyd\zeta,

\displaystyle\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log^{+}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}\leq\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}j_{\delta_{n}}(x-y,\xi-\zeta)|h^{n}_{\alpha}\log h^{n}_{\alpha}(y,\zeta)|dyd\zeta.

Integrating above two inequalities with respect to
(x,\xi)
and combining the definition of
h^{n}_{\alpha}
, one obtains

(3.7)

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}dxd\xi\leq

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{\alpha,0}\log f_{\alpha,0}dxd\xi,

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log^{+}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}dxd\xi\leq

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{\alpha,0}|\log f_{\alpha,0}|dxd\xi.

By (
3.5
) and (
3.7
), we know
\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}
converges strongly to
f_{\alpha,0}
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
and

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{\alpha,0}\log f_{\alpha,0}dxd\xi

\displaystyle\geq\limsup\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}dxd\xi.

Besides, by Corollary
2.2
, the assumption (
1.42
) and the first inequality in (
3.7
), one concludes that there exists a constant
C_{0}>0
such that for all
n\geq 1

\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log^{-}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}dxd\xi\leq C_{0}.

Combining the second inequality in (
3.7
), one sees

\sup\limits_{n}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}|\log\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}|dxd\xi\leq\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{\alpha,0}|\log f_{\alpha,0}|dxd\xi+C_{0}.

In summary, the above inequalities show that such
\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}
satisfies the first, the third and the last relations in (
3.2
) of Lemma
3.1
.

In order to obtain a sequence of functions that satisfies all the conditions of the Lemma
3.1
, let

f^{n}_{\alpha,0}=\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}+\tfrac{1}{n}\mathrm{exp}(-\tfrac{|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}}{2})\triangleq\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}+\varphi_{n}\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}).

Obviously,
f^{n}_{\alpha,0}
satisfies the first two conditions in (
3.2
). Using the elementary inequality
(u+v)\log(u+v)\leq u\log u+v\log v+u+v
and letting
u=\tilde{f}^{n}_{0},v=\varphi_{n},
one has

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log^{+}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}dxd\xi\leq

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}|\log\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}|dxd\xi+\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}dxd\xi

(3.8)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\tfrac{1+\log n}{n}+\tfrac{|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}}{2n})\mathrm{exp}(-\tfrac{|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}}{2})dxd\xi\leq C.

Since
\varphi_{n}\to 0
and
\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}\to f_{\alpha,0}
strongly in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
, one knows that
f^{n}_{\alpha,0}
converges strongly to
f_{\alpha,0}
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Similarly in arguments about
\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}
above, we can conclude that

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log^{-}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}dxd\xi\leq C_{2}

for some
C_{2}>0
. Then the third inequality in (
3.2
) holds. Besides, it is derived from the Differential Mean Value Theorem and
\log x<x
for
x>0
that

f^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log f^{n}_{\alpha,0}\leq\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}+\varphi_{n}+\varphi_{n}^{2}+\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}\varphi_{n}\,,

which concludes that
\limsup\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log f^{n}_{\alpha,0}dxd\xi\leq\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{\alpha,0}\log f_{\alpha,0}dxd\xi
. Namely, the forth relation in (
3.2
) holds. The proof of Lemma
3.1
is therefore finished.
∎

Lemma 3.2
.

Assume that
f_{\alpha,0}\in L^{1}_{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})_{+}
for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
satisfy

(3.9)

\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f_{\alpha,0}(1+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+I+|\log(I^{1-\frac{\delta(\alpha)}{2}}f_{\alpha,0})|)dxd\xi dI<+\infty.

Then there exists a sequence
\{f^{n}_{\alpha,0}\}_{n\geq 1}\subset\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
(the Schwartz space over
\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
) and a constant
C>0
such that

(1)

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(1+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+I)|f^{n}_{\alpha,0}-f_{\alpha,0}|dxd\xi dI=0
;

(2)

f^{n}_{\alpha,0}\geq\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{exp}(-\frac{|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+I}{2})
;

(3)

\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}(1+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+I+|\log(I^{1-\frac{\delta(\alpha)}{2}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0})|)dxd\xi dI\leq C
;

(4)

\limsup\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log(I^{1-\frac{\delta(\alpha)}{2}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0})dxd\xi dI\leq\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f_{\alpha,0}\log(I^{1-\frac{\delta(\alpha)}{2}}f_{\alpha,0})dxd\xi dI
.

Proof.

Let
h^{n}_{\alpha}(x,\xi,I)=f_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi,I)\chi_{n}(x,\xi)\lambda_{n}(I)
for all
n\geq 1
. Here
\lambda_{n}(I)=\mathbf{1}_{\{I\in\mathbb{R}_{+}:\frac{1}{n}\leq I\leq n\}}
and
\chi_{n}(x,\xi)=\mathbf{1}_{\{(x,\xi):|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}\leq n^{2}\}}
. It is obvious that

(3.10)

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(1+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+I)\cdot|h^{n}_{\alpha}-f_{\alpha,0}|dxd\xi dI=0.

Let
j_{\delta_{n}}(x,\xi)
and
\varphi_{\epsilon_{n}}(I)
be the standard mollification. Define

\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi,I)=\iiint_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}j_{\delta_{n}}(x-y,\xi-\zeta)h^{n}_{\alpha}(y,\zeta,\eta)\varphi_{\epsilon_{n}}(I-\eta)dyd\zeta d\eta,\ \epsilon_{n}\in(0,\tfrac{1}{2n}).

As a result,
\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}\in\mathscr{S}(x,\xi,I)_{+}
. Furthermore, as the similar arguments in (
3.5
), one can easily gain

(3.11)

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}-f_{\alpha,0}|(1+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+I)dxd\xi dI=0.

Then there exists a constant
C_{1}>0
such that

(3.12)

\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}(1+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+I)dxd\xi dI\leq C_{1}.

Observe that
\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}j_{\delta_{n}}dxd\xi=1
,
\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\varphi_{\epsilon_{n}}dI=1
and
\Phi(t)=t\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}t)
is continuous convex function over
\mathbb{R}_{+}
. It is then derived from the Jensen’s inequality that

\displaystyle\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0})\leq\iiint_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}j_{\delta_{n}}(x-y,\xi-\zeta)(h^{n}_{\alpha}\log(\eta^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}h^{n}_{\alpha})(y,\zeta,\eta))\varphi_{\epsilon_{n}}(I-\eta)dyd\zeta d\eta,

\displaystyle\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log^{+}(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0})\leq\iiint_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}j_{\delta_{n}}(x-y,\xi-\zeta)|h^{n}_{\alpha}\log(\eta^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}h^{n}_{\alpha})(y,\zeta,\eta)|\varphi_{\epsilon_{n}}(I-\eta)dyd\zeta d\eta.

Taking the similar methods in Lemma
3.1
, we obtain

(3.13)

\displaystyle\limsup\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0})dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f_{\alpha,0}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f_{\alpha,0})dxd\xi dI,

\displaystyle\limsup\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log^{+}(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0})dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f_{\alpha,0}|\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f_{\alpha,0})|dxd\xi dI.

Then by the proof of Lemma
2.4
, there exists a constant
C_{2}>0
such that

\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log^{-}(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0})dxd\xi dI\leq C_{2}.

Thus, one has

(3.14)

\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}|\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0})|dxd\xi dI\leq C.

The above inequalities show that such
\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}
satisfies the conditions (
1
), (
3
), (
4
) of Lemma
3.2
. To obtain a sequence of functions
f^{n}_{\alpha,0}
that satisfies all the conditions of the Lemma
3.2
, let

f^{n}_{\alpha,0}=\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}+\tfrac{1}{n}\mathrm{exp}(-\tfrac{|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+I}{2})\triangleq\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}+\varphi_{n}.

Then
f^{n}_{\alpha,0}\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})_{+}
. Obviously,
f^{n}_{\alpha,0}
satisfies the relations (
1
), (
2
) and

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}(1+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+I)dxd\xi dI\leq C\,.

By using the elementary inequality
(u+v)\log(u+v)\leq u\log u+v\log v+u+v
and letting
u=I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0},v=I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}\varphi_{n},
one has

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log^{+}(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha,0})dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}|\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0})|dxd\xi dI+\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(\tfrac{1+\log n}{n}+\tfrac{|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+I+\delta(\alpha)I}{2n})\mathrm{exp}(-\tfrac{|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+I}{2})dxd\xi dI\leq C(\alpha).

Combining the proof of Lemma
2.4
, we conclude that

\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}|\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha,0})|dxd\xi dI\leq C.

Then the relation (
3
) holds. Besides, it is derived from the Differential Mean Value Theorem that

f^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha,0})\leq\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}\tilde{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0})+\varphi_{n}(1+\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0})).

Note that by
\log x<x
over
x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0})\leq(1-\tfrac{\delta(\alpha)}{2})\mathbf{I}_{0<I<1}\log I+{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}\,.

Then there holds

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\varphi_{n}(1+\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}{f}^{n}_{\alpha,0}))dxd\xi dI\leq\tfrac{C}{n}\,.

Together with (
3.13
), one knows that the relation (
4
) holds. The proof of Lemma
3.2
is therefore finished.
∎

Together with Lemma
2.6
, Lemma
3.1
and Lemma
3.2
conclude the following lemma.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let the approximated initial data
f^{n}_{0}=(f^{n}_{\alpha,0})_{\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}}
of
f_{0}
be constructed in Lemma
3.1
and Lemma
3.2
. Then

(3.15)

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha=1}^{s_{0}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{\alpha,0}\log f_{\alpha,0}dxd\xi+\sum_{\alpha=s_{0}+1}^{s}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f_{\alpha,0}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f_{\alpha,0})dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\Big\{\sum_{\alpha=1}^{s_{0}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log f^{n}_{\alpha,0}dxd\xi+\sum_{\alpha=s_{0}+1}^{s}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha,0})dxd\xi dI\Big\}.

Hence
\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}H(f^{n}_{0})=H(f_{0})
, where the entropy
H(f_{0})
is defined in (
1.45
).

3.2.
Smooth approximation of collision kernels

In this subsection, the goal is to smoothly approximate the collision kernels
A_{0\alpha\beta},\ A_{1\alpha\beta},\ A_{1\beta\alpha}
and
A_{2\alpha\beta}
defined in (
1.34
), which will be used to construct the approximation of nonlinear collision operator.

Lemma 3.4
.

Assume collision kernel
A_{2\alpha\beta}
satisfies the assumption in (
1.44
), i.e.,

(1+|\xi|^{2}+I)^{-1}\int_{|z-\xi|\leq R}\int_{0<\eta-I<R}A_{2\alpha\beta}(z,\eta)dzd\eta\rightarrow 0,\quad|\xi|,I\rightarrow\infty,\ \forall R<+\infty.

Then there exists
B_{2\alpha\beta,n}\geq 0
satisfying

(1)

For any fixed
(\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r},\omega)\in[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2},B_{2\alpha\beta,n}(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r},\omega)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}),suppB_{2\alpha\beta,n}\subset\{(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r},\omega):\frac{1}{n}\leq|z|,\eta\leq n\}
and if
z\cdot\omega\leq\frac{1}{n},B_{2\alpha\beta,n}=0
;

(2)

For almost all
(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r},\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\times[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
,

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}B_{2\alpha\beta,n}=B_{2\alpha\beta}
, where
B_{2\alpha\beta}
is defined in (
1.27
);

(3)

For any
R<+\infty
,

\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}(1+|\xi|^{2}+I)^{-1}\int_{|z-\xi|\leq R}\int_{0<\eta-I<R}A_{2\alpha\beta,n}(z,\eta)dzd\eta\rightarrow 0,\ |\xi|,I\rightarrow\infty,

where

A_{2\alpha\beta,n}=\int_{[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{2\alpha\beta,n}\mathfrak{r}^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{r})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{R})^{(\delta(\alpha)+(\delta(\beta))/2-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega.

Proof.

Assume that
\chi_{n}(z,\eta)
and
\lambda_{n}(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r},\omega)
are respectively the characteristic functions of sets

\{(z,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}:\tfrac{1}{2n}\leq|z|,\eta\leq n-\tfrac{1}{2n}\}

and

\{(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r},\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\times[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}:z\cdot\omega\geq\tfrac{1}{2n},\ \tfrac{1}{2n}\leq\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r}\leq 1-\tfrac{1}{2n}\}.

Let

\displaystyle\hat{B}_{2\alpha\beta}=B_{2\alpha\beta}\cdot\mathfrak{r}^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{r})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}

\displaystyle(1-\mathfrak{R})^{(\delta(\alpha)+\delta(\beta))/2-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2},

\displaystyle\tilde{B}_{2\alpha\beta,n}=\hat{B}_{2\alpha\beta}\cdot\chi_{n}\cdot\lambda_{n},

\displaystyle\hat{B}_{2\alpha\beta,n}=j_{\delta_{n}}*\tilde{B}_{2\alpha\beta,n},

\displaystyle B_{2\alpha\beta,n}=\hat{B}_{2\alpha\beta,n}\cdot\mathfrak{r}^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}(1-\mathfrak{r})^{1-\delta(\beta)/2}

\displaystyle(1-\mathfrak{R})^{1-(\delta(\alpha)+\delta(\beta))/2}\mathfrak{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}

with
0<\delta_{n}\leq\frac{1}{2n}
, where the convolution is done for the variables
z,\eta
. Then
B_{2\alpha\beta,n}
satisfies the condition (
1
).

For all subset
K\Subset\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+},
there exists
n\geq 1
such that
K\subset\{(z,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}:0<|z|,\eta<n-\frac{1}{2n}\}
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{K}dzd\eta\int_{[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\hat{B}_{2\alpha\beta}-\tilde{B}_{2\alpha\beta,n}|d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{K\cap\{|z|\leq\frac{1}{2n}\}}dzd\eta\int_{[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\hat{B}_{2\alpha\beta}d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega+\int_{K\cap\{\eta\leq\frac{1}{2n}\}}dzd\eta\int_{[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\hat{B}_{2\alpha\beta}d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{K}dzd\eta\int_{[0,1]^{2}}d\mathfrak{r}d\mathfrak{R}\int_{z\cdot\omega<\frac{1}{2n}}\hat{B}_{2\alpha\beta}d\omega+\int_{K}dzd\eta\int_{[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\hat{B}_{2\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{1}_{0<\mathfrak{R}<\frac{1}{2n}}+\mathbf{1}_{0<\mathfrak{r}<\frac{1}{2n}})d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{K}dzd\eta\int_{[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\hat{B}_{2\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{1}_{1-\frac{1}{2n}<\mathfrak{R}<1}+\mathbf{1}_{1-\frac{1}{2n}<\mathfrak{r}<1})d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega.

Since
\hat{B}_{2\alpha\beta}=\tilde{\sigma}_{\alpha\beta}|g|(1-\mathfrak{R})E^{2}_{\alpha\beta}\in L^{1}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+};L^{1}([0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})),
the Dominated Convergence Theorem concludes that

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{K}dzd\eta\int_{[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\hat{B}_{2\alpha\beta}-\tilde{B}_{2\alpha\beta,n}|d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega=0.

Moreover, by the property of convolution, we know that for all
n\geq 1
there exists a
\delta_{n}^{*}\in(0,\frac{1}{2n})
such that for
\delta_{n}\in(0,\delta_{n}^{*})

\int_{K}dzd\eta\int_{[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\hat{B}_{2\alpha\beta,n}-\tilde{B}_{2\alpha\beta,n}|d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega<\tfrac{1}{2n}.

Therefore
\hat{B}_{2\alpha\beta,n}\rightarrow\hat{B}_{2\alpha\beta}\kern 5.0pt
in
L^{1}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+};L^{1}([0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}))
. It therefore follows that (
2
) is true.

Due to
\tilde{B}_{2\alpha\beta,n}\leq\hat{B}_{2\alpha\beta},
one has
A_{2\alpha\beta,n}\leq j_{\delta_{n}}*A_{2\alpha\beta}.
Then

\displaystyle\int_{|z-\xi|\leq R}\int_{0<\eta-I<R}A_{2\alpha\beta,n}(z,\eta)dzd\eta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{|z-\xi|\leq R}dz\int_{0<\eta-I<R}d\eta\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}j_{\delta_{n}}(\zeta,J)A_{2\alpha\beta}(z-\zeta,\eta-J)d\zeta dJ

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}j_{\delta_{n}}(\zeta,J)d\zeta dJ\int_{|\psi-\xi|\leq R+1}\int_{0<w-(I-\frac{1}{2})<R+1}A_{2\alpha\beta}(\psi,w)d\psi dw

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{|\psi-\xi|\leq R+1}\int_{0<w-(I-\frac{1}{2})<R+1}A_{2\alpha\beta}(\psi,w)d\psi dw.

Together with the assumption on
A_{2\alpha\beta}
, one then gains that for all
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle(1+|\xi|^{2}+I)^{-1}\int_{|z-\xi|\leq R}\int_{0<\eta-I<R}A_{2\alpha\beta,n}(z,\eta)dzd\eta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1+|\xi|^{2}+(I-\tfrac{1}{2}))^{-1}\int_{|\psi-\xi|\leq R+1}\int_{0<w-(I-\frac{1}{2})<R+1}A_{2\alpha\beta}(\psi,w)d\psi dw\rightarrow 0

as
|\xi|,I\rightarrow\infty
, which means that (
3
) holds. Therefore, the proof of Lemma
3.4
is finished.
∎

Lemma 3.5
.

Assume that collision kernels
A_{1\alpha\beta}
and
A_{1\beta\alpha}
satisfy the assumptions (
1.44
), i.e.,

\displaystyle(1+|\xi|^{2}+\eta)^{-1}\int_{|z-\xi|\leq R}A_{1\alpha\beta}(z,\eta)dz,\hskip 9.24994pt|\xi|,\eta\rightarrow\infty,\ \forall R<+\infty,

\displaystyle(1+|\xi|^{2})^{-1}\int_{|z-\xi|\leq R}\int_{0<\eta<R}A_{1\beta\alpha}(z,\eta)dzd\eta\rightarrow 0,\hskip 9.24994pt|\xi|\rightarrow\infty,\ \forall R<+\infty.

Then there exist
B_{1\alpha\beta,n}(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\omega),B_{1\beta\alpha,n}(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\omega)\geq 0
satisfying

(1)

For any
(\mathfrak{R},\omega)\in[0,1]\times\mathbb{S}^{2},\ B_{1\alpha\beta,n},B_{1\beta\alpha,n}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}),\ suppB_{1\alpha\beta,n},suppB_{1\beta\alpha,n}\subset\{(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\omega):\frac{1}{n}\leq|z|,\eta\leq n\}
and if
(\xi-\xi_{*})\cdot\omega\leq\frac{1}{n},B_{1\alpha\beta,n}=B_{1\beta\alpha,n}=0
;

(2)

For almost all
(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
,

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}B_{1\alpha\beta,n}=B_{1\alpha\beta},\quad\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}B_{1\beta\alpha,n}=B_{1\beta\alpha},

where
B_{1\alpha\beta}
and
B_{1\beta\alpha}
are respectively defined in (
1.23
) and (
1.25
);

(3)

For all
R<+\infty
,

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}(1+|\xi|^{2}+\eta)^{-1}\int_{|z-\xi|\leq R}A_{1\alpha\beta,n}(z,\eta)dz\rightarrow 0,\hskip 9.24994pt|\xi|,\eta\rightarrow\infty,

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}(1+|\xi|^{2})^{-1}\int_{|z-\xi|\leq R}\int_{0<\eta<R}A_{1\beta\alpha,n}(z,\eta)dzd\eta\rightarrow 0,\hskip 9.24994pt|\xi|\rightarrow\infty\,,

where

\displaystyle A_{1\alpha\beta,n}=\iint_{[0,1]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{1\alpha\beta,n}(1-\mathfrak{R})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}d\mathfrak{R}d\omega,A_{1\beta\alpha,n}=\iint_{[0,1]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{1\beta\alpha,n}(1-\mathfrak{R})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}d\mathfrak{R}d\omega.

Proof.

Denote by

\displaystyle\chi_{n}(z,\eta)=\mathbf{1}_{\{(z,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}:\frac{1}{2n}\leq|z|,\eta\leq n-\frac{1}{2n}\}}\,,

\displaystyle\lambda_{n}(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\omega)=\mathbf{1}_{\{(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}:z\cdot\omega\geq\frac{1}{2n},\ \frac{1}{2n}\leq\mathfrak{R}<1-\frac{1}{2n}\}}.

Let

\displaystyle\hat{B}_{1\alpha\beta}(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\omega)=B_{1\alpha\beta}(1-\mathfrak{R})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2},\ \hat{B}_{1\beta\alpha}(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\omega)=B_{1\beta\alpha}(1-\mathfrak{R})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2},

\displaystyle\tilde{B}_{1\alpha\beta,n}(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\omega)=\hat{B}_{1\alpha\beta}\cdot\chi_{n}\cdot\lambda_{n},\ \tilde{B}_{1\beta\alpha,n}(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\omega)=\hat{B}_{1\beta\alpha}\cdot\chi_{n}\cdot\lambda_{n},

\displaystyle\hat{B}_{1\alpha\beta,n}(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\omega)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}j_{\delta_{n}}(z-y)\tilde{B}_{1\alpha\beta,n}(y,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\omega)dy,

\displaystyle\hat{B}_{1\beta\alpha,n}(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\omega)=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\varphi_{\epsilon_{n}}(z-y,\eta-I)\tilde{B}_{1\beta\alpha,n}(y,I,\mathfrak{R},\omega)dydI,

\displaystyle B_{1\alpha\beta,n}(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\omega)=\hat{B}_{1\alpha\beta,n}(1-\mathfrak{R})^{1-\delta(\beta)/2}\mathfrak{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}},\ B_{1\beta\alpha,n}(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\omega)=\hat{B}_{1\beta\alpha,n}(1-\mathfrak{R})^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}\mathfrak{R}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,,

where
0<\delta_{n},\epsilon_{n}\leq\frac{1}{2n}
and
j_{\delta_{n}},\varphi_{\epsilon_{n}}
are the standard mollifiers. Then
B_{1\alpha\beta,n}
and
B_{1\beta\alpha,n}
satisfy the condition (
1
).

Taking the similar arguments in Lemma
3.4
, we obtain

\hat{B}_{1\alpha\beta,n}\rightarrow\hat{B}_{1\alpha\beta},\quad\hat{B}_{1\beta\alpha,n}\rightarrow\hat{B}_{1\beta\alpha}\quad\text{in }L^{1}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+};L^{1}([0,1]\times\mathbb{S}^{2})).

It then follows that (
2
) is true.

Due to
\tilde{B}_{1\alpha\beta,n}\leq\hat{B}_{1\alpha\beta},\tilde{B}_{1\beta\alpha,n}\leq\hat{B}_{1\beta\alpha}
we can conclude that
A_{1\alpha\beta,n}\leq j_{\delta_{n}}*A_{1\alpha\beta},A_{1\beta\alpha,n}\leq\varphi_{\epsilon_{n}}*A_{1\beta\alpha}.
Then for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
and
\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
,

\displaystyle\int_{|z-\xi|\leq R}A_{1\alpha\beta,n}(z,\eta)dz\leq\int_{|z-\xi|\leq R}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}j_{\delta_{n}}(\zeta)A_{1\alpha\beta}(z-\zeta,\eta)d\zeta dz

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}j_{\delta_{n}}(\zeta)\int_{|\psi+\zeta-\xi|\leq R}A_{1\alpha\beta}(\psi,\eta)d\psi d\zeta\leq\int_{|\psi-\xi|\leq R+1}A_{1\alpha\beta}(\psi,\eta)d\psi\,.

Moreover, for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
and
\beta\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
,

\displaystyle\int_{|z-\xi|\leq R}\int_{0<\eta<R}A_{1\beta\alpha,n}(z,\eta)dzd\eta\leq

\displaystyle\int_{|z-\xi|\leq R}\int_{0<\eta<R}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\varphi_{\epsilon_{n}}(\zeta,J)A_{1\beta\alpha}(z-\zeta,\eta-J)d\zeta dJd\eta dz

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\varphi_{\epsilon_{n}}(\zeta,J)\int_{|\psi+\zeta-\xi|\leq R}\int_{0<K+J<R}A_{1\beta\alpha}(\psi,K)d\psi dKdJd\zeta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{|\psi-\xi|\leq R+1}\int_{0<K+\frac{1}{2}<R+1}A_{1\beta\alpha}(\psi,K)dKd\psi.

Therefore, together with the assumptions on
A_{1\alpha\beta}
and
A_{1\beta\alpha}
, there hold that for all
n\geq 1
and
0<R<+\infty
,

\displaystyle(1+|\xi|^{2}+\eta)^{-1}\int_{|z-\xi|\leq R}A_{1\alpha\beta,n}(z,\eta)dz\rightarrow 0,\quad|\xi|,\eta\rightarrow\infty,

\displaystyle(1+|\xi|^{2})^{-1}\int_{|z-\xi|\leq R}\int_{0<\eta<R}A_{1\beta\alpha,n}(z,\eta)dzd\eta\rightarrow 0,\quad|\xi|\rightarrow\infty.

The condition (
3
) is thus true. The proof of Lemma
3.5
is then finished.
∎

Lemma 3.6
.

Assume that collision kernel
A_{0\alpha\beta}
satisfies the assumption (
1.34
), hence,

(1+|\xi|^{2})^{-1}\int_{|z-\xi|\leq R}A_{0\alpha\beta}(z)dz,\quad|\xi|\rightarrow\infty,\ \forall R<+\infty.

Then there exists
B_{0\alpha\beta,n}\geq 0
satisfying

(1)

For any
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2},B_{0\alpha\beta,n}(z,\omega)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}),suppB_{0\alpha\beta,n}\subset\{(z,\omega):\frac{1}{n}\leq|z|\leq n\}
and if
(\xi-\xi_{*})\cdot\omega\leq\frac{1}{n},B_{0\alpha\beta,n}=0
;

(2)

For almost all
(z,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2},\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}B_{0\alpha\beta,n}=B_{0\alpha\beta}
, where
B_{0\alpha\beta}
is defined in (
1.21
);

(3)

For any
0<R<+\infty
,

\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}(1+|\xi|^{2})^{-1}\int_{|z-\xi|\leq R}A_{0\alpha\beta,n}(z)dz\rightarrow 0,

where
A_{0\alpha\beta,n}(z)=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{0\alpha\beta,n}(z,\omega)\mathrm{d}\omega
.

Proof.

Denote by
\chi_{n}(z)=\mathbf{1}_{\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:\frac{1}{2n}\leq|z|\leq n-\frac{1}{2n}\}}
and
\lambda_{n}(z,\omega)=\mathbf{1}_{\{(z,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}:z\cdot\omega\geq\frac{1}{2n}\}}
. Let

\tilde{B}_{0\alpha\beta,n}(z,\omega)=B_{0\alpha\beta}\cdot\chi_{n}\cdot\lambda_{n},\ B_{0\alpha\beta,n}=j_{\delta_{n}}*\tilde{B}_{0\alpha\beta,n},

where
0<\delta_{n}\leq\frac{1}{2n}.
Here the convolution is done for the variables
z\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Then the kernel
B_{0\alpha\beta,n}
satisfies the condition (
1
). Taking the similar methods in Lemma
3.2
, we obtain

B_{0\alpha\beta,n}\rightarrow B_{0\alpha\beta}\text{ in }L^{1}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}^{3};L^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{2}))\,,

which infers (
2
). Moreover, the fact
\tilde{B}_{0\alpha\beta,n}\leq B_{0\alpha\beta}
implies
A_{0\alpha\beta,n}\leq j_{\delta_{n}}*A_{0\alpha\beta}.
Then one has

\displaystyle\int_{|z-\xi|\leq R}A_{0\alpha\beta,n}(z)dz\leq

\displaystyle\int_{|z-\xi|\leq R}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}j_{\delta_{n}}(\zeta)A_{0\alpha\beta}(z-\zeta)d\zeta dz

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}j_{\delta_{n}}(\zeta)\int_{|\psi+\zeta-\xi|\leq R}A_{0\alpha\beta}(\psi)d\psi d\zeta\leq\int_{|\psi-\xi|\leq R+1}A_{0\alpha\beta}(\psi)d\psi\,,

which, together with the assumption (
1.34
) of
A_{0\alpha\beta}
, concludes (
3
). The proof of Lemma
3.6
is therefore finished.
∎

3.3.
Approximation of collision operator

Now we construct the approximated collision operator
\tilde{Q}_{n}=(\tilde{Q}_{1,n},\cdots,\tilde{Q}_{s,n})
as follows. Recalling the collision kernels
B_{2\alpha\beta,n}
,
B_{1\alpha\beta,n}
,
B_{1\beta\alpha,n}
and
B_{0\alpha\beta,n}
constructed in Lemma
3.4
-
3.5
-
3.6
, we first define

\displaystyle\hat{B}_{1\alpha\beta,n}(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\omega)=B_{1\alpha\beta,n}(1-\mathfrak{R})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}\,,\hat{B}_{1\beta\alpha,n}(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\omega)=B_{1\beta\alpha,n}(1-\mathfrak{R})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}\,,

\displaystyle\hat{B}_{2\alpha\beta,n}(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r},\omega)=B_{2\alpha\beta,n}\mathfrak{r}^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{r})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{R})^{(\delta(\alpha)+\delta(\beta))/2-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}.

We now introduce the operators
Q_{\alpha,n}(f,f)
for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
.

If
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
, we define

\displaystyle Q_{\alpha,n}(f,f)(\xi)=\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}Q_{\alpha\beta,n}(f,f)(\xi)\,,

where

\displaystyle Q_{\alpha\beta,n}(f,f)(\xi)

\displaystyle=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{0\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},\omega)(f^{\prime}_{\alpha}f^{\prime}_{\beta_{*}}-f_{\alpha}f_{\beta_{*}})d\omega d\xi_{*}

\displaystyle=Q_{\alpha\beta,n}^{+}(f,f)(\xi)-Q_{\alpha\beta,n}^{-}(f,f)(\xi)

for
\beta\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
, and

\displaystyle Q_{\alpha\beta,n}(f,f)(\xi)

\displaystyle=\iiiint\limits_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\hat{B}_{1\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I_{*},\mathfrak{R},\omega)(\tfrac{f^{\prime}_{\alpha}f^{\prime}_{\beta_{*}}}{(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}-\tfrac{f_{\alpha}f_{\beta_{*}}}{I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}})I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}d\omega d\mathfrak{R}dI_{*}d\xi_{*}

\displaystyle=Q_{\alpha\beta,n}^{+}(f,f)(\xi)-Q_{\alpha\beta,n}^{-}(f,f)(\xi)

for
\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
. For the loss terms
Q_{\alpha\beta,n}^{-}(f,f)(\xi)
with
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
and
\beta\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
, one can represent

\displaystyle Q_{\alpha\beta,n}^{-}(f,f)(\xi)=f_{\alpha}L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f)(\xi)\,,

where

\displaystyle L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f)(\xi)=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{0\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},\omega)f_{\beta*}d\omega d\xi_{*}\,,\ \beta\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}\,,

\displaystyle L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f)(\xi)=\iiiint\limits_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\hat{B}_{1\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I_{*},\mathfrak{R},\omega)f_{\beta_{*}}d\omega d\mathfrak{R}dI_{*}d\xi_{*}\,,\ \beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}\,.

Denote by
L_{\alpha,n}(f)(\xi)=\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f)(\xi)
for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
.

If
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, we define

\displaystyle Q_{\alpha,n}(f,f)(\xi,I)=\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}Q_{\alpha\beta,n}(f,f)(\xi,I)\,,

where

\displaystyle Q_{\alpha\beta,n}(f,f)(\xi,I)=

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\hat{B}_{1\beta\alpha,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I,\mathfrak{R},\omega)(\tfrac{f^{\prime}_{\alpha}f^{\prime}_{\beta_{*}}}{(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}}-\tfrac{f_{\alpha}f_{\beta_{*}}}{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}})I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}d\omega d\mathfrak{R}d\xi_{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Q_{\alpha\beta,n}^{+}(f,f)(\xi,I)-Q_{\alpha\beta,n}^{-}(f,f)(\xi,I)

for
\beta\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
, and

\displaystyle Q_{\alpha\beta,n}(f,f)(\xi,I)=

\displaystyle\iiiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\times[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\tfrac{f^{\prime}_{\alpha}f^{\prime}_{\beta_{*}}}{(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}-\tfrac{f_{\alpha}f_{\beta_{*}}}{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}})

\displaystyle\times\hat{B}_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*},\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r},\omega)I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}d\omega d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}dI_{*}d\xi_{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Q_{\alpha\beta,n}^{+}(f,f)(\xi,I)-Q_{\alpha\beta,n}^{-}(f,f)(\xi,I)

for
\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
. Moreover, the loss terms
Q_{\alpha\beta,n}^{-}(f,f)(\xi,I)
with
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
and
\beta\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
can be represented by

\displaystyle Q_{\alpha\beta,n}^{-}(f,f)(\xi,I)=f_{\alpha}L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f)(\xi,I)\,,

where

\displaystyle L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f)(\xi,I)=\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\hat{B}_{1\beta\alpha,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I,\mathfrak{R},\omega)f_{\beta*}d\omega d\mathfrak{R}d\xi_{*}\,,\ \beta\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}\,,

\displaystyle L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f)(\xi,I)=\iiiint\limits_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\times[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\hat{B}_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*},\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r},\omega)f_{\beta*}d\omega d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}dI_{*}d\xi_{*}\,,\ \beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}\,.

Denote by
L_{\alpha,n}(f)(\xi,I)=\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f)(\xi,I)
for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
. Furthermore, we employ the symbols
Q_{\alpha,n}^{\pm}(f,f)(\mathbf{Z})=\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}Q_{\alpha\beta,n}^{\pm}(f,f)(\mathbf{Z})
for
\alpha,\beta\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
and
\mathbf{Z}=\xi
or
(\xi,I)
. Then
Q_{\alpha,n}(f,f)=Q^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)-Q^{-}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)
.

Applying the above symbols, we construct the smoothly approximated collision operator
\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}
as follows:

(3.16)

\displaystyle\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}(N_{n}(f))^{-1}Q^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)(\xi),\quad&\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\},\\
(N_{n}(f))^{-1}Q^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)(\xi,I),\quad&\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}(N_{n}(f))^{-1}Q_{\alpha,n}(f,f)(\xi),\quad&\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\},\\
(N_{n}(f))^{-1}Q_{\alpha,n}(f,f)(\xi,I),\quad&\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}(N_{n}(f))^{-1}L_{\alpha,n}(f)(\xi),\quad&\ \ \ \alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\},\\
(N_{n}(f))^{-1}L_{\alpha,n}(f)(\xi,I),\quad&\ \ \ \alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}\,,\end{cases}

where

(3.17)

\displaystyle N_{n}(f)=1+\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{s_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|f_{\alpha}|d\xi+\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{\alpha=s_{0}+1}^{s}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|f_{\alpha}|d\xi dI\,.

Now we study the
L^{1}
and
L^{\infty}
properties of the approximated collision operator
\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}
, which will be used to investigate the existence of the approximation problem (
3.1
).

Lemma 3.7

(
L^{1}
estimates on variable
\mathbf{Z}
)
.

Denote by the variable
\mathbf{Z}
by

\mathbf{Z}=\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha}:=\begin{cases}\mathbf{\xi},\qquad\kern 5.0pt\,\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}\,,\\
(\mathbf{\xi},I),\quad\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}\,.\end{cases}

There exists a positive constant
C_{s,n}
depending on
s,n
such that for all distribution functions
f(\mathbf{Z}),g(\mathbf{Z})
with the components
f_{\alpha},g_{\alpha}\in L^{1}(d\mathbf{Z})
,

\displaystyle\|\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)(\mathbf{Z})\|_{L^{1}}\leq C_{s,n}\|f_{\alpha}(\mathbf{Z})\|_{L^{1}},

\displaystyle\|\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)(\mathbf{Z})-\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}(g,g)(\mathbf{Z})\|_{L^{1}}\leq C_{s,n}\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}\|f_{\beta}(\mathbf{Z})-g_{\beta}(\mathbf{Z})\|_{L^{1}}.

Proof.

For all
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\},

\displaystyle\|\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)\|_{L^{1}}+\|\tilde{Q}^{-}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)\|_{L^{1}},

\displaystyle\|\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)-\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}(g,g)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)-\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(g,g)\|_{L^{1}}+\|\tilde{Q}^{-}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)-\tilde{Q}^{-}_{\alpha,n}(g,g)\|_{L^{1}}.

Using invariant properties of
B_{2\alpha\beta}
, that is,
B_{2\alpha\beta}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*},\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r},\omega)=B_{2\alpha\beta}(\xi^{\prime}-,\xi^{\prime}_{*},I^{\prime}+I^{\prime}_{*},\mathfrak{R}^{\prime},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})
for
\alpha,\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
(see (
1.33
)), we easily deduce that
\hat{B}_{2\alpha\beta,n}
is also invariant for such an exchange of state. Then from using the change variables
(\xi,I)\rightarrow(\xi^{\prime},I^{\prime})
, it follows that for
\alpha,\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
and for any
\varphi(\xi,I)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})

(3.18)

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}Q^{+}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f,f)\varphi d\xi dI

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}\times(\mathbb{R}_{+})^{2}\times[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\hat{B}_{2\alpha\beta,n}\tfrac{f^{\prime}_{\alpha}f^{\prime}_{\beta_{*}}}{(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}\varphi d\xi d\xi_{*}d\mathfrak{r}d\mathfrak{R}d\omega dIdI_{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{2}\times(\mathbb{R}_{+})^{2}\times[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\hat{B}_{2\alpha\beta,n}f_{\alpha}f_{\beta_{*}}\varphi^{\prime}d\xi d\xi_{*}d\mathfrak{r}d\mathfrak{R}d\omega dIdI_{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}Q^{-}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f,f)\varphi^{\prime}d\xi dI,

where
\varphi^{\prime}=\varphi(\xi^{\prime},I^{\prime})
. Moreover, by the similar arguments in (
3.18
), we concludes that for the other indices
\alpha,\beta
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha}}Q^{+}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f,f)\varphi d\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha}=\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha}}Q^{-}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f,f)\varphi^{\prime}d\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha}\,.

It then follows that for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
,

\displaystyle\|Q^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)\|_{L^{1}}\leq

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\beta=1}^{s_{0}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}A_{0\alpha\beta,n}|f_{\alpha}f_{\beta*}|d\xi d\xi_{*}+\sum\limits_{\beta=s_{0}+1}^{s}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}A_{1\alpha\beta,n}|f_{\alpha}f_{\beta*}|d\xi d\xi_{*}dI_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\alpha,n}\|f_{\alpha}\|_{L^{1}}\sum\limits_{\beta=1}^{s}\|f_{\beta}\|_{L^{1}},

and for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
,

\displaystyle\|Q^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)\|_{L^{1}}\leq

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\beta=1}^{s_{0}}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}A_{1\beta\alpha,n}|f_{\alpha}f_{\beta}|d\xi d\xi_{*}dI+\sum\limits_{\beta=s_{0}+1}^{s}\iiiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}A_{2\alpha\beta,n}|f_{\alpha}f_{\beta_{*}}|d\xi d\xi_{*}dIdI_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\alpha,n}\|f_{\alpha}\|_{L^{1}}\sum\limits_{\beta=1}^{s}\|f_{\beta}\|_{L^{1}}.

Here we have utilized the facts that the approximated kernels
A_{0\alpha\beta,n},A_{1\alpha\beta,n},A_{1\beta\alpha,n},A_{2\alpha\beta,n}
constructed in Lemmas
3.4
-
3.5
-
3.6
belong to
L^{\infty}
with upper bounds depending on
n
. Observe that
\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}\|f_{\beta}\|_{L^{1}}(N_{n}(f))^{-1}\leq n
, where
N_{n}(f)
is defined in (
3.17
). As a consequence, it is easy to see that
\|\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)\|_{L^{1}}\leq C_{\alpha,n}\|f_{\alpha}\|_{L^{1}}
for all
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
. Similarly, one has
\|\tilde{Q}^{-}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)\|_{L^{1}}\leq C_{\alpha,n}\|f_{\alpha}\|_{L^{1}}
for all
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
. Then
\|\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)\|_{L^{1}}\leq C_{\alpha,n}\|f_{\alpha}\|_{L^{1}}
. In summary, we infer that

(3.19)

\|\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)\|_{L^{1}}\leq C_{s,n}\|f_{\alpha}\|_{L^{1}}.

Observe that for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
,

\displaystyle\|\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)-

\displaystyle\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(g,g)\|_{L^{1}}=\sum\limits_{\beta=1}^{s_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}d\xi|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\{(N_{n}(f))^{-1}f_{\alpha}f_{\beta*}-(N_{n}(g))^{-1}g_{\alpha}g_{\beta*}\}A_{0\alpha\beta,n}d\xi_{*}|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\beta=s_{0}+1}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}d\xi|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\{(N_{n}(f))^{-1}f_{\alpha}f_{\beta*}-(N_{n}(g))^{-1}g_{\alpha}g_{\beta*}\}A_{1\alpha\beta,n}d\xi_{*}dI_{*}|\,.

Note that

\displaystyle|(N_{n}(f))^{-1}f_{\alpha}f_{\beta*}-(N_{n}(g))^{-1}g_{\alpha}g_{\beta_{*}}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(N_{n}(f))^{-1}|f_{\beta*}||f_{\alpha}-g_{\alpha}|+(N_{n}(g))^{-1}|g_{\alpha}||f_{\beta*}-g_{\beta*}|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle|f_{\beta*}g_{\alpha}|(N_{n}(f))^{-1}(N_{n}(g))^{-1}\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}\|f_{\beta}-g_{\beta}\|_{L^{1}}.

By using the facts
0\leq A_{0\alpha\beta,n},A_{1\alpha\beta,n}\leq C_{s,n}
and

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}\|f_{\beta}\|_{L^{1}}(N_{n}(f))^{-1}\leq n\,,\ \sum_{\beta=1}^{s}\|g_{\beta}\|_{L^{1}}(N_{n}(g))^{-1}\leq n\,,

one then has

\displaystyle\|\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}^{+}(f,f)-\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}^{+}(g,g)\|_{L^{1}}\leq C_{s,n}\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}\|f_{\beta}-g_{\beta}\|_{L^{1}}

for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
. Similarly, for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, there holds

\displaystyle\|\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}^{+}(f,f)-\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}^{+}(g,g)\|_{L^{1}}\leq C_{s,n}\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}\|f_{\beta}-g_{\beta}\|_{L^{1}}\,.

Similarly, one infers that

\displaystyle\|\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}^{-}(f,f)-\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}^{-}(g,g)\|_{L^{1}}\leq C_{s,n}\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}\|f_{\beta}-g_{\beta}\|_{L^{1}}\,.

Therefore, the proof of Lemma
3.7
is finished.
∎

Lemma 3.8

(
L^{\infty}
esitmates)
.

There exists a constant
C_{s,n}>0
depending on
s,n
such that for all distribution functions
f=(f_{1},\cdots,f_{s})
with the component
f_{\alpha}\in L^{\infty}\cap L^{1}
,

\|\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C_{\alpha,n}\|f_{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}

for all
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
.

Proof.

We first consider the quantity
\tilde{Q}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f,f)
defined in (
3.16
) for the case
\alpha,\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
. Recall that
\tilde{Q}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f,f)=(N_{n}(f))^{-1}Q_{\alpha\beta,n}(f,f)
with
Q_{\alpha\beta,n}(f,f)=Q_{\alpha\beta,n}^{+}(f,f)-Q_{\alpha\beta,n}^{-}(f,f)
. For the quantity
Q_{\alpha\beta,n}^{+}(f,f)
,

\displaystyle Q_{\alpha\beta,n}^{+}(f,f)\leq\|f_{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\times[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*},\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r},\omega)\tfrac{|f_{\beta}(\xi^{\prime}_{*},I^{\prime}_{*})|}{(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}

\displaystyle\times\mathfrak{r}^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{r})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{R})^{(\delta(\alpha)+\delta(\beta))/2-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}d\xi_{*}dI_{*}d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega\,.

By using the expressions of
E_{\alpha\beta},\mathfrak{R}
and
\mathfrak{r}
in (
1.17
), (
1.18
) and (
1.19
), respectively, one has

\displaystyle(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}=\mathfrak{r}^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{r})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{R})^{(\delta(\alpha)+\delta(\beta))/2-2}E_{\alpha\beta}^{(\delta(\alpha)+\delta(\beta))/2-2}\,.

Due to
E_{\alpha\beta}=\tfrac{\mu_{\alpha\beta}}{2}|g|^{2}+I+I_{*}
, one knows that

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{E_{\alpha\beta}^{(\delta(\alpha)+\delta(\beta))/2-2}}I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}\leq 1\,.

Moreover, Lemma
3.4
shows that

suppB_{2\alpha\beta,n}(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r},\omega)\subset\{(z,\eta,\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r},\omega):\tfrac{1}{n}\leq|z|,\eta\leq n,\ \tfrac{1}{n}\leq\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r}\leq 1-\tfrac{1}{n}\}\,.

Then

\displaystyle Q_{\alpha\beta,n}^{+}(f,f)\leq\tilde{C}_{n}\|f_{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\times[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*},\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r},\omega)|f_{\beta}(\xi^{\prime}_{*},I^{\prime}_{*})|

\displaystyle\times(1-\mathfrak{R})\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}d\xi_{*}dI_{*}d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega.

Furthermore, due to
E_{\alpha\beta}=\frac{\mu_{\alpha\beta}}{2}|g|^{2}+I+I_{*}
and
I_{*}^{\prime}=(1-\mathfrak{r})(1-\mathfrak{R})E_{\alpha\beta}
, one then has
dI_{*}^{\prime}=d[(1-\mathfrak{r})(1-\mathfrak{R})(\frac{\mu_{\alpha\beta}}{2}|g|^{2}+I+I_{*})]=(1-\mathfrak{r})(1-\mathfrak{R})dI_{*}
, namely,
dI_{*}=(1-\mathfrak{r})^{-1}(1-\mathfrak{R})^{-1}dI_{*}^{\prime}
. Then

(3.20)

\displaystyle Q_{\alpha\beta,n}^{+}(f,f)\leq\tilde{C}_{n}\|f_{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}I_{2\alpha\beta,n}\,,

where

(3.21)

\displaystyle I_{2\alpha\beta,n}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\times[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*},\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r},\omega)|f_{\beta}(\xi^{\prime}_{*},I^{\prime}_{*})|(1-\mathfrak{r})^{-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}d\xi_{*}dI_{*}^{\prime}d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega

Now we control the integral
I_{2\alpha\beta,n}
given in (
3.21
). Let
t=|\xi^{\prime}-\xi^{\prime}_{*}|=|g^{\prime}|
. Then
\xi^{\prime}_{*}=\xi^{\prime}-|\xi^{\prime}-\xi^{\prime}_{*}|\omega=\xi^{\prime}-t\omega
. We also extend the kernel
B_{2\alpha\beta,n}
on the variable
\theta
associated with the variable
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
from
(0,\frac{\pi}{2})
to
(0,\pi)
such that
B_{\alpha\beta,n}=0
if
\theta\in[\frac{\pi}{2},\pi)
. The extension is such that
\omega
goes through the whole
\mathbb{S}^{2}
Consider the plane
\Pi(\xi^{\prime},\omega)
that is perpendicular to vector
\omega
through point
\xi^{\prime}
. For any given
\omega
,
\xi-\xi_{*}
can be decomposed as
(s,v)
variables, where
s=(\xi-\xi_{*},\omega),v\in\Pi(\xi^{\prime},\omega)
and
d\xi_{*}=-dsdv
. Denote by
s=(\xi-\xi_{*},\omega)=(\xi-\xi_{*},\frac{\xi^{\prime}-\xi^{\prime}_{*}}{|\xi^{\prime}-\xi^{\prime}_{*}|})=|g|\cos\theta
. Recalling that
|g^{\prime}|^{2}+\tfrac{2}{\mu_{\alpha\beta}}\Delta I=|g|^{2}
, one has
s=\sqrt{t^{2}+\frac{2}{\mu_{\alpha\beta}}\Delta I}\cos\theta
. Then

ds=d(|g|\cos\theta)=\tfrac{t\cos\theta}{\sqrt{t^{2}+\frac{2}{\mu_{\alpha\beta}}\Delta I}}dt.

As a result, for the case
\alpha,\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
,

(3.22)

\displaystyle I_{2\alpha\beta,n}\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}dt\int_{\Pi(\xi^{\prime},\omega)}dv\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*},\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r},\omega)

\displaystyle\times|f_{\beta}(\xi^{\prime}-t\omega,I^{\prime}_{*})|(1-\mathfrak{r})^{-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}\tfrac{t\cos\theta}{\sqrt{t^{2}+\frac{2}{\mu_{\alpha\beta}}\Delta I}}dI^{\prime}_{*}d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega.

By Lemma
3.4
, one knows
B_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*},\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r},\omega)=0
if
s=|g|\cos\theta<\frac{1}{n}
.

Case 1.
\Delta I\leq 0
.

If
t<\tfrac{1}{n\cos\theta},\ s=|g|\cos\theta=\sqrt{t^{2}+\tfrac{2}{\mu_{\alpha\beta}}\Delta I}\cos\theta<t\cos\theta<\frac{1}{n}
. That means
B_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*},\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r},\omega)=0
for
t<\tfrac{1}{n\cos\theta}
. Notices that
|g|^{2}=t^{2}+\frac{2}{\mu_{\alpha\beta}}\Delta I
and
\tfrac{1}{|g|^{2}}=\tfrac{\cos^{2}\theta}{s^{2}}\leq n^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\leq n^{2}
for
s\geq\tfrac{1}{n}
. Then
|g|^{2}\tfrac{1}{|g|^{2}}\frac{t\cos\theta}{\sqrt{t^{2}+\frac{2}{\mu_{\alpha\beta}}\Delta I}}\leq n^{2}t\cos\theta\sqrt{t^{2}+\frac{2}{\mu_{\alpha\beta}}\Delta I}\leq n^{2}t^{2}
. Consequently, the quantity
I_{2\alpha\beta,n}
can be further bounded by

\displaystyle I_{2\alpha\beta,n}\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{n\cos\theta}}^{+\infty}dt\int_{\Pi(\xi^{\prime},\omega)}|g|^{2}\tfrac{1}{|g|^{2}}dv\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*},\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r},\omega)

\displaystyle\times|f_{\beta}(\xi^{\prime}-t\omega,I^{\prime}_{*})|(1-\mathfrak{r})^{-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}\tfrac{t\cos\theta}{\sqrt{t^{2}+\frac{2}{\mu_{\alpha\beta}}\Delta I}}dI^{\prime}_{*}d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle n^{2}\int_{\frac{1}{n\cos\theta}}^{+\infty}t^{2}dt\int_{\Pi(\xi^{\prime},\omega)}dv\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{2\alpha\beta,n}|f_{\beta}(\xi^{\prime}-t\omega,I^{\prime}_{*})|(1-\mathfrak{r})^{-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}dI^{\prime}_{*}d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega.

Case 2.
\Delta I>0
.

Recalling that
\cos\theta=\tfrac{g\cdot g^{\prime}}{|g||g^{\prime}|}
in (
1.13
), one knows that as
t\to 0^{+}
,

\displaystyle g\cdot g^{\prime}=t\sqrt{t^{2}+\tfrac{2}{\mu_{\alpha\beta}}\Delta I}\cos\theta\to 0\,,

which further means that
\cos\theta\to 0
as
t\to 0^{+}
. By the construction of
B_{2\alpha\beta,n}
in Lemma
3.4
, one infers that there is a small
\kappa_{n}>0
such that
B_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*},\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r},\omega)=0
as
t<\kappa_{n}
. Consequently, the integral
I_{2\alpha\beta,n}
can be dominated by

\displaystyle I_{2\alpha\beta,n}\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\kappa_{n}}^{+\infty}dt\int_{\Pi(\xi^{\prime},\omega)}t^{2}\tfrac{1}{t^{2}}dv\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times[\delta_{n},1-\delta_{n}]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*},\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r},\omega)

\displaystyle\times|f_{\beta}(\xi^{\prime}-t\omega,I^{\prime}_{*})|(1-\mathfrak{r})^{-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}\tfrac{t\cos\theta}{\sqrt{t^{2}+\frac{2}{\mu_{\alpha\beta}}\Delta I}}dI^{\prime}_{*}d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\kappa_{n}^{2}}\int_{\kappa_{n}}^{+\infty}t^{2}dt\int_{\Pi(\xi^{\prime},\omega)}dv\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{2\alpha\beta,n}|f_{\beta}(\xi^{\prime}-t\omega,I^{\prime}_{*})|(1-\mathfrak{r})^{-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}dI^{\prime}_{*}d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega.

Therefore, there exists
C^{\prime}_{n}>0
such that for all
\Delta I\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle I_{2\alpha\beta,n}\leq C^{\prime}_{n}\int_{0}^{+\infty}t^{2}dt\int_{\Pi(\xi^{\prime},\omega)}dv\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*},\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{r},\omega)

\displaystyle\times|f_{\beta}(\xi^{\prime}-t\omega,I^{\prime}_{*})|(1-\mathfrak{r})^{-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}dI^{\prime}_{*}d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega\,.

Moreover, we denote by
C_{1}
the maximum area of the intersection between plane
\Pi(\xi^{\prime},\omega)
and set
suppB_{2\alpha\beta,n}
and denote by
C_{2}
the maximum value of
B_{2\alpha\beta,n}
. Then the integral
I_{2\alpha\beta,n}
can further controlled by

(3.23)

\displaystyle I_{2\alpha\beta,n}\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}C_{2}\int_{[\frac{1}{n},1-\frac{1}{n}]^{2}}(1-\mathfrak{r})^{-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}\int_{0}^{+\infty}t^{2}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}|f_{\beta}(\xi^{\prime}-t\omega,I^{\prime}_{*})|dI^{\prime}_{*}d\omega

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{\prime\prime}_{n}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|f_{\beta}(\xi^{\prime}-y,I^{\prime}_{*})|dydI^{\prime}_{*}=C^{\prime\prime}_{n}\|f_{\beta}\|_{L^{1}}\,.

From substituting (
3.23
) into (
3.20
), it derives that for
\alpha,\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
,

(3.24)

\displaystyle Q_{\alpha\beta,n}^{+}(f,f)\leq C_{n}\|f_{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|f_{\beta}\|_{L^{1}}\,.

Following the similar arguments in (
3.24
), one can also derive that

(3.25)

\displaystyle Q_{\alpha\beta,n}^{+}(f,f)\leq C_{n}\|f_{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|f_{\beta}\|_{L^{1}}

for
\alpha,\beta\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
, or
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
,
\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, or
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
,
\beta\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
. As a consequence, the bounds (
3.24
) and (
3.25
) imply that for all
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
,

(3.26)

\displaystyle\|Q_{\alpha,n}^{+}(f,f)\|_{L^{\infty}}=\|\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}Q_{\alpha\beta,n}^{+}(f,f)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C_{n,s}\|f_{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}\|f_{\beta}\|_{L^{1}}\,.

By the structures of
B_{2\alpha\beta,n}
,
B_{1\alpha\beta,n}
,
B_{1\beta\alpha,n}
and
B_{0\alpha\beta,n}
constructed in Lemmas
3.4
-
3.5
-
3.6
, one easily has
L_{\alpha,n}(f)=\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f)\leq C_{n,s}\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}\|f_{\beta}\|_{L^{1}}
for all
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
, which infer that

(3.27)

\displaystyle Q_{\alpha,n}^{-}(f,f)=f_{\alpha}L_{\alpha,n}(f)\leq C_{n,s}\|f_{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}\|f_{\beta}\|_{L^{1}}\,.

The bounds (
3.26
) and (
3.27
) reduce to

\displaystyle\|\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)\|_{L^{\infty}}=(N_{n}(f))^{-1}\|Q_{\alpha,n}(f,f)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C_{n,s}\|f_{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}\|f_{\beta}\|_{L^{1}}\leq C_{n,s}\|f_{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}

for all
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
, where
(N_{n}(f))^{-1}\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}\|f_{\beta}\|_{L^{1}}\leq n
is used. The proof of Lemma
3.8
is therefore finished.
∎

3.4.
Global existence of approximation equation

In this subsection, based on the Lemma
3.7
and
3.8
, we prove the existence and uniqueness of the nonnegative smooth (regularity) solution
f^{n}
to the approximate problem (
3.1
). Moreover, we derive the conservation laws (associated with mass, moment, angular moment and energy), the entropy identity and the uniform bounds of the approximated problem (
3.1
).

Lemma 3.9

(Existence, Uniqueness and Positivity)
.

Consider the approximated initial data
f_{\alpha,0}^{n}
constructed in Lemmas
3.1
-
3.2
and the approximated collision operator
\tilde{Q}_{n}=(\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n})_{\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}}
constructed in (
3.16
). Then the approximated problem (
3.1
) admits a unique distributional solution
f^{n}=(f^{n}_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}}
satisfying that for any fixed
T>0
,

(1)

for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\},f^{n}_{\alpha}\in C([0,+\infty);L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}))
and

f^{n}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi)\geq\tfrac{1}{n}\mathrm{exp}[-c_{0}(|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2})]\quad\textrm{for all }\ (t,x,\xi)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3};

(2)

for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\},f^{n}_{\alpha}\in C([0,+\infty);L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}))
,

f^{n}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)\geq\tfrac{1}{n}\mathrm{exp}[-c_{0}(|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+I)]\quad\textrm{for all }\ (t,x,\xi,I)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\,.

Here the constant
c_{0}=c_{0}(n,T)>0
.

Proof.

Step 1. Existence and uniqueness.
For simplicity, we denote by
\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha}=\xi\in\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha}=\mathbb{R}^{3}
for
\xi\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
and
\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha}=(\xi,I)\in\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha}=\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
.

For all
f_{\alpha}(x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha};dxd\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})
with the norm
\|f_{\alpha}\|_{L^{1}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha}}|f_{\alpha}(x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})|dxd\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha}
, we define

\displaystyle U(t)f_{\alpha}(x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})=f_{\alpha}(x-t\xi,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})\,.

It is easy to see that
U(t+\tau)f_{\alpha}(x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})=U(t)U(\tau)f_{\alpha}(x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})
and
U(0)f_{\alpha}(x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})=f_{\alpha}(x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})
. Namely,
\{U(t)\}_{t\geq 0}
is a operator semigroup on
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha};dxd\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})
. Moreover,

A(f_{\alpha})=\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow 0^{+}}\tfrac{U(t)f_{\alpha}-f_{\alpha}}{t}=-\xi\cdot\nabla_{x}f_{\alpha}\,,

which means that
A(f_{\alpha})
is the generator of the operator semigroup
U(t)f(x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})
. Denote by

\displaystyle X=\{f=(f_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}}|f_{\alpha}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha};dxd\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})\}=\prod_{\alpha=1}^{s}L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha};dxd\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})

endowed with the norm
\|f\|_{X}=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{s}\|f_{\alpha}\|_{L^{1}}<\infty
. By Lemma
3.7
, one has

\displaystyle\|\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)-\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}(g,g)\|_{L^{1}}\leq C_{s,n}\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}\|f_{\beta}-g_{\beta}\|_{L^{1}},\ \|\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)\|_{L^{1}}\leq C_{s,n}\|f_{\alpha}\|_{L^{1}}\,,

which means that the map
G:X\to X
such that

f=(f_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}}\mapsto\tilde{Q}_{n}(f,f)=(\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}(f,f))_{\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}}

is Lipschitz continuous. Then there is a unique
f\in C([0,+\infty);X)
solving the following Cauchy problem (
3.1
) with the form

(3.28)

\tfrac{d}{dt}f=A(f)+G(f)\,,\ f|_{t=0}=f_{0}:=(f^{n}_{\alpha,0})_{\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}}

with the mild form. Namely, for all
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
, the components
f_{\alpha}
of
f
satisfy

\displaystyle f^{n}_{\alpha}(t,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})=f^{n}_{\alpha,0}(x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})+\int_{0}^{t}G_{\alpha}(f^{n}_{\alpha}(\tau,x-(t-\tau)\xi,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha}))d\tau\quad a.e.\ (x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha}\,.

Here
A(f)=(A(f_{\alpha}))_{\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}}
and
G(f)=(G_{\alpha}(f_{\alpha}))_{\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}}
with
G_{\alpha}(f_{\alpha})=\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)
. By the Lemma
2.9
, one infers that
f^{n}
is also the unique distributional solution of problem (
3.1
).

Step 2. Positivity.
We first consider the following auxiliary Cauchy problem

(3.29)

\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t}g^{n}+\xi\cdot\nabla_{x}g^{n}=F(g^{n})\,,\ g^{n}|_{t=0}=f^{n}_{0}\,,

where
g^{n}=(g^{n}_{\alpha}(t,x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha}))_{\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}}
,
F(g^{n})=(F_{1}(g^{n}),\cdots,F_{s}(g^{n}))
,
F_{\alpha}(g^{n})=\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}^{+}(g^{n}_{\star},g^{n}_{\star})-\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(g^{n}_{\star})g^{n}_{\alpha}
with
g_{\star}=(|g^{n}_{\alpha}|)_{\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}}
. By Lemma
3.7
, one infers that
F_{\alpha}(g^{n})
is a bounded and Lipschitz continuous operator on
X
. Then the Cauchy problem (
3.29
) admits a unique mild solution
g^{n}\in C([0,+\infty);X)
. Lemma
2.10
then implies that for all
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
,

\displaystyle g^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})-g^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(0,x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})\mathrm{exp}[-\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(g^{n}_{\star})^{\sharp}(\tau,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})d\tau]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(g^{n}_{\star},g^{n}_{\star})^{\sharp}(\tau,x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})\mathrm{exp}[-\int_{\tau}^{t}\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(g^{n}_{\star})^{\sharp}(\tau^{\prime},x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})d\tau^{\prime}]d\tau\,,

where
g^{\sharp}(t,x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})=g(t,x+t\xi,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})
. By changing the variable
x+t\xi\rightarrow x
and using the initial condition
g^{n}|_{t=0}=f_{0}^{n}
, one gains that for all
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
,

\displaystyle g^{n}_{\alpha}(t,x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})=

\displaystyle f_{\alpha,0}^{n}(x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})\mathrm{exp}[-\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(g^{n}_{\star})(\tau,x-(t-\tau)\xi,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})d\tau]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(g^{n}_{\star},g^{n}_{\star})(\tau,x-(t-\tau)\xi,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})\mathrm{exp}[-\int_{\tau}^{t}\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(g^{n}_{\star})(\tau^{\prime},x-(t-\tau^{\prime})\xi,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})d\tau^{\prime}]d\tau.

Note that
f_{\alpha,0}^{n}(x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})>0
and
\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(g^{n}_{\star},g^{n}_{\star})(\tau,x-(t-\tau)\xi,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})\geq 0
. One concludes that the solution
g^{n}_{\alpha}(t,x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})\geq 0
for all
(t,x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha}
and for all
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
. Due to
g_{\alpha}\geq 0
, one has
F(g^{n})=G(g^{n})
. This means that
g^{n}
is also a solution to the problem (
3.28
) with nonnegative components
g_{\alpha}\geq 0
(
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
). By the uniqueness of the approximate problem (
3.1
) (or equivalently (
3.28
)), we know that
f^{n}_{\alpha}(t,x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})=g^{n}_{\alpha}(t,x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})\geq 0
for all
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
.

At the end, we prove the positive lower bound of the components
f_{\alpha}(t,x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})\geq 0
for all
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
. Observe that

\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t}f^{n}_{\alpha}+\xi\cdot\nabla_{x}f^{n}_{\alpha}=\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})-\tilde{Q}^{-}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})\geq-\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})f^{n}_{\alpha}\,.

Moreover, by Lemmas
3.4
-
3.5
-
3.6
, one knows that
B_{2\alpha\beta,n}
,
B_{1\alpha\beta,n}
,
B_{1\beta\alpha,n}
and
B_{0\alpha\beta,n}
admit some upper bounds
C_{n,s}>0
. Then one easily has
L_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})=\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})\leq C_{n,s}\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}\|f_{\beta}^{n}\|_{L^{1}}
for all
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
, which deduces from
(N_{n}(f^{n}))^{-1}\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}\|f_{\beta}^{n}\|_{L^{1}}\leq n
that

\displaystyle f^{n}_{\alpha}\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})=(N_{n}(f^{n}))^{-1}{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})f^{n}_{\alpha}\leq C_{1}(n)f^{n}_{\alpha}

for some constant
C_{1}(n)>0
. Consequently,

\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t}f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}=\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})^{\sharp}\geq-C_{1}(n)f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}.

Then for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})\geq e^{-C_{1}(n)t}f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(0,x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})\geq e^{-C_{1}(n)T}f_{\alpha,0}^{n}(x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})\,,

which, together with Lemma
3.1
and Lemma
3.2
, means that

\displaystyle f^{n}_{\alpha}(t,x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})\geq e^{-C_{1}(n)T}f_{\alpha,0}^{n}(x-t\xi,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})\geq\tfrac{1}{n}\mathrm{exp}(-\tfrac{|x-t\xi|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+2C_{1}(n)T}{2})\,,\qquad\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}\,,

\displaystyle f^{n}_{\alpha}(t,x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})\geq e^{-C_{1}(n)T}f_{\alpha,0}^{n}(x-t\xi,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})\geq\tfrac{1}{n}\mathrm{exp}(-\tfrac{|x-t\xi|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+I+2C_{1}(n)T}{2})\,,\quad\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}\,.

Note that
|x-t\xi|^{2}\leq 2|x|^{2}+2T^{2}|\xi|^{2}
for all
t\in[0,T]
. Take
c_{0}=\max\{1,T^{2}+\frac{1}{2},C_{1}(n)T\}>0
. Then

\displaystyle f^{n}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi)\geq\tfrac{1}{n}\mathrm{exp}(-c_{0}(|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}))\,,\qquad\quad\ \ \alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}\,,

\displaystyle f^{n}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)\geq\tfrac{1}{n}\mathrm{exp}(-c_{0}(|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+I))\,,\quad\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}\,.

Therefore, the proof of Lemma
3.9
is finished.
∎

Now we give the regularity results on the approximated problem (
3.1
).

Lemma 3.10

(Regularity)
.

Let
T>0
and the integers
m,k\geq 1
be arbitrarily fixed. Assume that
f^{n}
is the distributional solution to the approximated problem (
3.1
) constructed in Lemma
3.9
. Then there is a constant
C=C(n,m,k,T)>0
such that the solution
f^{n}
further obeys the following statements:

(1)
For all
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
and any multi-index
\boldsymbol{\zeta}=(\zeta_{0},\zeta_{1},\cdots,\zeta_{6})\in\mathbb{N}^{7}
with
|\boldsymbol{\zeta}|=\sum_{i=0}^{6}\zeta_{i}\leq m
, we have

D^{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}f^{n}_{\alpha}\in L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap L^{\infty}((0,T);L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3};(1+|x|^{k}+|\xi|^{k})dxd\xi))\,,

and

\sup_{(t,x,\xi)\in(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}|D^{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}f^{n}_{\alpha}|+\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(1+|x|^{k}+|\xi|^{k})|D^{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}f^{n}_{\alpha}|dxd\xi\leq C\,;

(2)
For all
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
and any multi-index
\boldsymbol{\gamma}=(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{6},\gamma_{7})\in\mathbb{N}^{8}
with
|\boldsymbol{\gamma}|=\sum_{i=0}^{7}\gamma_{i}\leq m
, we have

D^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}f^{n}_{\alpha}\in L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})\cap L^{\infty}((0,T);L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+};(1+|x|^{k}+|\xi|^{k}+I^{k})dxd\xi dI))\,,

and

\sup_{(t,x,\xi,I)\in(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|D^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}f^{n}_{\alpha}|+\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(1+|x|^{k}+|\xi|^{k}+I^{k})|D^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}f^{n}_{\alpha}|dxd\xi dI\leq C\,.

Proof.

In order to prove the smoothness of the approximated solution, we mainly focus on the following mild form of (
3.1
) constructed in Lemma
3.9
:

(3.30)

f^{n}_{\alpha}(t,x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})=U(t)f^{n}_{\alpha,0}(x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})+\int_{0}^{t}U(t-\tau)\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})(\tau,x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})d\tau\,,

where
U(t)f(x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})=f(x-t\xi,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})
is the operator semigroup. Here
\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha}=\xi\in\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha}=\mathbb{R}^{3}
for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
and
\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha}=(\xi,I)\in\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha}=\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
. Observe that the approximated initial data and collision kernels are all smooth. Then, by applying the derivative operators
D^{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}
and
D^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
to (
3.30
), employing the Grönwall inequality, combining the Lemmas
3.7
-
3.8
and using the Induction Principle for orders of derivatives, one can conclude the results of this lemma. For simplicity, we omit the details here.
∎

Lemma 3.11
.

Assume that
f^{n}
is the distributional solution to the approximated problem (
3.1
) constructed in Lemma
3.9
. Then it further enjoys the following properties:

(1)

Mass, momentum, energy and angular momentum are all conserved. Hence, for all
t\geq 0
and
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
, we have

\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\[5.69054pt]
\xi\\[5.69054pt]
|\xi|^{2}\\[5.69054pt]
|x-t\xi|^{2}\\[5.69054pt]
\end{array}\right)dxd\xi=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{\alpha,0}^{n}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\[5.69054pt]
\xi\\[5.69054pt]
|\xi|^{2}\\[5.69054pt]
|x|^{2}\\[5.69054pt]
\end{array}\right)dxd\xi;

For all
t>0
and
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, we have

\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\[5.69054pt]
\xi\\[5.69054pt]
\frac{1}{2}m_{\alpha}|\xi|^{2}+I\\[5.69054pt]
|x-t\xi|^{2}\\[5.69054pt]
\end{array}\right)dxd\xi dI=\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f_{\alpha,0}^{n}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\[5.69054pt]
\xi\\[5.69054pt]
\frac{1}{2}m_{\alpha}|\xi|^{2}+I\\[5.69054pt]
|x|^{2}\\[5.69054pt]
\end{array}\right)dxd\xi dI.

(2)

Entropy identity is true. Hence for all
t\geq 0
,

(3.31)

\displaystyle H(f^{n}_{0})

\displaystyle=H(f^{n})(t)+\tfrac{1}{4}\sum\limits_{\alpha=1}^{s_{0}}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tilde{e}_{\alpha}^{n}(\tau,x,\xi)dxd\xi

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{4}\sum\limits_{\alpha=s_{0}+1}^{s}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tilde{e}_{\alpha}^{n}(\tau,x,\xi,I)dxd\xi dI,

where the density of entropy dissipation

(3.32)

\displaystyle\tilde{e}_{\alpha}^{n}

\displaystyle=(N_{n}(f^{n}))^{-1}\sum\limits_{\beta=1}^{s}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})^{3}}(\tfrac{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}{f_{\alpha}^{n}}^{\prime}{f_{\beta_{*}}^{n}}^{\prime}}{f^{n}_{\alpha}f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}-1)

\displaystyle\times\log(\tfrac{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}{f_{\alpha}^{n}}^{\prime}{f_{\beta_{*}}^{n}}^{\prime}}{f^{n}_{\alpha}f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}})\tfrac{f^{n}_{\alpha}f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}}{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}W_{\alpha\beta,n}d\xi_{*}d\xi^{\prime}d\xi^{\prime}_{*}dI_{*}dI^{\prime}dI^{\prime}_{*},

the
H
-functional
H(f)
is defined in (
1.45
), and the transition probabilities
W_{\alpha\beta,n}
corresponds to the collision kernels
B_{2\alpha\beta,n}
,
B_{1\alpha\beta,n}
,
B_{1\beta\alpha,n}
and
B_{0\alpha\beta,n}
constructed in Lemmas
3.4
-
3.5
-
3.6
.

(3)

For all
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
and
t\geq 0
, we have

(3.33)

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi)(1+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2})dxd\xi\leq

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi)(1+2|x|^{2}+(2t^{2}+1)|\xi|^{2})dxd\xi,

and

(3.34)

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{t\geq 0}

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi)|\log f^{n}_{\alpha}|dxd\xi+\tfrac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{+\infty}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tilde{e}^{n}_{\alpha}dxd\xi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi)(|\log f^{n}_{\alpha,0}|+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2})dxd\xi+C_{1};

For all
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
and
t\geq 0
, we have

(3.35)

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)(1+|x|^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}m_{\alpha}|\xi|^{2}+I)dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi,I)\{1+2|x|^{2}+(2t^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}m_{\alpha})|\xi|^{2}+(\tfrac{4}{m_{\alpha}}t^{2}+1)I)\}dxd\xi dI,

and

(3.36)

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{t\geq 0}

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)|\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha})|dxd\xi dI+\tfrac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{+\infty}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tilde{e}^{n}_{\alpha}dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi,I)\{|\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha,0})|+M_{\alpha}(|x|^{2}+m_{\alpha}|\xi|^{2}+2I)\}dxd\xi dI+C_{1},

where
M_{\alpha}=\max\{1,\frac{1}{m_{\alpha}}\}
and the constant
C_{1}>0
is independent of
n
.

Proof.

Note that the collision kernels
B_{2\alpha\beta,n}
,
B_{1\alpha\beta,n}
,
B_{1\beta\alpha,n}
and
B_{0\alpha\beta,n}
constructed in Lemmas
3.4
-
3.5
-
3.6
enjoy the same symmetry properties of
B_{2\alpha\beta}
,
B_{1\alpha\beta}
,
B_{1\beta\alpha}
and
B_{0\alpha\beta}
in (
1.21
)-(
1.23
)-(
1.25
)-(
1.27
). Then the all structure properties of the collision operator
Q
will also be valid for the approximated collision operator
\tilde{Q}_{n}=(\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n})_{\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}}
constructed in (
3.16
). Then the collision invariant form of
Q

\varphi_{i}=ae_{i}+b\mathbf{m}\xi+c(\mathbf{m}|\xi|^{2}+2\mathbf{I}),\quad i=1,\cdots,s

is also that of
\tilde{Q}_{n}
, where
a,c\in\mathbb{R}
and
b\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
are arbitrary, see Proposition
2.2
. As a result, multiplying the equation (
3.1
) by the collision invariants and integrating by parts over
(t,x,\xi)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
and
(t,x,\xi,I)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, one can obtain the conclusion (
1
) in this lemma. The conclusion (
2
) of this lemma can be derived from the same arguments of (
2.4
) resulted from Section 2.2 of
[
12
]
.

Next we focus on the conclusion (
3
) of this lemma. By the conclusion (
1
), one infers that for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
and
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha}|x|^{2}dxd\xi dI\leq

\displaystyle 2\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha}(|x-t\xi|^{2}+t^{2}|\xi|^{2})dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}(|x|^{2}+t^{2}|\xi|^{2}+\tfrac{2}{m_{\alpha}}t^{2}I)dxd\xi dI.

Then there holds

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha}(1+|x|^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}m_{\alpha}|\xi|^{2}+I)dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}\{1+2|x|^{2}+(2t^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}m_{\alpha})|\xi|^{2}+(\tfrac{4}{m_{\alpha}}t^{2}+1)I)\}dxd\xi dI.

Similarly for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
and
t\geq 0
, one has

\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha}(1+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2})dxd\xi\leq\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}(1+2|x|^{2}+(2t^{2}+1)|\xi|^{2})dxd\xi.

Now we prove the estimate (
3.34
) for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
. Note that

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha}|\log f^{n}_{\alpha}|dxd\xi=

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha}\log f^{n}_{\alpha}dxd\xi-2\iint_{0<f^{n}_{\alpha}\leq 1}f^{n}_{\alpha}\log f^{n}_{\alpha}dxd\xi.

The entropy identity (
3.31
) shows that

\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha}\log f^{n}_{\alpha}dxd\xi\leq\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}\log f^{n}_{\alpha,0}dxd\xi\leq\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}|\log f^{n}_{\alpha,0}|dxd\xi

for a constant
C>0
independent of
n
. Observe that
t\log\frac{1}{t}\leq C_{0}\sqrt{t}
for
t\in(0,1)
for some
C_{0}\geq 0
. Then

\displaystyle-2\iint_{0<f^{n}_{\alpha}\leq 1}f^{n}_{\alpha}\log f^{n}_{\alpha}dxd\xi=

\displaystyle-2\iint_{0<f^{n}_{\alpha}\leq e^{-|x|^{2}-|\xi|^{2}}}f^{n}_{\alpha}\log f^{n}_{\alpha}dxd\xi-2\iint_{e^{-|x|^{2}-|\xi|^{2}}<f^{n}_{\alpha}\leq 1}f^{n}_{\alpha}\log f^{n}_{\alpha}dxd\xi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2C_{0}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}}{2}}dxd\xi+2\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha}(|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2})dxd\xi\,.

Notice that
\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha}(|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2})dxd\xi=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha}(|x-t\xi|^{2}+|\xi|^{2})dxd\xi=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}(|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2})dxd\xi
by using the conclusion (
1
) of this lemma. As a result, for some constant
C_{1}>0
independent of
n
,

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha}|\log f^{n}_{\alpha}|dxd\xi\leq\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}(|\log f^{n}_{\alpha,0}|+2|x|^{2}+2|\xi|^{2})dxd\xi+C_{1}\,.

Together with (
3.31
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{t\geq 0}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha}|\log f^{n}_{\alpha}|dxd\xi+\tfrac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{+\infty}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tilde{e}^{n}_{\alpha}dxd\xi\leq 2\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}(|\log f^{n}_{\alpha,0}|+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2})dxd\xi+C_{1}.

For the cases
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
