, we only deal with the integral

-\iiint_{0<I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha}\leq 1}f^{n}_{\alpha}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha})dxd\xi dI\,.

By using the decomposition
\{0<I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha}\leq 1\}=\{0<I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha}\leq e^{-|x|^{2}-|\xi^{2}|-I}\}\cup\{e^{-|x|^{2}-|\xi^{2}|-I}<I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha}\leq 1\}
, one infers that

\displaystyle-\iiint_{0<I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha}\leq 1}f^{n}_{\alpha}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha})dxd\xi dI\leq

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha}(|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+I)dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle+C_{0}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}+|\xi^{2}|+I}{2}}dxd\xi dI\,.

Following the same arguments in the cases
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
, one can obtain that

(3.37)

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{t\geq 0}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha}|\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha})|dxd\xi dI+\tfrac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{+\infty}dt\iiint_{R^{3}\times R^{3}\times R_{+}}\tilde{e}^{n}_{\alpha}dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha,0}\{|\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha,0})|+M_{\alpha}(|x|^{2}+m_{\alpha}|\xi|^{2}+2I)\}dxd\xi dI+C_{1},

where
M_{\alpha}=\max\{1,\frac{1}{m_{\alpha}}\}
. This means that the conclusion (
3
) holds. Therefore, the proof of Lemma
3.11
is finished.
∎

4.
Weak
L^{1}
compactness for nonlinear collision operator

In this section, the main goal is to study the relatively weak
L^{1}
compactness of
(1+f^{n}_{\alpha})^{-1}\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})
for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
, where
f^{n}
is the mild solution to the approximated problem (
3.1
), and
\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})
is defined in (
3.16
). The ideas of this section are inspired by the DiPerna-Lions’ work
[
17
]
. We first consider the loss term part
(1+f^{n}_{\alpha})^{-1}\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}^{-}(f^{n},f^{n})
, where
\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}^{-}(f^{n},f^{n})=f^{n}_{\alpha}\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})
. Here the key point is to study the operators
\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})
by using the properties of
A_{2\alpha\beta,n}
,
A_{1\alpha\beta,n}
,
A_{1\beta\alpha,n}
and
A_{0\alpha\beta,n}
mentioned in Lemmas
3.4
-
3.5
-
3.6
. Then we investigate the relatively weak
L^{1}
compactness of the gain term part
(1+f^{n}_{\alpha})^{-1}\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}^{+}(f^{n},f^{n})
. The core of approach is to show the Arkeryd-type inequality
[
4
]
that for
K>1
,

(4.1)

\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})\leq\tfrac{1}{\log K}\tilde{e}^{n}_{\alpha}+K\tilde{Q}^{\mp}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})\,,

where
\tilde{e}^{n}_{\alpha}
is defined in (
3.32
). Then we can use the relatively weak
L^{1}
compactness of loss term part to obtain that of gain term part.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
T,R>0
. Assume that
f^{n}
is the unique non-negative distributional solution of (
3.1
) constructed in Lemma
3.9
. Then the following statements hold:

(1)

(1+f^{n}_{\alpha})^{-1}\tilde{Q}^{-}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})
is
relatively weakly compact in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}))
for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
;

(2)

(1+f^{n}_{\alpha})^{-1}\tilde{Q}^{-}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})
is
relatively weakly compact in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)))
for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots s\}
.

Proof.

Recall that by (
3.16
), one has
\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})=N_{n}(f^{n})^{-1}\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})
. For
\alpha,\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, it is implied by (
3.16
) and Lemma
3.4
that for any
0<R<\infty
,

\displaystyle L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})=\iint\limits_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}A_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*})f^{n}_{\beta}(t,x,\xi_{*},I_{*})dI_{*}d\xi_{*}\,,

\displaystyle\lim_{|\xi|,I\to+\infty}\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}(1+|\xi|^{2}+I)^{-1}\int_{|z-\xi|\leq R}\int_{0<\eta-I<R}A_{2\alpha\beta,n}(z,\eta)dzd\eta\rightarrow 0\,.

Then for any
\epsilon>0
there is a
C_{\epsilon}>0
independent of
n
such that

(4.2)

\displaystyle\int_{|z-\xi|\leq R}\int_{0<\eta-I<R}A_{2\alpha\beta,n}(z,\eta)dzd\eta\leq\epsilon(1+|\xi|^{2}+I)+C_{\epsilon}\,.

One then infers that for
\alpha,\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
,

(4.3)

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})(t,x,\xi,I)dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta}(t,x,\xi_{*},I_{*})(\iint_{B_{R}\times(0,R)}A_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*})d\xi dI)dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta}(t,x,\xi_{*},I_{*})(1+|\xi_{*}|^{2}+I_{*})dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}+C_{\epsilon}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta}(t,x,\xi_{*},I_{*})dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}.

Together with Lemma
3.5
and Lemma
3.6
, the similar arguments above imply that for
\alpha,\beta\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
,

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}}L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})(t,x,\xi)dxd\xi\leq\epsilon\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\beta}(t,x,\xi_{*})(1+|\xi_{*}|^{2})dxd\xi_{*}+C_{\epsilon}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\beta}(t,x,\xi_{*})dxd\xi_{*},

and for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\},\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
,

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}}L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})(t,x,\xi)dxd\xi\leq\epsilon\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta}(t,x,\xi_{*},I_{*})(1+|\xi_{*}|^{2}+I_{*})dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}

\displaystyle+C_{\epsilon}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta}(t,x,\xi_{*},I_{*})dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*},

and for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\},\beta\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
,

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})(t,x,\xi,I)dxd\xi dI\leq\epsilon\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\beta}(t,x,\xi_{*})(1+|\xi_{*}|^{2})dxd\xi_{*}

\displaystyle+C_{\epsilon}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\beta}(t,x,\xi_{*})dxd\xi_{*}\,.

By the definition of
\tilde{Q}^{-}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})
in (
3.16
), it is easy to see

(4.4)

0\leq\tfrac{1}{1+f^{n}_{\alpha}}\tilde{Q}^{-}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})\leq L_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})=\sum\limits_{\beta=1}^{s}L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})\,.

Together with the uniform bounds in Lemma
3.11
, it infers that
(1+f^{n}_{\alpha})^{-1}\tilde{Q}^{-}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})
is bounded in
L^{1}
for all
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
.

For
\alpha,\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, denote by
L^{m}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})
the operators corresponding to the cutoff kernels
A^{m}_{\alpha\beta,n}(z,\eta)=A_{\alpha\beta,n}(z,\eta)\mathbf{1}_{|z|\leq m}\mathbf{1}_{0<\eta<m}
(
m>0
). Hence,

\displaystyle L^{m}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}A^{m}_{\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*})f_{\beta*}d\xi_{*}dI_{*}\,.

Let

a^{m}_{2\alpha\beta,n}=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}A_{2\alpha\beta,n}(z,\eta)\mathbf{1}_{|z|\leq m}\mathbf{1}_{0<\eta<m}dzd\eta>0,\ \Psi(t)=t(\log t)^{+}.

Observe that
(\log x)^{+}=(\log\frac{x}{y}y)^{+}\leq(\log\frac{x}{y})^{+}+|\log y|
for all
x,y>0
, which means that
\Psi(x)\leq y\Psi(\frac{x}{y})+x|\log y|
. It then holds

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}\Psi(L^{m}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n}))dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle a^{m}_{2\alpha\beta,n}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}\Psi(\tfrac{L^{m}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})}{a^{m}_{2\alpha\beta,n}})dxd\xi dI+|\log a^{m}_{2\alpha\beta,n}|\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}L^{m}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})dxd\xi dI\,.

Note that

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}L^{m}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})dxd\xi dI=

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}A^{m}_{\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*})f_{\beta_{*}}d\xi_{*}dI_{*}dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f_{\beta_{*}}\iint_{B_{R}\times(0,R)}A^{m}_{\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*})d\xi dIdxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle a^{m}_{2\alpha\beta,n}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f_{\beta_{*}}dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}\,.

Owing to the convexity of
\Psi
, the Jensen’s inequality indicates that

\displaystyle a^{m}_{2\alpha\beta,n}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}\Psi(\tfrac{L^{m}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})}{a^{m}_{2\alpha\beta,n}})dxd\xi dI\leq\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}L^{m}_{\alpha\beta,n}(\Psi(f^{n}))dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq a^{m}_{2\alpha\beta,n}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\Psi(f^{n}_{\beta_{*}})dxd\xi dI=a^{m}_{2\alpha\beta,n}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}(\log f^{n}_{\beta_{*}})^{+}dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}\,.

As a result,

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}\Psi(L^{m}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n}))dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle a^{m}_{2\alpha\beta,n}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}(\log f^{n}_{\beta_{*}})^{+}dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}+a^{m}_{2\alpha\beta,n}|\log a^{m}_{2\alpha\beta,n}|\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}.

By using the uniform bounds (
3.35
) and (
3.36
), one has

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}(\log f^{n}_{\beta_{*}})^{+}dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}+\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}\log(I^{1-\frac{\delta(\beta)}{2}}f^{n}_{\beta_{*}})^{+}dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}+\tfrac{\delta(\beta)}{2}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}(1+I_{*})dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}\leq C(T).

Moreover, taking
\xi=0
,
I=0
,
\epsilon=1
and
R=m
in (
4.2
), one has
0<a^{m}_{2\alpha\beta,n}\leq C_{m}
for all
n\geq 1
. Note that
x(\log x)^{-}\leq C_{0}\sqrt{x}
for some constant
C_{0}>0
. Then for all
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle a^{m}_{2\alpha\beta,n}|\log a^{m}_{2\alpha\beta,n}|=a^{m}_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\log a^{m}_{2\alpha\beta,n})^{+}+a^{m}_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\log a^{m}_{2\alpha\beta,n})^{-}\leq C_{m}(\log C_{m})^{+}+C_{0}\sqrt{C_{m}}\,.

Consequently, the above estimates conclude that for
t\in[0,T]
,

(4.5)

\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}\Psi(L^{m}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n}))(t)dxd\xi dI\leq C(T,m)\,,\ \alpha,\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}\,,

where the constant
C(T,m)>0
only depending on
T
and
m
. Note that
\frac{\Psi(t)}{t}=(\log t)^{+}\to+\infty
as
t\to+\infty
. Then De La Vallée-Poussin Criterion (Lemma
2.2
) shows that
\{L^{m}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})\}_{n\geq 1}\subseteq L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)))
is equi-integrable.

We then prove the tightness of the sequence
\{L^{m}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})\}_{n\geq 1}
. Indeed, by the uniform bounds (
3.35
),

\displaystyle\int_{|x|>M}dx\iint_{B_{R}\times(0,R)}L^{m}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})d\xi dI\leq

\displaystyle a^{m}_{2\alpha\beta,n}\int_{|x|>M}dx\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}d\xi_{*}dI_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C_{m}}{M^{2}}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta*}|x|^{2}dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}\rightarrow 0\quad(M\rightarrow+\infty).

Consequently, by using Dunford-Pettis Theorem (Lemma
2.1
), we know that for any fixed
m>0
,
\{L^{m}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})\}_{n\geq 1}
is relatively weakly compact in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)))
for all
\alpha,\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
.

Next we show the relatively weak compactness of
\{L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})\}_{n\geq 1}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)))
for all
\alpha,\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
. Note that by the uniform bounds (
3.35
),

\displaystyle\|L^{m}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})(t)-L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})(t)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}dxd\xi dI\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}A_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*})f^{n}_{\beta*}(\mathbf{1}_{|\xi-\xi_{*}|>m}+\mathbf{1}_{I+I_{*}>m})d\xi_{*}dI_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta*}(\mathbf{1}_{|\xi_{*}|>m-R}+\mathbf{1}_{I_{*}>m-R})dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}\iint_{B_{R}\times(0,R)}A_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*})d\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C_{\epsilon}}{m-R}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta*}\cdot I_{*}dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}+\tfrac{C_{\epsilon}}{(m-R)^{2}}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta*}|\xi_{*}|^{2}dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\epsilon\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta*}(1+|\xi_{*}|^{2}+I_{*})dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}\rightarrow 0\quad(m\rightarrow\infty\,,\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+})

holds for all
t\in[0,T]
. Therefore, the relatively weak compactness of
\{L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})\}_{n\geq 1}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)))
is completed.

Following the previous arguments, one knows that
\{L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})\}_{n\geq 1}
is relatively weakly compact in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)))
for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
,
\beta\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
. Then for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
,

\displaystyle L_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})=\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})

is relatively weakly compact in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)))
. By (
4.4
), we know that the sequence
\{\frac{1}{1+f^{n}_{\alpha}}\tilde{Q}^{-}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})\}_{n\geq 1}
is relatively weakly compact in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)))
for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
. Similarly, we can also prove that
\{\frac{1}{1+f^{n}_{\alpha}}\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}^{-}(f^{n},f^{n})\}_{n\geq 1}
is relatively weakly compact in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}))
for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
. The proof of Lemma
4.1
is therefore finished.
∎

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
T,R>0
. Assume that
f^{n}
is the unique non-negative distributional solution of (
3.1
) constructed in Lemma
3.9
. Then the following statements hold:

(1)

For
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
,

(1+f^{n}_{\alpha})^{-1}\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})
is
relatively weakly compact in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}))
;

(2)

For
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots s\}
,

(1+f^{n}_{\alpha})^{-1}\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})
is
relatively weakly compact in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)))
.

Proof.

We first prove the Arkeryd inequality (
4.1
), hence, for all
K>1
and all
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
,

\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})\leq\tfrac{1}{\log K}\tilde{e}^{n}_{\alpha}+K\tilde{Q}^{\mp}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n}),

where
\tilde{e}^{n}_{\alpha}
is defined in (
3.32
). Denote by
\tilde{e}^{n}_{\alpha}=N_{n}(f^{n})^{-1}\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}e^{n}_{\alpha\beta}
with
N_{n}(f^{n})
defined in (
3.17
) and

\displaystyle e^{n}_{\alpha\beta}(t,x,\mathbf{Z})=\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})^{3}}W_{\alpha\beta,n}(\tfrac{f_{\alpha}^{n}{{}^{\prime}}f_{\beta*}^{n}{{}^{\prime}}}{(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}-\tfrac{f^{n}_{\alpha}f^{n}_{\beta*}}{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}})

\displaystyle\times\log\tfrac{f_{\alpha}^{n}{{}^{\prime}}f_{\beta*}^{n}{{}^{\prime}}I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}{f^{n}_{\alpha}f^{n}_{\beta*}(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}d\xi_{*}d\xi^{\prime}d\xi^{\prime}_{*}dI_{*}dI^{\prime}dI^{\prime}_{*}\,.

We introduce the compared set

A^{n}_{K}=\{(\xi,\xi_{*},I,I_{*},\omega):\tfrac{f_{\alpha}^{n}{{}^{\prime}}f_{\beta_{*}}^{n}{{}^{\prime}}}{(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}\geq K\tfrac{f^{n}_{\alpha}f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}}{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}\},\quad B^{n}_{K}=(A^{n}_{K})^{c}.

Then one has

\displaystyle\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})^{3}}W_{\alpha\beta,n}\tfrac{f_{\alpha}^{n}{{}^{\prime}}f_{\beta*}^{n}{{}^{\prime}}}{(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}\mathbf{1}_{A^{n}_{K}}d\xi_{*}d\xi^{\prime}d\xi^{\prime}_{*}dI_{*}dI^{\prime}dI^{\prime}_{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})^{3}}W_{\alpha\beta,n}(\tfrac{f_{\alpha}^{n}{{}^{\prime}}f_{\beta*}^{n}{{}^{\prime}}}{(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}-\tfrac{f^{n}_{\alpha}f^{n}_{\beta*}}{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}})\mathbf{1}_{A^{n}_{K}}d\xi_{*}d\xi^{\prime}d\xi^{\prime}_{*}dI_{*}dI^{\prime}dI^{\prime}_{*}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})^{3}}W_{\alpha\beta,n}\tfrac{f^{n}_{\alpha}f^{n}_{\beta*}}{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}\mathbf{1}_{A^{n}_{K}}d\xi_{*}d\xi^{\prime}d\xi^{\prime}_{*}dI_{*}dI^{\prime}dI^{\prime}_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\log K}e^{n}_{\alpha\beta}+\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})^{3}}W_{\alpha\beta,n}\tfrac{f^{n}_{\alpha}f^{n}_{\beta*}}{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}\mathbf{1}_{A^{n}_{K}}d\xi_{*}d\xi^{\prime}d\xi^{\prime}_{*}dI_{*}dI^{\prime}dI^{\prime}_{*}.

It then implies that for
K>1
,

\displaystyle Q^{+}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n},f^{n})=

\displaystyle\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})^{3}}W_{\alpha\beta,n}\tfrac{f_{\alpha}^{n}{{}^{\prime}}f_{\beta*}^{n}{{}^{\prime}}}{(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}\mathbf{1}_{A^{n}_{K}}d\xi_{*}d\xi^{\prime}d\xi^{\prime}_{*}dI_{*}dI^{\prime}dI^{\prime}_{*}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})^{3}}W_{\alpha\beta,n}\tfrac{f_{\alpha}^{n}{{}^{\prime}}f_{\beta*}^{n}{{}^{\prime}}}{(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}\mathbf{1}_{B^{n}_{K}}d\xi_{*}d\xi^{\prime}d\xi^{\prime}_{*}dI_{*}dI^{\prime}dI^{\prime}_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\log K}e^{n}_{\alpha\beta}+\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})^{3}}W_{\alpha\beta,n}\tfrac{f^{n}_{\alpha}f^{n}_{\beta*}}{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}\mathbf{1}_{A^{n}_{K}}d\xi_{*}d\xi^{\prime}d\xi^{\prime}_{*}dI_{*}dI^{\prime}dI^{\prime}_{*}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle K\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})^{3}}W_{\alpha\beta,n}\tfrac{f^{n}_{\alpha}f^{n}_{\beta*}}{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}\mathbf{1}_{B^{n}_{K}}d\xi_{*}d\xi^{\prime}d\xi^{\prime}_{*}dI_{*}dI^{\prime}dI^{\prime}_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\log K}e^{n}_{\alpha\beta}+KQ^{-}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n},f^{n}).

Similarly, we can prove that for
\alpha,\beta\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
,

\displaystyle Q^{-}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n},f^{n})

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{\log K}e^{n}_{\alpha\beta}+KQ^{+}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n},f^{n})\,.

Therefore, the Arkeryd inequality (
4.1
) follows from the previous arguments.

By Lemma
3.11
, one knows that
\tilde{e}^{n}_{\alpha}
is bounded in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
and is bounded in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
. Then the Arkeryd inequality (
4.1
) and Lemma
4.1
imply that for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}(\mathbf{1}_{A}+\mathbf{1}_{|x|\geq M})\tfrac{1}{1+f^{n}_{\alpha}}\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq K\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}(\mathbf{1}_{A}+\mathbf{1}_{|x|\geq M})\tfrac{1}{1+f^{n}_{\alpha}}\tilde{Q}^{-}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})dxd\xi dI+\tfrac{C^{\prime}_{T}}{\log K}\rightarrow 0

as
|A|\rightarrow 0
,
M\rightarrow+\infty
and
K\to+\infty
. Similarly, for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}}(\mathbf{1}_{A}+\mathbf{1}_{|x|\geq M})\tfrac{1}{1+f^{n}_{\alpha}}\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})dxd\xi\rightarrow 0\quad(|A|\rightarrow 0,M\rightarrow+\infty,K\to+\infty)\,.

Therefore, the Dunford-Pettis Theorem (Lemma
2.1
) shows that
(1+f^{n}_{\alpha})^{-1}\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})
is relatively weakly compact in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R})
for all
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
, and that for all
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\},(1+f^{n}_{\alpha})^{-1}\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})
is relatively weakly compact in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))
.
∎

5.
Convergence for nonlinear collision operator

In this section, we mainly prove the convergence for the quadratic form of the collision operator under the weak convergence
f^{n}\rightharpoonup f
in
L^{1}
as
n\to\infty
. However, only weak compactness of
f^{n}
is insufficient to verify the convergence of nonlinear form. As a result, we first employ the famous averaged velocity lemma, and similar the so-called averaged velocity-internal energy lemma to deal with the internal variable
I\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
. The goal of these lemmas is to enhance the compactness of the weak convergence sequence
f^{n}
.

5.1.
Averaged velocity(-internal energy) lemma

In this subsection, we mainly introduce the important
averaged velocity lemma
corresponding to the velocity variable
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
and the
averaged velocity-internal energy
lemma associated velocity variable
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
and internal energy variable
I\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
. The previous has been established in works
[
17
,
23
,
24
]
, and the later can be established by using the almost same arguments of
[
17
,
23
,
24
]
. We will omit the detailed proofs here for notational simplicity.

Lemma 5.1

(Averaged velocity-internal energy lemma)
.

Let
g^{n}(t,x,\xi,I)
weakly converge to
g(t,x,\xi,I)
in
L^{1}_{loc}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
, and
f^{n}(t,x,\xi,I)
weakly converge to
f(t,x,\xi,I)
in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
. Moreover, they satisfy

\tfrac{\partial f^{n}}{\partial t}+\xi\cdot\nabla_{x}f^{n}=g^{n}\quad\text{in}\ \mathscr{D}^{\prime}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}).

Set
\{\varphi^{n}(t,x,\xi,I)\}_{n\geq 1}\subset L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
converge to
\varphi(t,x,\xi,I)

a.e.

(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Then the sequence
\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}\varphi^{n}d\xi dI
strongly converges to
\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f\varphi d\xi dI
in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
.

Lemma 5.2

(Averaged velocity lemma)
.

Let
g^{n}(t,x,\xi)
weakly converge to
g(t,x,\xi)
in
L^{1}_{loc}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
, and
f^{n}(t,x,\xi)
weakly converge to
f(t,x,\xi)
in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Moreover, they satisfy

\tfrac{\partial f^{n}}{\partial t}+\xi\cdot\nabla_{x}f^{n}=g^{n}\quad\text{in}\ \mathscr{D}^{\prime}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}).

Set
\{\phi^{n}(t,x,\xi)\}_{n\geq 1}\subset L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
converge to
\phi(t,x,\xi)

a.e.

(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Then the sequence
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}\phi^{n}d\xi
strongly converges to
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f\phi d\xi
in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
.

Based on Lemma
5.1
and Lemma
5.2
above, one can generalize the previous results. We denote by
(E,\mu)
the given measure space.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let the bounded sequence
\{\varphi^{n}(t,x,\xi,I,e)\}_{n\geq 1}
in
L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+};L^{1}(E))
satisfy that there exists
\varphi(t,x,\xi,I,e)\in L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+};L^{1}(E))
such that

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|\varphi^{n}-\varphi\|_{L^{1}(E)}(t,x,\xi,I)=0\ a.e.\ (0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}.

Besides,
f^{n}(t,x,\xi,I)
and
g^{n}(t,x,\xi,I)
are given in Lemma
5.1
. Then the sequence
\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}\varphi^{n}d\xi dI
strongly converges to
\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f\varphi d\xi dI
in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times E)
.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let the bounded sequence
\{\phi^{n}(t,x,\xi,e)\}_{n\geq 1}
in
L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3};L^{1}(E))
satisfy that there exists
\phi\in L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3};L^{1}(E))
such that

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|\phi^{n}-\phi\|_{L^{1}(E)}(t,x,\xi)=0\ a.e.\ (0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}.

Besides,
f^{n}(t,x,\xi)
and
g^{n}(t,x,\xi)
are given in Lemma
5.2
. Then the sequence
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}\phi^{n}d\xi
strongly converges to
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f\phi d\xi
in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times E)
.

5.2.
Convergence of approximated solution
f^{n}_{\alpha}

For the approximated solutions
f^{n}_{\alpha}>0
(
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
) to (
3.1
) with uniform properties in Lemma
3.11
, we will study its convergence in this subsection. We first consider its renormalized form
g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}=\frac{1}{\delta}\log(1+\delta f^{n}_{\alpha})\ (\delta>0)
, which satisfies

(5.1)

\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t}g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}+\xi\cdot\nabla_{x}g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}=(1+\delta f^{n}_{\alpha})^{-1}\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n}).

It is easy to see that
0<g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}<f^{n}_{\alpha}
. Moreover, Lemma
2.3
, Corollary
2.1
and Lemma
3.11
show that as
n\to+\infty
,

\displaystyle f^{n}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi)\to f_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi)\quad\textrm{weakly in }\ L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})\,,\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}\,,

\displaystyle f^{n}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)\to f_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)\quad\textrm{weakly in }\ L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})\,,\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}\,,

in the sense of subsequence. Then there exist
\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}\in L^{1}
such that as
n\to+\infty
,

\displaystyle g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}(t,x,\xi)\to\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}(t,x,\xi)\quad\textrm{weakly in }\ L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})\,,\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}\,,

\displaystyle g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)\to\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)\quad\textrm{weakly in }\ L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})\,,\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}\,,

in the sense of subsequence. Here
\delta>0
. We remark that
f_{\alpha}\geq\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}\geq 0

a.e.(t,x,\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha})
, where
\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha}=\xi
for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
and
\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha}=(\xi,I)
for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
.

Besides, it infers by Lemma
4.1
and Lemma
4.2
in Section
4
that
(1+\delta f^{n}_{\alpha})^{-1}\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})
is weakly relatively compact in
L^{1}
. Lemma
5.2
indicates that for all
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
and
\phi(t,x,\xi)\in L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
,
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}\phi d\xi
is relatively compact in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Furthermore, Lemma
5.1
implies that for all
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
and
\varphi(t,x,\xi,I)\in L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
,
\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}\varphi d\xi dI
is relatively compact in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Hence, in the sense of subsequence as
n\to+\infty
,

(5.2)

\displaystyle\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}\phi d\xi-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}\phi d\xi\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}\to 0\quad\text{for}\ \alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\},

\displaystyle\|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}\varphi d\xi dI-\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}\varphi d\xi dI\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}\to 0\quad\text{for}\ \alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}.

Now we study the limit
\delta\to 0^{+}
.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
T>0
be fixed.

(1)

For
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
, one has

(5.3)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}\sup\limits_{t\in(0,T)}\|f^{n}_{\alpha}-g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}=0,\ \lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\|f_{\alpha}-\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}=0;

(2)

For
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, one has

(5.4)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}\sup\limits_{t\in(0,T)}\|f^{n}_{\alpha}-g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}=0,\ \lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\|f_{\alpha}-\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}=0.

Proof.

Let
\beta_{\delta}(t)=\frac{1}{\delta}\log(1+\delta t)\ (\delta>0)
. It is easy to see that for all
t\in[0,+\infty)
,

0\leq t-\beta_{\delta}(t)\leq\epsilon_{R}(\delta)t+t\mathbf{1}_{t\geq R},

where
\epsilon_{R}(\delta)\rightarrow 0
as
\delta\rightarrow 0_{+}.

Then, for all
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, we derive that as
\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}
and
R\rightarrow+\infty
,

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}\sup\limits_{t\in(0,T)}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|f^{n}_{\alpha}-g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}|dxd\xi dI=\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}\sup\limits_{t\in(0,T)}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|f^{n}_{\alpha}-\tfrac{1}{\delta}\log(1+\delta f^{n}_{\alpha})|dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon_{R}(\delta)\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}\sup\limits_{t\in(0,T)}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha}dxd\xi dI+\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}\sup\limits_{t\in(0,T)}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha}\mathbf{1}_{f^{n}_{\alpha}\geq R}dxd\xi dI\to 0\,.

Note that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}-f_{\alpha}|dxd\xi dI=\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}-f_{\alpha})sgn(\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}-f_{\alpha})dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}-g^{n}_{\delta\alpha})sgn(\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}-f_{\alpha})dxd\xi dI+\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|f^{n}_{\alpha}-g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}|dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(f^{n}_{\alpha}-f_{\alpha})sgn(\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}-f_{\alpha})dxd\xi dI\,.

Since
f^{n}_{\alpha},g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}
weakly converge to
f_{\alpha},\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}
in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
as
n\to+\infty
, one has

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}-g^{n}_{\delta\alpha})sgn(\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}-f_{\alpha})dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(f^{n}_{\alpha}-f_{\alpha})sgn(\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}-f_{\alpha})dxd\xi dI\to 0\ (n\to+\infty)\,.

Moreover, as
\delta\to 0^{+}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|f^{n}_{\alpha}-g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}|dxd\xi dI\leq T\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}\sup\limits_{t\in(0,T)}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|f^{n}_{\alpha}-g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}|dxd\xi dI\to 0\,.

Therefore, for all
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
,

\|\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}-f_{\alpha}\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}\rightarrow 0\quad(\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}).

Similarly, for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
and as
\delta\to 0^{+}
,

\sup\limits_{n}\sup\limits_{t\in(0,T)}\|f^{n}_{\alpha}-g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}\to 0\,,\ \|\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}-f_{\alpha}\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}\rightarrow 0\,.

The proof of Lemma
5.5
is then finished.
∎

Next we give the following enhanced averaged velocity (-internal energy) lemma by using the renormalized approximated solution
g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}=\frac{1}{\delta}\log(1+\delta f^{n}_{\alpha})
for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
.

Lemma 5.6
.

For all
\varphi(t,x,\xi,I)\in L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
,
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\},
and
1\leq p<\infty
,

\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha}\varphi d\xi dI\rightarrow\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f_{\alpha}\varphi d\xi dI\quad\text{strongly in }\ L^{p}((0,T);L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})).

Proof.

Observe that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(f^{n}_{\alpha}-f_{\alpha})\varphi d\xi dI|dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}T\sup_{n\geq 1}\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|f^{n}_{\alpha}-g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}|dxd\xi dI+\int_{0}^{T}dt\int_{R^{3}}|\iint_{R^{3}\times R_{+}}(g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}-\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha})\varphi d\xi dI|dx

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}-f_{\alpha}|dxd\xi dI.

It follows from (
5.2
) that as
n\to+\infty
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\int_{R^{3}}|\iint_{R^{3}\times R_{+}}(g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}-\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha})\varphi d\xi dI|dx\to 0\,.

Lemma
5.5
shows that as
\delta\to 0^{+}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|f^{n}_{\alpha}-g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}|dxd\xi dI+\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}-f_{\alpha}|dxd\xi dI\to 0\,.

As a result,
\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha}\varphi d\xi dI
strongly converges to
\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f_{\alpha}\varphi d\xi dI
in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
as
n\to+\infty
.

Furthermore, combining the bound (
3.35
)-(
3.36
) in Lemma
3.11
, one easily sees that

\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(f^{n}_{\alpha}-f_{\alpha})\varphi d\xi dI|dx\leq\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(f^{n}_{\alpha}+f_{\alpha})dxd\xi dI\leq C_{T}\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Then for all
1\leq p<\infty
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(f^{n}_{\alpha}-f_{\alpha})\varphi d\xi dI|dx)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{t\in(0,T)}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(f^{n}_{\alpha}-f_{\alpha})\varphi d\xi dI|dx)^{p-1}\cdot\int_{0}^{T}dt\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(f^{n}_{\alpha}-f_{\alpha})\varphi d\xi dI|dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{p-1}_{T}\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{p-1}\int_{0}^{T}dt\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(f^{n}_{\alpha}-f_{\alpha})\varphi d\xi dI|dx\rightarrow 0\quad(n\rightarrow\infty).

Thus, the proof of Lemma
5.6
is finished.
∎

Following the similar arguments above, we establish the following result:

Corollary 5.1
.

For all
\phi(t,x,\xi)\in L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
,
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\},
and
1\leq p<\infty
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\alpha}\phi d\xi\rightarrow\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{\alpha}\phi d\xi\quad\text{strongly in }\ L^{p}((0,T);L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})).

Next we study the continuity on the time variable
t
of the limit functions
f_{\alpha}
with
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
. Recall the notations
f^{\sharp}(t,x,\xi,I)=f(t,x+t\xi,\xi,I)
or
f^{\sharp}(t,x,\xi)=f(t,x+t\xi,\xi)
.

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
T>0
fixed. There is a constant
C_{T}>0
such that following statements hold:

(1)

For
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\},\ f_{\alpha},f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}\in C([0,\infty);L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}))_{+},

(5.5)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{\alpha}(1+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+|\log f_{\alpha}|)dxd\xi\leq C_{T};

(2)

For
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\},f_{\alpha},f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}\in C([0,\infty);L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}))_{+},

(5.6)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f_{\alpha}(1+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+I+|\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f_{\alpha})|)dxd\xi dI\leq C_{T}.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we only consider the complicated cases
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
. For any
\varphi(x,\xi,I)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
, one has

\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t))\varphi(x,\xi,I)dxd\xi dI=\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(f^{n}_{\alpha}(t)-f_{\alpha}(t))\varphi(x-t\xi,\xi,I)dxd\xi dI.

Since
f^{n}_{\alpha}
weakly converges to
f_{\alpha}
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
by Lemma
2.3
and Lemma
3.11
, one know that for any
t\in[0,T],\ f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)
is weakly converges to
f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
. By utilizing the weakly lower semicontinuity of norms, we obtain

\|f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}\leq\liminf\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}.

Note that for any
R,\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\|f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-g^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(t)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}+\|g^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(\tau)-f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}

\displaystyle+\|g^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(t)-g^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}+\|g^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(t)-g^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times(B_{R}\times(0,R))^{c})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\sup_{n\geq 1}\sup\limits_{t\in(0,T)}\|f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-g^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(t)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}+\sup_{n\geq 1}\|g^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(t)-g^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}

\displaystyle+\|g^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(t)-g^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times(B_{R}\times(0,R))^{c})}.

Due to
0<g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}<f^{n}_{\alpha}
, we know that for any
t\in[0,T],\ g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}(t)
is weakly converges to
\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}(t)
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
. Then there is a
\epsilon_{R}>0
with
\lim_{R\to+\infty}\epsilon_{R}=0
such that for all
0\leq t,\tau\leq T
,

\displaystyle\|g^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(t)-g^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times(B_{R}\times(0,R))^{c})}\leq\epsilon_{R}\,.

Moreover, by (
5.1
), we easily obtain

\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t}(g^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha})=(\tfrac{1}{1+\delta f^{n}_{\alpha}}\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n}))^{\sharp}.

Then for all
0\leq\tau,t\leq T
and
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
,

\|g^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(t)-g^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}\leq|\int_{\tau}^{t}d\sigma\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}\tfrac{1}{1+\delta f^{n}_{\alpha}}|\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})|dxd\xi dI|.

Since
(1+\delta f^{n}_{\alpha})^{-1}\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})
is relatively weakly compact in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))
by Lemma
4.1
and
4.2
, we derive that

\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}\|g^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(t)-g^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}\leq\omega_{\delta}(|t-\tau|),

where
\omega_{\delta}(\lambda)\rightarrow 0^{+}
as
\lambda\rightarrow 0^{+}
. Furthermore, Lemma
5.5
shows that as
\delta\to 0^{+}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\sup\limits_{t\in(0,T)}\|f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-g^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(t)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}\to 0\,.

Consequently, one has

\displaystyle\|f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}\leq 2\sup_{n\geq 1}\sup\limits_{t\in(0,T)}\|f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-g^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(t)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}+\epsilon_{R}+\omega_{\delta}(|t-\tau|)

for any
R,\delta>0
and
0\leq\tau,t\leq T
. We first take
\tau\to t
, then take
\delta\to 0^{+}
, and finally take
R\to+\infty
, so that

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\to t}\|f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}=0\,,

which means
f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)\in C([0,+\infty);L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}))
.

Next we prove that
f_{\alpha}(t)\in C([0,+\infty);L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}))
. Notices that

\displaystyle\|f_{\alpha}(t)-f_{\alpha}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}=\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|f_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)-f_{\alpha}(\tau,x,\xi,I)|dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|f_{\alpha}(t,x+\tau\xi,\xi,I)-f_{\alpha}(\tau,x+\tau\xi,\xi,I)|dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|f_{\alpha}(t,x+\tau\xi,\xi,I)-f_{\alpha}(t,x+t\xi,\xi,I)|dxd\xi dI+\|f_{\alpha}^{\sharp}(t)-f_{\alpha}^{\sharp}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|f_{\alpha}(t,x+(\tau-t)\xi,\xi,I)-f_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)|dxd\xi dI+\|f_{\alpha}^{\sharp}(t)-f_{\alpha}^{\sharp}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}\,.

The fact
f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)\in C([0,+\infty);L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}))
indicates that
\|f_{\alpha}^{\sharp}(t)-f_{\alpha}^{\sharp}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}\to 0
as
\tau\to t
. It is further derived from the absolute continuity of integral shows that

\lim\limits_{\tau\rightarrow t}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|f_{\alpha}(t,x+(\tau-t)\xi,\xi,I)-f_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)|dxd\xi dI=0.

Consequently,
f_{\alpha}(t)\in C([0,+\infty);L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}))
.

At the end, we prove the bound (
5.6
). For any
R>0
and
t\in(0,T)
, one easily obtains

\varphi_{R}\triangleq(1+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+I)\mathbf{1}_{|x|\leq R}\mathbf{1}_{|\xi|\leq R}\mathbf{1}_{0\leq I\leq R}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}).

Recall that
f^{n}_{\alpha}(t)
weakly converges to
f_{\alpha}(t)
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
for all
t\in[0,T]
. It is easily derived from Lemma
3.11
that for some
C_{T}>0
independent of
R
,

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)\varphi_{R}dxd\xi dI=

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)\varphi_{R}dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha}(1+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+I)dxd\xi dI\leq C_{T}\,.

By taking
R\to+\infty
, we obtain that for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
,

(5.7)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f_{\alpha}(1+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+I)dxd\xi dI\leq C_{T}.

By analogous arguments of (
5.7
), one knows that for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
,

(5.8)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{\alpha}(1+|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2})dxd\xi\leq C_{T}.

Moreover, Lemma
2.6
implies that for
t\in[0,T]
,

(5.9)

H(f)\leq\liminf\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}H(f^{n})\leq C_{T}^{\prime}.

Furthermore, as the similar arguments in (
3.37
), one sees that for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
,

(5.10)

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f_{\alpha}|\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f_{\alpha})|dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq 2\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f_{\alpha}(|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}+I)dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle+\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f_{\alpha}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f_{\alpha})dxd\xi dI+C_{1},

and for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
,

(5.11)

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{\alpha}|\log f_{\alpha}|dxd\xi\leq 2\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{\alpha}(x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2})dxd\xi+\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{\alpha}\log f_{\alpha}dxd\xi+C_{1}.

As a consequence, the bounds (
5.7
), (
5.9
), (
5.10
) and (
5.11
) conclude the bound (
5.6
).

Remark that the cases
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
can be proved by the similar arguments above. Therefore, the proof of Lemma
5.7
is finished.
∎

5.3.
Convergence for nonlinear collision operator

In this subsection, we aim at justifying the convergence of the nonlinear collision operator
\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}
. We first consider the operator
\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})
, which is the main ingredients of the lose term
\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}^{-}(f^{n},f^{n})
, (see (
3.16
)). Actually, based on
L^{1}
-weak convergence
f^{n}_{\alpha}
to
f^{\alpha}
, we will study the convergent relations between
\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})
and
L_{\alpha}(f)
defined in (
1.37
).

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
T,R>0
fixed.

(1)

For
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
, one has

(5.12)

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})=L_{\alpha}(f)\quad\text{strongly in }L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R});

(2)

For
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, one has

(5.13)

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})=L_{\alpha}(f)\quad\text{strongly in }L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)).

Proof.

Let
E=B_{R},\ F=B_{R}\times(0,R)
and
r>0
fixed. Consider the variables
t\in(0,T)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
\xi\in B_{R}
,
I\in(0,R)
,
\xi_{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
and
I_{*}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Denote by

\displaystyle\varphi^{n}_{1}(t,x,\xi_{*},\xi)

\displaystyle=A_{0\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*})\mathbf{1}_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r}\in L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3};L^{1}(E)),

\displaystyle\varphi^{n}_{2}(t,x,\xi_{*},I_{*},\xi)

\displaystyle=A_{1\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I_{*})\mathbf{1}_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r}\mathbf{1}_{0\leq I_{*}\leq r}\in L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+};L^{1}(E)),

\displaystyle\varphi^{n}_{3}(t,x,\xi_{*},\xi,I)

\displaystyle=A_{1\beta\alpha,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I)\mathbf{1}_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r}\in L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3};L^{1}(F)),

\displaystyle\varphi^{n}_{4}(t,x,\xi_{*},I_{*},\xi,I)

\displaystyle=A_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*})\mathbf{1}_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r}\mathbf{1}_{0\leq I_{*}\leq r}\in L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+};L^{1}(F))\,.

Similarly, let

\displaystyle\varphi_{1}(t,x,\xi_{*},\xi)=A_{0\alpha\beta}(\xi-\xi_{*})\mathbf{1}_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r},\quad\quad\ \ \,\varphi_{2}(t,x,\xi_{*},I_{*},\xi)=A_{1\alpha\beta}(\xi-\xi_{*},I_{*})\mathbf{1}_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r}\mathbf{1}_{0\leq I_{*}\leq r},

\displaystyle\varphi_{3}(t,x,\xi_{*},\xi,I)=A_{1\beta\alpha}(\xi-\xi_{*},I)\mathbf{1}_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r},\quad\varphi_{4}(t,x,\xi_{*},I_{*},\xi,I)=A_{2\alpha\beta}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*})\mathbf{1}_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r}\mathbf{1}_{0\leq I_{*}\leq r}.

Together with Lemmas
3.4
-
3.5
-
3.6
, one obtains

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|\varphi^{n}_{1}-\varphi_{1}\|_{L^{1}(B_{R})}=0,\quad a.e.\ (t,x,\xi_{*})\in(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3},

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|\varphi^{n}_{2}-\varphi_{2}\|_{L^{1}(B_{R})}=0,\quad a.e.\ (t,x,\xi_{*},I_{*})\in(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+},

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|\varphi^{n}_{3}-\varphi_{3}\|_{L^{1}(B_{R}\times(0,R))}=0,\quad a.e.\ (t,x,\xi_{*})\in(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3},

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|\varphi^{n}_{4}-\varphi_{4}\|_{L^{1}(B_{R}\times(0,R))}=0,\quad a.e.\ (t,x,\xi_{*},I_{*})\in(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}.

Then Lemma
5.4
shows that

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r}g^{n}_{\delta\beta_{*}}A_{0\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*})d\xi_{*}

\displaystyle=\int_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r}\tilde{g}_{\delta\beta_{*}}A_{0\alpha\beta}(\xi-\xi_{*})d\xi_{*},

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r}\int_{0\leq I_{*}\leq r}g^{n}_{\delta\beta_{*}}A_{1\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I_{*})d\xi_{*}dI_{*}

\displaystyle=\int_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r}\int_{0\leq I_{*}\leq r}\tilde{g}_{\delta\beta_{*}}A_{1\alpha\beta}(\xi-\xi_{*},I_{*})d\xi_{*}dI_{*}

strongly in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R})
. Moreover, Lemma
5.3
indicates that

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}

\displaystyle\int_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r}g^{n}_{\delta\beta_{*}}A_{1\beta\alpha,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I)d\xi_{*}=\int_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r}\tilde{g}_{\delta\beta_{*}}A_{1\beta\alpha}(\xi-\xi_{*},I)d\xi_{*},

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}

\displaystyle\int_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r}\int_{0\leq I_{*}\leq r}g^{n}_{\delta\beta_{*}}A_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*})d\xi_{*}dI_{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r}\int_{0\leq I_{*}\leq r}\tilde{g}_{\delta\beta_{*}}A_{2\alpha\beta}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*})d\xi_{*}dI_{*}

strongly in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))
.

Recall that
0<g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}<f^{n}_{\alpha}
for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
. For the cases
\alpha,\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, following the similar arguments in (
4.3
), one knows that for all
\epsilon>0
there exists
C_{\epsilon}>0
such that

\displaystyle\|\int_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r}\int_{0\leq I_{*}\leq r}(f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}-g^{n}_{\delta\beta_{*}})A_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*})d\xi_{*}dI_{*}\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(f^{n}_{\beta*}-g^{n}_{\delta\beta*})(1+|\xi_{*}|^{2}+I_{*})dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C_{\epsilon}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|f^{n}_{\beta*}-g^{n}_{\delta\beta*}|dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}.

By Lemma
5.5
, one knows that as
\delta\to 0^{+}
,

\displaystyle C_{\epsilon}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|f^{n}_{\beta*}-g^{n}_{\delta\beta*}|dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}\leq C_{\epsilon}T\sup_{n\geq 1}\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|f^{n}_{\beta*}-g^{n}_{\delta\beta*}|dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}\to 0\,.

Moreover, by Lemma
3.11
,

\displaystyle\epsilon\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(f^{n}_{\beta*}-g^{n}_{\delta\beta*})(1+|\xi_{*}|^{2}+I_{*})dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\epsilon T\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta*}(1+|\xi_{*}|^{2}+I_{*})dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}\leq C_{T}\epsilon\to 0\ (\epsilon\to 0)\,.

Consequently, as
\delta\to 0^{+}
,

\displaystyle\|\int_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r}\int_{0\leq I_{*}\leq r}(f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}-g^{n}_{\delta\beta_{*}})A_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*})d\xi_{*}dI_{*}\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}\to 0\,.

Similarly, as
\delta\to 0^{+}
,

\|\int_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r}\int_{0\leq I_{*}\leq r}(f_{\beta_{*}}-\tilde{g}_{\delta\beta_{*}})A_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*})d\xi_{*}dI_{*}\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}\rightarrow 0.

Therefore, for the cases
\alpha,\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, as
n\rightarrow+\infty
and
\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}
,

\displaystyle\|\int_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r}\int_{0\leq I_{*}\leq r}(f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}-f_{\beta_{*}})A_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*})d\xi_{*}dI_{*}\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\int_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r}\int_{0\leq I_{*}\leq r}(g^{n}_{\delta\beta_{*}}-\tilde{g}_{\delta\beta_{*}})A_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*})d\xi_{*}dI_{*}\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\|\int_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r}\int_{0\leq I_{*}\leq r}(f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}-g^{n}_{\delta\beta_{*}})A_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*})d\xi_{*}dI_{*}\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}

(5.14)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\|\int_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r}\int_{0\leq I_{*}\leq r}(f_{\beta_{*}}-\tilde{g}_{\delta\beta_{*}})A_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*})d\xi_{*}dI_{*}\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}\rightarrow 0.

On the other hand, following the similar arguments in (
4.3
) and combining with the uniform bounds in Lemma
3.11
, one knows that for any
\epsilon>0
there is a
C_{\epsilon}>0
such that

(5.15)

\displaystyle\|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta*}A_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*})(\mathbf{1}_{|\xi_{*}|>r}+\mathbf{1}_{I_{*}>r})d\xi_{*}dI_{*}\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C_{\epsilon}}{r^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta*}|\xi_{*}|^{2}dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}+\tfrac{C_{\epsilon}}{r}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta*}\cdot I_{*}dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\epsilon\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta*}(1+|\xi_{*}|^{2}+I_{*})d\xi_{*}dI_{*}\rightarrow 0\quad(r\rightarrow\infty,\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}).

Similarly, for
\alpha,\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
,

(5.16)

\lim_{r\to+\infty}\|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f_{\beta*}(A_{2\alpha\beta}+A_{2\alpha\beta,n})(\mathbf{1}_{|\xi_{*}|>r}+\mathbf{1}_{I_{*}>r})d\xi_{*}dI_{*}\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}=0.

Therefore, the convergence (
5.3
)-(
5.15
)-(
5.16
) imply that as
n\to+\infty
and
r\to+\infty
,

(5.17)

\displaystyle\|L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})-L_{\alpha\beta}(f)\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\int_{|\xi_{*}|\leq r}\int_{0\leq I_{*}\leq r}(f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}-f_{\beta_{*}})A_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*})d\xi_{*}dI_{*}\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}A_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\mathbf{1}_{|I_{*}|>r}+\mathbf{1}_{I_{*}>r})d\xi_{*}dI_{*}\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f_{\beta_{*}}(A_{2\alpha\beta}+A_{2\alpha\beta,n})(\mathbf{1}_{|I_{*}|>r}+\mathbf{1}_{I_{*}>r})d\xi_{*}dI_{*}\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}\rightarrow 0

for
\alpha,\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
.

Similarly, for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
,
\beta\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
,

(5.18)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to+\infty}L_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})=L_{\alpha\beta}(f)\quad\textrm{strongly in }\ L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))\,.

Then, by (
5.17
)-(
5.18
), we have

(5.19)

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}L_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})=L_{\alpha}(f)\quad\text{strongly in }\ L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))

for all
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, which means that
\sup_{n\geq 1}\|L_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}\leq C
.

Recalling the definition of
N_{n}(f^{n})
in (
3.17
), we know that
N_{n}(f^{n})^{-1}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
and converges to
1
almost everywhere. Together with (
5.19
),

(5.20)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})-L_{\alpha}(f)\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})-L_{\alpha}(f)\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}+\|\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})-{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}.

Then, by applying Egorov’s theorem, for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists a measurable subset
A\subset(0,T)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)
such that
N_{n}(f^{n})^{-1}
converges uniformly to
1
on
A
, and
\mu\left(((0,T)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))\setminus A\right)<\epsilon
. Thus,

(5.21)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\|\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})-{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}=0.

Equation (
5.19
) implies that
L_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})
is weakly compact in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))
. Then, as
\epsilon\to 0^{+}
and
R\to\infty
,

(5.22)

\|L_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)\setminus A)}+\|L_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times(B_{R})^{c}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}\to 0.

Substituting (
5.19
), (
5.21
), and (
5.22
) into (
5.20
), we obtain that the strong convergence holds.
Similarly, for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
, we have

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})=L_{\alpha}(f)\quad\text{in}\ L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}).

As a result, the proof of Lemma
5.8
is finished.
∎

Next we investigate the convergence of
\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}
.

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
R>0
and
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
. Suppose
\varphi(t,x,\xi,I)\in L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
with
supp\varphi\subseteq(0,T)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)
. Then for all
\epsilon>0
there exists a Borel subset
E\subset(0,T)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)
such that
|E^{c}|=|(0,T)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)\setminus E|<\epsilon
and

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})\varphi\mathbf{1}_{E}d\xi dI=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}Q^{\pm}_{\alpha}(f,f)\varphi\mathbf{1}_{E}d\xi dI\quad\text{strongly in }\ L^{1}((0,T)\times B_{R}).

Proof.

By Egorov’s theorem, for all
\epsilon>0
, there exists a Borel subset
E_{0}\subset(0,T)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)
such that
|E_{0}^{c}|=|(0,T)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)\setminus E_{0}|<\epsilon
and
\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})
uniformly converges to
L_{\alpha}(f)
on
E_{0}
. Besides, it is derived from Lusin theorem that there exists a closed subset
B\subset(0,T)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)
such that
|B^{c}|=|(0,T)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)\setminus B|<\epsilon
and
L_{\alpha}(f)
is bounded on
B
. Setting
E=E_{0}\cap B
and utilizing uniform convergence, we obtain
\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})
is uniformly bounded on
E
. Together with the uniform bounds in Lemma
3.11
and Lemma
5.6
, one has

(5.23)

\displaystyle\|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(\tilde{Q}^{-}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})-Q^{-}_{\alpha}(f,f))\varphi\mathbf{1}_{E}d\xi dI\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times B_{R})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\sup\limits_{E}|\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})-L_{\alpha}(f)|\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times R_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha}dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(f^{n}_{\alpha}-f_{\alpha})L_{\alpha}(f)\varphi\mathbf{1}_{E}d\xi dI\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times B_{R})}\rightarrow 0,

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Therefore, the situation of
\tilde{Q}^{-}_{\alpha,n}
is established.

Denote by
f\wedge g=\min\{f,g\}
. Let

\displaystyle B_{\alpha\beta,n;m}=B_{\alpha\beta,n}\wedge m\mathbf{1}_{|z|\leq m}\mathbf{1}_{0\leq\eta\leq m}\,,\ B^{m}_{\alpha\beta,n}=B_{\alpha\beta,n}(\mathbf{1}_{B_{\alpha\beta,n}>m}+\mathbf{1}_{|z|>m}+\mathbf{1}_{\eta>m})\,.

Write

\displaystyle A_{1\beta\alpha,n;m}(z,\eta)

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,1]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{1\beta\alpha,n;m}\mathfrak{R}^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}d\mathfrak{R}d\omega,

\displaystyle A^{m}_{1\beta\alpha,n}(z,\eta)

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,1]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B^{m}_{1\beta\alpha,n}\mathfrak{R}^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}d\mathfrak{R}d\omega,

\displaystyle A_{2\alpha\beta,n;m}(z,\eta)

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{2\alpha\beta,n;m}\mathfrak{r}^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{r})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{R})^{(\delta(\alpha)+\delta(\beta)/2-1)}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{R}d\omega,

\displaystyle A^{m}_{2\alpha\beta,n}(z,\eta)

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B^{m}_{2\alpha\beta,n}\mathfrak{r}^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{r})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{R})^{(\delta(\alpha)+\delta(\beta)/2-1)}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega.

Denote by
\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n;m}(f,f)
and
\tilde{Q}^{m,\pm}_{\alpha,n}(f,f)
the corresponding normalized collision operators. Similarly, let
B_{\alpha\beta;m}=B_{\alpha\beta}\wedge m\mathbf{1}_{|z|\leq m}\mathbf{1}_{0\leq\eta\leq m}
,
B^{m}_{\alpha\beta}=B_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{1}_{B_{\alpha\beta}>m}+\mathbf{1}_{|z|>m}+\mathbf{1}_{\eta>m})
. Similarly we obtain the forms for
A_{\alpha\beta;m},A^{m}_{\alpha\beta},\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha;m}(f,f)
and
\tilde{Q}^{m,\pm}_{\alpha}(f,f)
. Then by the Arkeryd-type inequality (
4.1
),

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}|(\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n;m}(f^{n},f^{n})-\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n}))||\varphi|\mathbf{1}_{E}dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}\tilde{Q}^{m,+}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})|\varphi|\mathbf{1}_{E}dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{\log K}+K\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}|\varphi|\mathbf{1}_{E}f^{n}_{\alpha}\tilde{L}^{m}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})dxd\xi dI,

where

\displaystyle\tilde{L}^{m}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})

\displaystyle=N_{n}(f^{n})^{-1}\sum\limits_{\beta=1}^{s_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}_{\beta*}A^{m}_{1\beta\alpha,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I)d\xi_{*}

\displaystyle+N_{n}(f^{n})^{-1}\sum\limits_{\beta=s_{0}+1}^{s}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\beta*}A^{m}_{2\alpha\beta,n}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*})d\xi_{*}dI_{*}.

Notice that
0\leq A_{\alpha\beta,n;m}\leq A_{\alpha\beta,n}
and
\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})
is uniformly bounded on
E
. It is then easy to see that
\tilde{L}^{m}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})\mathbf{1}_{E}\leq\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})\mathbf{1}_{E}
and
A^{m}_{\alpha\beta,n}\downarrow 0
as
m\uparrow+\infty
. Therefore, by the Lebesgue Control Convergence Theorem, we can prove for any fixed
n\geq 1
,

(5.24)

\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}|\varphi|\mathbf{1}_{E}f^{n}_{\alpha}\tilde{L}^{m}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})dxd\xi dI\downarrow 0\ \text{as}\ m\uparrow+\infty.

Similarly as in (
5.24
), it infers that

(5.25)

\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}|\varphi|\mathbf{1}_{E}f_{\alpha}L^{m}_{\alpha}(f)dxd\xi dI\downarrow 0\ \text{as}\ m\uparrow+\infty,

where

L^{m}_{\alpha}(f)=\sum\limits_{\beta=1}^{s_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{\beta*}A^{m}_{1\beta\alpha}(\xi-\xi_{*},I)d\xi_{*}+\sum\limits_{\beta=s_{0}+1}^{s}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f_{\beta_{*}}A^{m}_{2\alpha\beta}(\xi-\xi_{*},I+I_{*})d\xi_{*}dI_{*}.

Following the same arguments in (
5.23
), we get that for any fixed
m\geq 1
and
n\rightarrow+\infty
,

(5.26)

\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}\varphi\mathbf{1}_{E}f^{n}_{\alpha}\tilde{L}^{m}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})dxd\xi dI\rightarrow\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}\varphi\mathbf{1}_{E}f_{\alpha}L^{m}_{\alpha}(f)dxd\xi dI.

Combining with the relations (
5.24
)-(
5.26
), it can be concluded that

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{m\rightarrow\infty}\sup\limits_{n}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}\varphi\mathbf{1}_{E}f^{n}_{\alpha}\tilde{L}^{m}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})dxd\xi dI=0.

Therefore, by taking
m\to+\infty
and
K\to+\infty
,

(5.27)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{m\rightarrow+\infty}\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}dxd\xi dI|(\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n;m}(f^{n},f^{n})-\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n}))||\varphi|\mathbf{1}_{E}=0.

Similarly, one can also derives that

(5.28)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{m\rightarrow\infty}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}dxd\xi dI|Q^{+}_{\alpha;m}(f,f)-Q^{+}_{\alpha}(f,f)||\varphi|\mathbf{1}_{E}=0.

Moreover, (
3.18
) and (
5.23
) show that for any fixed
m\geq 1
,

(5.29)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n;m}(f^{n},f^{n})-Q^{+}_{\alpha;m}(f,f))\varphi\mathbf{1}_{E}d\xi dI\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times B_{R})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(\tilde{Q}^{-}_{\alpha,n;m}(f^{n},f^{n})-Q^{-}_{\alpha;m}(f,f))\varphi^{\prime}\mathbf{1}_{E}d\xi dI\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times B_{R})}=0,

where
\varphi^{\prime}=\varphi(t,x,\xi^{\prime},I^{\prime})
. Together with (
5.27
), (
5.28
) and (
5.29
), one then gains

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})\varphi\mathbf{1}_{E}d\xi dI=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}Q^{+}_{\alpha}(f,f)\varphi\mathbf{1}_{E}d\xi dI\quad\text{strongly in }\ L^{1}((0,T)\times B_{R}).

Therefore, the proof of Lemma
5.9
is finished.
∎

By employing the similar arguments in Lemma
5.9
above, we can establish the following corollary.

Corollary 5.2
.

Let
R>0
and
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
. Let
\varphi(t,x,\xi)\in L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
with
supp\varphi\subset(0,T)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}
. Then for all
\epsilon>0
there exists a Borel subset
E\subset(0,T)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}
such that
|E^{c}|=|(0,T)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}\setminus E|<\epsilon
and

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})\varphi\mathbf{1}_{E}d\xi=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}Q^{\pm}_{\alpha}(f,f)\varphi\mathbf{1}_{E}d\xi\quad\text{strongly in}\ L^{1}((0,T)\times B_{R}).

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
T,R>0
and
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
. For given
\delta>0
, let
\beta_{\delta}(t)=t\wedge\frac{1}{\delta}=\min\{t,\frac{1}{\delta}\}
or
\beta_{\delta}(t)=\frac{t}{1+\delta t}
. Then

\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(\beta_{\delta}(f^{n}),\beta_{\delta}(f^{n}))\to Q^{\pm}_{\alpha}(\tilde{\beta}_{\delta},\tilde{\beta}_{\delta})\quad\text{weakly in }\ L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)).

where
\tilde{\beta}_{\delta}(t,x,\xi,I)
is the weak
L^{1}
limit of
\beta_{\delta}(f^{n})(t,x,\xi,I)
.

Proof.

It is not hard to verify that
\beta_{\delta}(t)
is bounded, Lipschitz on
[0,+\infty)
,
\beta^{\prime}(t)\leq\frac{C}{1+t}
for some
C>0
and

(5.30)

0\leq t-\beta_{\delta}(t)\leq\mathbf{1}_{t\geq\frac{1}{\delta}}t.

Recall that
f^{n}_{\alpha}
is relatively weakly compact in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
, so is
\beta(f^{n}_{\alpha})
. Moreover,

0\leq\beta^{\prime}_{\delta}(f^{n}_{\alpha})\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}^{\pm}(f^{n},f^{n})\leq\tfrac{C_{\delta}}{1+f^{n}_{\alpha}}\tilde{Q}_{\alpha,n}^{\pm}(f^{n},f^{n}).

Then Lemma
4.1
shows that
\beta^{\prime}_{\delta}(f^{n}_{\alpha})\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})
is relatively weakly compact in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))
. To figure out that the conclusions of
g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}
in Subsection
5.2
are all valid for
\beta_{\delta}(f^{n}_{\alpha})
, such as (
5.2
) and Lemma
5.5
. Moreover, the similar processes of Lemma
5.8
indicate that

(5.31)

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(\beta_{\delta}(f^{n}))=L_{\alpha}(\tilde{\beta}_{\delta})\quad\text{strongly in }\ L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)).

Furthermore, from the similar proof processes of Lemma
5.9
, we can derive that for all
\varphi(t,x,\xi,I)\in L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))
there exists a Borel subset
E\subset(0,T)\times B_{M}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)
(where
M>R
) such that
|E^{c}|<\epsilon
and

(5.32)

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(\beta_{\delta}(f^{n}),\beta_{\delta}(f^{n}))-Q^{\pm}_{\alpha}(\tilde{\beta}_{\delta},\tilde{\beta}_{\delta}))\varphi\mathbf{1}_{E}d\xi dI\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times B_{R})}=0.

It is also easy to see that by the relatively compactness of
\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(\beta_{\delta}(f^{n}))
in (
5.31
) and the relation (
3.18
),

(5.33)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{B_{M}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(\beta_{\delta}(f^{n}),\beta_{\delta}(f^{n}))\varphi\mathbf{1}_{E^{c}}dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{B_{M}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}\beta_{\delta}(f^{n}_{\alpha})\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(\beta_{\delta}(f^{n}))\varphi^{\prime}1_{E^{c}}dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{T}\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\sup_{n\geq 1}\int_{E^{c}}\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(\beta_{\delta}(f^{n}))dtdxd\xi dI\rightarrow 0

as
\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}.
Similarly, we can obtain the following conclusions:

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}\sup\limits_{n}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{B_{M}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}\tilde{Q}^{-}_{\alpha,n}(\beta_{\delta}(f^{n}),\beta_{\delta}(f^{n}))\mathbf{1}_{E^{c}}dxd\xi dI=0,

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{M\rightarrow\infty}\sup\limits_{n}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\int_{|x|>M}dx\iint_{B_{R}\times(0,R)}\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(\beta_{\delta}(f^{n}),\beta_{\delta}(f^{n}))d\xi dI=0,

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{B_{M}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}Q^{\pm}_{\alpha}(\tilde{\beta}_{\delta},\tilde{\beta}_{\delta})\mathbf{1}_{E^{c}}dxd\xi dI=0,

(5.34)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{M\rightarrow\infty}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\int_{|x|>M}dx\iint_{B_{R}\times(0,R)}Q^{\pm}_{\alpha}(\tilde{\beta}_{\delta},\tilde{\beta}_{\delta})d\xi dI=0.

Combining with the relations (
5.32
)-(
5.3
), it can be concluded that

(5.35)

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}(\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(\beta_{\delta}(f^{n}),\beta_{\delta}(f^{n}))-Q^{\pm}_{\alpha}(\tilde{\beta}_{\delta},\tilde{\beta}_{\delta}))\varphi dxd\xi dI=0.

Therefore, the proof of Lemma
5.10
is finished.
∎

By applying the analogous arguments in Lemma
5.10
, one gains the following corollary.

Corollary 5.3
.

Let
T,R>0
and for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
. For given
\delta>0
, let
\beta_{\delta}(t)=t\wedge\frac{1}{\delta}
or
\beta_{\delta}(t)=\frac{t}{1+\delta t}
. Then

\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(\beta_{\delta}(f^{n}),\beta_{\delta}(f^{n}))\to Q^{\pm}_{\alpha}(\tilde{\beta}_{\delta},\tilde{\beta}_{\delta})\quad\text{weakly in}\ L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R})

where
\tilde{\beta}_{\delta}(t,x,\xi)
is the weak
L^{1}
limit of
\beta_{\delta}(f^{n})(t,x,\xi)
.

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
T,\delta>0
,
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
and
\varphi(t,x,\xi,I)\in L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
. Then

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tfrac{\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})}{1+\delta\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})}\varphi d\xi dI=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tfrac{Q^{\pm}_{\alpha}(f,f)}{1+\delta L_{\alpha}(f)}\varphi d\xi dI\quad\text{strongly in }L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}).

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\delta=1
. We derive from the Arkeryd inequality (
4.1
) and
(1+\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n}))^{-1}\tilde{Q}^{-}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})\leq f^{n}_{\alpha}
that
(1+\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n}))^{-1}\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})
is relatively weakly compact in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
. Let
R>0
be arbitrary. For any
\epsilon>0
, we take the set
E\subseteq(0,T)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)
given in Lemma
5.9
. Then
\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})\rightrightarrows L_{\alpha}(f)
on
E
and
(1+\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n}))^{-1}\leq 1
,
(1+{L}_{\alpha}(f))^{-1}\leq 1
. Moreover, Lemma
5.9
also indicates that

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})\varphi\mathbf{1}_{E}d\xi dI=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}Q^{\pm}_{\alpha}(f,f)\varphi\mathbf{1}_{E}d\xi dI\quad\text{strongly in }\ L^{1}((0,T)\times B_{R}).

Then we conclude

(5.36)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tfrac{\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})}{1+\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})}\varphi\mathbf{1}_{E}d\xi dI=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tfrac{Q^{\pm}_{\alpha}(f,f)}{1+L_{\alpha}(f)}\varphi\mathbf{1}_{E}d\xi dI\hskip 9.24994pt\text{strongly in }L^{1}((0,T)\times B_{R}).

On the other hand, the relatively weak tightness of
(1+\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n}))^{-1}\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})
in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
guarantees that

(5.37)

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}\|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tfrac{\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})}{1+\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})}\varphi\mathbf{1}_{E^{c}}d\xi dI\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times B_{R})}\rightarrow 0\ (\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+})

and

(5.38)

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}[\tfrac{\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})}{1+\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})}-\tfrac{Q^{\pm}_{\alpha}(f,f)}{1+L_{\alpha}(f)}]\varphi d\xi dI\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times(B_{R})^{c})}\to 0\ (R\to+\infty)\,.

Then the convergences (
5.36
), (
5.37
) and (
5.38
) imply that

(5.39)

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tfrac{\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})}{1+\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})}\varphi d\xi dI=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tfrac{Q^{\pm}_{\alpha}(f,f)}{1+L_{\alpha}(f)}\varphi d\xi dI\quad\text{strongly in }L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}).

The proof of Lemma
5.11
is consequently finished.
∎

Corollary 5.4
.

Let
T,\delta>0
,
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
and
\varphi(t,x,\xi)\in L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Then

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tfrac{\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})}{1+\delta\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})}\varphi d\xi=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tfrac{Q^{\pm}_{\alpha}(f,f)}{1+\delta L_{\alpha}(f)}\varphi d\xi\quad\text{strongly in }L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}).

At the end, we study the integrability of the limit of nonlinear collision operator.

Lemma 5.12
.

For any given
T,R>0
and all
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
,

\displaystyle\tfrac{Q^{-}_{\alpha}(f,f)}{1+f_{\alpha}}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+};L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)),\ \tfrac{Q^{+}_{\alpha}(f,f)}{1+f_{\alpha}}\in L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)).

Proof.

Observe that
(1+f_{\alpha})^{-1}Q^{-}_{\alpha}(f,f)\leq L_{\alpha}(f)
. By employing the same arguments in (
4.3
), one knows that for any
\epsilon>0
there is a
C_{\epsilon}>0
such that

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}L_{\alpha}(f)(t,x,\xi,I)dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon\sum_{\beta=1}^{s_{0}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{\beta}(t,x,\xi_{*})(1+|\xi_{*}|^{2})dxd\xi_{*}+\epsilon\sum_{\beta=s_{0}+1}^{s}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f_{\beta}(t,x,\xi_{*},I_{*})(1+|\xi_{*}|^{2}+I_{*})dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}

\displaystyle+C_{\epsilon}\sum_{\beta=1}^{s_{0}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}f_{\beta}(t,x,\xi_{*})dxd\xi_{*}+C_{\epsilon}\sum_{\beta=s_{0}+1}^{s}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f_{\beta}(t,x,\xi_{*},I_{*})dxd\xi_{*}dI_{*}\leq C_{T,\epsilon}

for all
t\geq 0
. Here Lemma
5.7
is utilized. By taking
\epsilon=1
, we then obtain

\tfrac{Q^{-}_{\alpha}(f,f)}{1+f_{\alpha}}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+};L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)).

Next we study the gain term
Q_{\alpha}^{+}(f,f)
. For any
\delta>0
, it infers from the Arkeryd-type inequality (
4.1
) that for all
K>1

(5.40)

\tfrac{\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})}{1+\delta\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})}\leq K\tfrac{\tilde{Q}^{\mp}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})}{1+\delta\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})}+\tfrac{\tilde{e}^{n}_{\alpha}}{\log K}\,.

By (
3.36
) in Lemma
3.11
, one sees that
\tilde{e}^{n}_{\alpha}
is a nonnegative bounded sequence in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
. Then there exists a nonnegative finite measure
\mu_{\alpha}
such that

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{e}^{n}_{\alpha}=\mu_{\alpha}\quad\text{in }\ \mathscr{D}^{\prime}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}).

Moreover, Lemma
5.11
indicates that

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\tfrac{\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})}{1+\delta\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})}=\tfrac{Q^{\pm}_{\alpha}(f,f)}{1+\delta L_{\alpha}(f)}\quad\text{in }\ \mathscr{D}^{\prime}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}).

Therefore, in the sense of distribution,

(5.41)

\tfrac{Q^{\pm}_{\alpha}(f,f)}{1+\delta L_{\alpha}(f)}\leq K\tfrac{Q^{\mp}_{\alpha}(f,f)}{1+\delta L_{\alpha}(f)}+\tfrac{\mu_{\alpha}}{\log K}.

for
K>1
.

By the Lebesgue Decomposition Theorem, the measure
\mu_{\alpha}
can be decomposed as
\mu_{\alpha}=\mu_{\alpha}^{1}+\mu_{\alpha}^{2}
, where
\mu_{\alpha}^{1}\ll dtdxd\xi dI
(hence,
\mu_{\alpha}^{1}
is absolutely continuous corresponding to the measure
dtdxd\xi dI
), and
\mu_{\alpha}^{2}\perp dtdxd\xi dI
(hence,
\mu_{\alpha}^{2}
is singular with respect to the measure
dtdxd\xi dI
). Furthermore, the Radon-Nikodym Theorem implies that there is a nonnegative function
e_{\alpha}^{*}(t,x,\xi,I)\in L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
such that
\mu_{\alpha}^{1}=e_{\alpha}^{*}(t,x,\xi,I)dtdxd\xi dI
.
\mu_{\alpha}^{2}\perp dtdxd\xi dI
means that the concentrated set
A
with respect to the measure
\mu_{\alpha}^{2}
is actually a zero-measured set corresponding to the natural measure
dtdxd\xi dI
. Then the bound (
5.41
) indicates that for
K>1
,

\displaystyle\tfrac{Q^{\pm}_{\alpha}(f,f)}{1+\delta L_{\alpha}(f)}(t,x,\xi,I)\leq K\tfrac{Q^{\mp}_{\alpha}(f,f)}{1+\delta L_{\alpha}(f)}(t,x,\xi,I)+\tfrac{e_{\alpha}^{*}(t,x,\xi,I)}{\log K}\hskip 9.24994pta.e.\ (t,x,\xi,I)\in(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\,.

By letting
\delta\to 0^{+}
, one has

(5.42)
