Q^{\pm}_{\alpha}(f,f)\leq KQ^{\mp}_{\alpha}(f,f)+(\log K)^{-1}e_{\alpha}^{*}(t,x,\xi,I),

which means that

\tfrac{Q^{+}_{\alpha}(f,f)}{1+f_{\alpha}}(t,x,\xi,I)\leq K\tfrac{Q^{-}_{\alpha}(f,f)}{1+f_{\alpha}}(t,x,\xi,I)+\tfrac{e_{\alpha}^{*}(t,x,\xi,I)}{\log K}.

Due to
\tfrac{Q^{-}_{\alpha}(f,f)}{1+f_{\alpha}}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+};L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))
and
e_{\alpha}^{*}(t,x,\xi,I)\in L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
, one gains that
(1+f_{\alpha})^{-1}Q^{+}_{\alpha}(f,f)\in L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))
. The proof of Lemma
5.12
is therefore finished.
∎

By the analogous arguments in Lemma
5.12
, one infers the following corollary.

Corollary 5.5
.

For any given
T,R>0
and all
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
,

\displaystyle\tfrac{Q^{-}_{\alpha}(f,f)}{1+f_{\alpha}}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+};L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}),\ \tfrac{Q^{+}_{\alpha}(f,f)}{1+f_{\alpha}}\in L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}).

6.
Existence of renormalized solution to (
1.1
)

In this section, the main goal is to prove that the limit function
f=(f_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}}
constructed in previous section is exactly the renormalized solution to (
1.1
). By Lemma
2.9
, it is equivalent to show that
f
is a mild solution of (
1.1
) and
(1+f_{\alpha})^{-1}Q^{\pm}_{\alpha}(f,f)\in L^{1}_{loc}
. Note that Lemma
5.12
and Corollary
5.5
have been shown that
(1+f_{\alpha})^{-1}Q^{\pm}_{\alpha}(f,f)\in L^{1}_{loc}
for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
. We only need to verify that
f
is a mild solution of (
1.1
). By employing Lemma
2.10
and Lemma
5.8
, it is equivalent to prove that the components
f_{\alpha}
of
f
satisfy the equality (
2.8
) and (
2.9
).

6.1.
Supersolutions of equation (
1.1
)

In this subsection, based on Lemma
2.10
, we consider the solution
f^{n}
of (
3.1
) with positive components
f^{n}_{\alpha}
. After extracting a subsequence we will prove that the weak-
L^{1}
limit
f=(f_{1},\cdots,f_{s})
is a mild supersolution of (
1.1
) in the sense that
f
satisfies the exponential form (
2.8
), (
2.9
) with the equality replaced by an inequality. Namely,

Definition 6.1

(Supersolution)
.

We say
f=(f_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}}
is a supersolution if for all
t\geq 0
there hold:

(1)

for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
, one has

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}Q^{+}_{\alpha}(f,f)^{\sharp}(\tau,x,\xi)\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau,x,\xi)))d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi)-f_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi)\mathrm{exp}(-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi))\quad a.e.\ (x,\xi)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3};

(2)

for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, one has

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}Q^{+}_{\alpha}(f,f)^{\sharp}(\tau,x,\xi,I)\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau,x,\xi,I)))d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)-f_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi,I)\mathrm{exp}(-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I))\quad a.e.\ (x,\xi,I)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}.

Recall that
f^{n}
is the mild solution of the approximated problem (
3.1
). By the proof of Lemma
4.1
, one knows that
\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})\in L^{1}_{loc}
. Then Lemma
2.10
tells us that for all
0\leq t<\infty
and for almost everywhere
(x,\xi)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
with
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
,

(6.1)

\displaystyle f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi)-f^{n}_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi)\mathrm{exp}(-\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t,x,\xi))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})^{\sharp}(s,x,\xi)\mathrm{exp}(-(\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t,x,\xi)-\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(s,x,\xi)))ds,

and for almost everywhere
(x,\xi)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
with
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
,

(6.2)

\displaystyle f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)-f^{n}_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi,I)\mathrm{exp}(-\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t,x,\xi,I))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})^{\sharp}(s,x,\xi,I)\mathrm{exp}(-(\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t,x,\xi,I)-\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(s,x,\xi,I)))ds,

where

\displaystyle\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t,x,\xi)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n}_{\alpha})^{\sharp}(s,x,\xi)ds,\quad\text{for }\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\},

\displaystyle\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t,x,\xi,I)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n}_{\alpha})^{\sharp}(s,x,\xi,I)ds,\quad\text{for }\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}.

Here we employ the notations
g^{\sharp}(t,x,\xi)=g(t,x+t\xi,\xi)
and
g^{\sharp}(t,x,\xi,I)=g(t,x+t\xi,\xi,I)
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
T>0
and
t\in[0,T]
.

(1)

For
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
, one has

(6.3)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}[f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi)-f^{n}_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi)\mathrm{exp}(-\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t,x,\xi))]

\displaystyle=f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi)-f_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi)\mathrm{exp}(-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi))\quad\text{weakly in }L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3});

(2)

For
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, one has

(6.4)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}[f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)-f^{n}_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi,I)\mathrm{exp}(-\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t,x,\xi,I))]

\displaystyle=f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)-f_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi,I)\mathrm{exp}(-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I))\quad\text{weakly in }L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}).

Here
F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}
is defined in (
2.7
).

Proof.

By Lemma
5.8
, one infers that for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
,

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\max\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\|\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R})}\leq\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})-L_{\alpha}(f)\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R})}=0,

and for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
,

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\max\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\|\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}\leq\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})-L_{\alpha}(f)\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}=0.

Therefore,
\mathrm{exp}(-\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t,x,\xi))\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
converges to
\mathrm{exp}(-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi))

a.e.

(x,\xi)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
and
\mathrm{exp}(-\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t,x,\xi,I))\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
converges to
\mathrm{exp}(-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I))

a.e.

(x,\xi,I)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
. Moreover, Lemma
3.1
and Lemma
3.2
show that
f^{n}_{\alpha,0}
strongly converges to
f_{\alpha,0}
in
L^{1}
. Then the Product Limit Theorem (Lemma
2.5
) tells us that
f^{n}_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi,I)\mathrm{exp}(-\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t,x,\xi,I))
weakly converges to
f_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi,I)\mathrm{exp}(-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I))
for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, and
f^{n}_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi)\mathrm{exp}(-\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t,x,\xi))
weakly converges to
f_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi)\mathrm{exp}(-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi))
for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
. Together with Lemma
2.3
, Corollary
2.1
and Lemma
3.11
, the proof of Lemma
6.1
is finished.
∎

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
T,R>0
and
t\in[0,T]
.

(1)

For
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
, one has

(6.5)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(h^{n}_{M},h^{n}_{M})^{\sharp}(\tau)\mathrm{exp}(-(\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t)-\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(\tau)))d\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}Q^{+}_{\alpha}(h_{M},h_{M})^{\sharp}(\tau)\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)))d\tau\quad\text{weakly in }L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R});

(2)

For
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, one has

(6.6)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(h^{n}_{M},h^{n}_{M})^{\sharp}(\tau)\mathrm{exp}(-(\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t)-\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(\tau)))d\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}Q^{+}_{\alpha}(h_{M},h_{M})^{\sharp}(\tau)\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)))d\tau\quad\text{weakly in }L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)),

where
h^{n}_{M}=(h^{n}_{\alpha M})_{\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}}
with
h^{n}_{\alpha M}=f^{n}_{\alpha}\wedge M=\min\{f^{n}_{\alpha},M\}
for
M>0
. Moreover, the components
h_{\alpha M}
of
h_{M}=(h_{\alpha M})_{\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}}
is the
L^{1}
weak limit of
h^{n}_{\alpha M}
.

Proof.

For
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, Lemma
5.10
implies

\tilde{Q}^{\pm}_{\alpha,n}(h^{n}_{M},h^{n}_{M})\to Q^{\pm}_{\alpha}(h_{M},h_{M})\quad\text{weakly in}\ L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)).

It is further implied by Lemma
5.8
that as
n\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle\|(\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t)-\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(\tau))-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau))\|_{L^{1}((0,t)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\int_{\tau}^{t}(\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})^{\sharp}(\sigma)-L_{\alpha}(f)^{\sharp}(\sigma))d\sigma\|_{L^{1}((0,t)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle T\|\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})(\sigma)-L_{\alpha}(f)(\sigma)\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}\rightarrow 0,

which means that
\mathrm{exp}(-(\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t)-\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(\tau)))\in L^{\infty}((0,t)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))
converges to
\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)))

a.e.

(0,t)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)
. Then the Product Limit Theorem (Lemma
2.5
) concludes the convergence (
6.6
). Analogously, the convergence (
6.5
) for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
also holds. The proof of Lemma
6.2
is then finished.
∎

Remark 6.1
.

For all
t\in[0,T]
and as
M\to+\infty
,
(h_{\alpha M}^{n},h_{\alpha M})\to(f^{n}_{\alpha},f_{\alpha})
strongly in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha})
for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
. Indeed, for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, the inequality (
5.30
) and the weak convergence of
f^{n}_{\alpha}
infers that

\lim\limits_{M\rightarrow\infty}\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|h^{n}_{\alpha M}-f^{n}_{\alpha}|dxd\xi dI\leq\lim\limits_{M\rightarrow\infty}\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\iiint_{f^{n}_{\alpha}>M}f^{n}_{\alpha}dxd\xi dI=0.

Moreover, as
M\rightarrow\infty
,

(6.7)

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|h_{\alpha M}-f_{\alpha}|dxd\xi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(h_{\alpha M}-h^{n}_{\alpha M})sgn(h_{\alpha M}-f_{\alpha})dxd\xi dI+\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|h^{n}_{\alpha M}-f^{n}_{\alpha}|dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(f^{n}_{\alpha}-f_{\alpha})sgn(h_{\alpha M}-f_{\alpha})dxd\xi dI\rightarrow 0.

The cases
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
can be derived analogously.

Lemma 6.3
.

f
is a supersolution to (
1.1
) given in Definition
6.1
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we only prove the cases
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
. Note that
h^{n}_{\alpha M}\leq f^{n}_{\alpha}
, which means that
\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(h^{n}_{M},h^{n}_{M})\leq\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})
. Together with (
6.2
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(h^{n}_{M},h^{n}_{M})^{\sharp}(\tau,x,\xi,I)\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t,x,\xi,I)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(\tau,x,\xi,I)))d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)-f^{n}_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi,I)\mathrm{exp}(-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t,x,\xi,I))\quad a.e.\ (x,\xi,I)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}.

By letting
n\to+\infty
in the above inequality, Lemma
6.1
and
6.2
give us

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}Q^{+}_{\alpha}(h_{\alpha M},h_{\alpha M})^{\sharp}(\tau)\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)))d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)-f_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi,I)\mathrm{exp}(-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I))\quad a.e.\ (x,\xi,I)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}.

Since
h_{\alpha M}\uparrow f_{\alpha}
as
M\uparrow+\infty
, the conclusion results from the Levi’s Monotonic Convergence Theorem. Then the proof of Lemma
6.3
is finished.
∎

6.2.
Subsolutions of equation (
1.1
)

In this subsection, we will prove that
f
is a subsolution of (
1.1
) defined as follows.

Definition 6.2

(Subsolution)
.

We say
f=(f_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}}
is a subsolution if for all
t\geq 0
there hold:

(1)

for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
, one has

(6.8)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}Q^{+}_{\alpha}(f,f)^{\sharp}(\tau,x,\xi)\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau,x,\xi)))d\tau

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi)-f_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi)\mathrm{exp}(-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi))\quad a.e.\ (x,\xi)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3};

(2)

for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, one has

(6.9)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}Q^{+}_{\alpha}(f,f)^{\sharp}(\tau,x,\xi,I)\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau,x,\xi,I)))d\tau

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)-f_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi,I)\mathrm{exp}(-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I))\quad a.e.\ (x,\xi,I)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}.

Now we rewrite the approximated renormalized equation (
5.1
) as

\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t}g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}+\xi\cdot\nabla_{x}g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}=\tfrac{\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})}{1+\delta f^{n}_{\alpha}}+\{g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}-\tfrac{f^{n}_{\alpha}}{1+\delta f^{n}_{\alpha}}\}\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})-g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n}).

For
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, by multiplying
g^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}\mathrm{exp}\tilde{F}_{\alpha,n}
and integrating the resultant equation over characteristics, we obtain

(6.10)

\displaystyle g^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(t)-g^{n}_{\delta\alpha,0}\mathrm{exp}(-\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t))=\int_{0}^{t}\{\tfrac{\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})^{\sharp}}{1+\delta f^{n\sharp}_{\alpha}}+j^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})^{\sharp}\}(\tau)\mathrm{exp}(-(\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t)-\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(\tau)))d\tau

for
t\geq 0
and
a.e.\ (x,\xi,I)\in(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
, where
j^{n}_{\delta\alpha}=g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}-\frac{f^{n}_{\alpha}}{1+\delta f^{n}_{\alpha}}
. Similarly, the above equation also holds for the cases
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
.

Obviously,
j^{n}_{\delta\alpha}(t)
is relatively weakly compact in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
for any fixed
t\geq 1
. Assume that
j^{n}_{\delta\alpha},\ j^{n}_{\delta\alpha}\wedge K
weakly converge to
j_{\delta\alpha},\ j^{K}_{\delta\alpha}
, respectively. Since the function
t\wedge K
is concave, one knows
j^{K}_{\delta\alpha}\leq j_{\delta\alpha}\wedge K
. Using the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
5.5
, we obtain

(6.11)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{K\rightarrow+\infty}\sup\limits_{t\in(0,T)}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|j_{\delta\alpha}-j^{K}_{\delta\alpha}|dxd\xi dI=0\quad\text{for }\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}.

Similarly for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
,

(6.12)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{K\rightarrow+\infty}\sup\limits_{t\in(0,T)}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}|j_{\delta\alpha}-j^{K}_{\delta\alpha}|dxd\xi=0.

By Lemma
5.7
and Lemma
6.3
, one derives that for all
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}Q^{+}_{\alpha}(f,f)^{\sharp}\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)))d\tau\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})\quad\text{for }\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\},

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}Q^{+}_{\alpha}(f,f)^{\sharp}\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)))d\tau\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})\quad\text{for }\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}.

Furthermore, using the inequality (
5.42
) and the fact
e_{\alpha}^{*}(t,x,\xi,I)\in L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
, we get

(6.13)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}Q^{-}_{\alpha}(f,f)^{\sharp}\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)))d\tau\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})\hskip 9.24994pt\text{for }\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\},

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}Q^{-}_{\alpha}(f,f)^{\sharp}\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)))d\tau\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})\hskip 9.24994pt\text{for }\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}.

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
R>0
and
t\geq 0
.

(1)

For
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
, one has

(6.14)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{t}j^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(\tau)\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})^{\sharp}(\tau)\mathrm{exp}(-(\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t)-\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(\tau)))d\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}j^{\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(\tau)L_{\alpha}(f)^{\sharp}(\tau)\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)))d\tau\quad\text{weakly in }\ L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R});

(2)

For
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, one has

(6.15)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{t}j^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(\tau)\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})^{\sharp}(\tau)\mathrm{exp}(-(\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t)-\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(\tau)))d\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}j^{\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(\tau)L_{\alpha}(f)^{\sharp}(\tau)\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)))d\tau\quad\text{weakly in }\ L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)).

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we only consider the cases
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
. Note that
\lim_{x\to+\infty}[\frac{1}{\delta}\log(1+\delta x)-\frac{x}{1+\delta x}]/x=0
, which means that
j^{n}_{\delta\alpha}\mathbf{1}_{j^{n}_{\delta\alpha\geq K}}\leq\epsilon_{\delta}(K)f^{n}_{\alpha}
for some
\epsilon_{\delta}(K)\rightarrow 0
as
K\rightarrow+\infty
. By (
6.1
)-(
6.2
), Arkeryd inequality (
4.1
) and Lemma
3.11
, we obtain that for all
\varphi(x,\xi,I)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
,

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tilde{Q}^{-}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})^{\sharp}\mathrm{exp}(-(\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t)-\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(\tau)))|\varphi|dxd\xi dI\leq C_{T}\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}\,.

Then for all
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
and

(6.16)

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\varphi||\int_{0}^{t}(j^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}-j^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}\wedge K)\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})^{\sharp}\mathrm{exp}(-(\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t)-\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(\tau)))d\tau|dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\delta}(K)\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tilde{Q}^{-}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})^{\sharp}\mathrm{exp}(-(\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t)-\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(\tau)))|\varphi|dxd\xi dI\rightarrow 0

as
K\rightarrow\infty
. Due to
j^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}\wedge K,j^{K\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}\in L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))
and
\varphi L_{\alpha}(f)\in L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))
, we obtain
j^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}\wedge K
weakly-* converges to
j^{K\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}
and

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}(j^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}\wedge K-j^{K\sharp}_{\delta\alpha})\varphi L_{\alpha}(f)dxd\xi dI=0.

As a result, together with Lemma
5.8
,

\displaystyle|\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}(j^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}\wedge K\cdot\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})^{\sharp}-j^{K\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}L_{\alpha}(f)^{\sharp})\varphi dxd\xi dI|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}\varphi(j^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}\wedge K-j^{K\sharp}_{\delta\alpha})L_{\alpha}(f)dxd\xi dI|

\displaystyle+K\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}|\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})^{\sharp}-L_{\alpha}(f)^{\sharp}|dxd\xi dI\rightarrow 0

as
n\rightarrow+\infty
. That means

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{t}j^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}\wedge K\cdot\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})^{\sharp}d\tau=\int_{0}^{t}j^{K\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}L_{\alpha}(f)^{\sharp}d\tau\quad\text{weakly in}\ L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)).

Using the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
6.1
, we obtain

(6.17)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{t}j^{n\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}\wedge K\cdot\tilde{L}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n})^{\sharp}\mathrm{exp}(-(\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(t)-\tilde{F}^{\sharp}_{\alpha,n}(\tau)))d\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}j^{K\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}L_{\alpha}(f)^{\sharp}\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)))d\tau\quad\text{weakly in}\ L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)).

Then by the strong convergence (
6.11
) and Egorov’s theorem, for all
\epsilon>0
there exists a Borel set
E\subset[0,t]\times B_{R^{\prime}}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)
such that
|E^{c}|=|[0,t]\times B_{R^{\prime}}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)\setminus E|<\epsilon
and
j^{K\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}
converges uniformly to
j^{\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}
on
E
. Notices that
0\leq j^{n}_{\delta\alpha}\leq f^{n}_{\alpha}
. We know that the limit
0\leq j_{\delta\alpha}\leq f_{\alpha}
in the sense of distribution. It further holds for almost all
(t,x,\xi,I)\in(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)
. Recall that
j_{\delta\alpha}^{K}\leq j_{\delta\alpha}\wedge K\leq j_{\delta\alpha}\leq f_{\alpha}
. Therefore, for any given
R,R^{\prime}>0
and
t\geq 0
,

(6.18)

\displaystyle|\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\iiint_{B_{R^{\prime}}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}(j^{K\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}-j^{\sharp}_{\delta\alpha})L_{\alpha}(f)^{\sharp}\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)))dxd\xi dI|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{E}|j^{K\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}-j^{\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}|\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\iiint_{B_{R^{\prime}}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}L_{\alpha}(f)^{\sharp}dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\int_{E^{c}}Q^{-}_{\alpha}(f,f)^{\sharp}\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)))dxd\xi dId\tau\rightarrow 0\ (\text{as }K\rightarrow+\infty,\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+})\,.

Moreover, due to
Q^{-}_{\alpha}(f,f)^{\sharp}\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)))\in L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))
, one has

(6.19)

\displaystyle|\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\iiint_{\{|x|>R^{\prime}\}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}(j^{K\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}-j^{\sharp}_{\delta\alpha})L_{\alpha}(f)^{\sharp}\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)))dxd\xi dI|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\iiint_{\{|x|>R^{\prime}\}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)}Q^{-}_{\alpha}(f,f)^{\sharp}\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)))dxd\xi dI\rightarrow 0\ (R^{\prime}\to+\infty)\,.

As a result, the convergences (
6.16
), (
6.17
), (
6.18
) and (
6.19
) reduce to the convergence (
6.15
). Moreover, the corresponding result (
6.14
) for the cases
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
can be analogously proved. Then the proof of Lemma
6.4
is finished.
∎

Lemma 6.5
.

f
is a subsolution of (
1.1
) given in Definition
6.2
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we only consider the cases
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
. Lemma
4.2
shows that
(1+\delta f^{n}_{\alpha})^{-1}\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})
is relatively weakly compact in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))
for any given
R,T>0
. Then for
t>0
there is
Q^{+}_{\delta\alpha}\in L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))
such that

(6.20)

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\tfrac{\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})}{1+\delta f^{n}_{\alpha}}=Q^{+}_{\delta\alpha}\quad\text{weakly in }\ L^{1}((0,t)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)).

For all
t,R>0
, we take
\varphi\in L^{\infty}((0,t)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})_{+}
with support set contained in
(0,t)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)
. Then Lemma
5.9
implies that there exists a Borel set
E\subset(0,t)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)
such that
|E^{c}|<\epsilon
and

\displaystyle\int_{E}Q^{+}_{\delta\alpha}\varphi dtdxd\xi dI=\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{E}\tfrac{\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})}{1+\delta f^{n}_{\alpha}}\varphi dtdxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{E}\tilde{Q}^{+}_{\alpha,n}(f^{n},f^{n})\varphi dtdxd\xi dI=\int_{E}Q^{+}_{\alpha}(f,f)\varphi dtdxd\xi dI,

which means that

(6.21)

Q^{+}_{\delta\alpha}\leq Q^{+}_{\alpha}(f,f),\ a.e.\ (t,x,\xi,I)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}

by letting
\epsilon\to 0^{+}
and
t,R\to+\infty
.

As
n\to+\infty
, from taking
L^{1}
weak limit in both sides of (
6.10
) and using (
6.15
), (
6.20
) and (
6.21
), we derive that for all
t\geq 0
and almost everywhere
(x,\xi,I)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\tilde{g}^{\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)-\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha,0}(x,\xi,I)\mathrm{exp}(-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\{j^{\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(\tau)L_{\alpha}(f)^{\sharp}(\tau)+Q^{+}_{\alpha}(f,f)^{\sharp}(\tau)\}\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)))d\tau.

Here
\tilde{g}_{\delta\alpha}
is the
L^{1}
weak limit of
g^{n}_{\delta\alpha}
.

Observe that for
M>0
,

\displaystyle 0\leq j^{n}_{\delta\alpha}\leq f^{n}_{\alpha}-\tfrac{f^{n}_{\alpha}}{1+\delta f^{n}_{\alpha}}\leq\delta Mf^{n}_{\alpha}+\mathbf{1}_{f^{n}_{\alpha}\geq M}f^{n}_{\alpha}\,.

We then gain

\displaystyle 0\leq j_{\delta\alpha}\leq\delta Mf_{\alpha}+k_{M\alpha}\,,

where
k_{M\alpha}\geq 0
is the
L^{1}
weak limit of
\mathbf{1}_{f^{n}_{\alpha}\geq M}f^{n}_{\alpha}
. Then the
L^{1}
weak compactness of
f^{n}_{\alpha}
implies that for all
\varphi\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})_{+}
,

0\leq\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}k_{M\alpha}\varphi dxd\xi dI\leq\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\varphi f^{n}_{\alpha}\mathbf{1}_{f^{n}_{\alpha}\geq M}dxd\xi dI\rightarrow 0\ (M\rightarrow+\infty).

This further indicates that
k_{M\alpha}\to 0
for almost all
(t,x,\xi,I)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
as
M\to+\infty
. Consequently, for all
\varphi\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})_{+}
, as
\delta\to 0^{+}
and
M\to+\infty
,

(6.22)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\varphi j^{\sharp}_{\delta\alpha}(\tau)L_{\alpha}(f)^{\sharp}(\tau)\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)))dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta M\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\varphi Q_{\alpha}^{-}(f,f)^{\sharp}(\tau)\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)))dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\varphi k^{\sharp}_{M\alpha}L_{\alpha}(f)^{\sharp}(\tau)\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau)))dxd\xi dI\to 0,

where the fact (
6.13
) and the Lebesgue’s Control Convergence Theorem have been used. Together with Lemma
5.5
, one concludes the inequality (
6.9
) for the cases
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
in Definition
6.2
. Moreover, the inequality (
6.8
) for the cases
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
in Definition
6.2
can be proved analogously above. Therefore, the proof of Lemma
6.5
is finished.
∎

6.3.
Proof of existence of the renormalized solution to (
1.1
)

Combining with Lemma
6.3
and
6.5
, we know that for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}Q^{+}_{\alpha}(f,f)^{\sharp}(\tau,x,\xi)\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau,x,\xi)))d\tau

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi)-f_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi)\mathrm{exp}(-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi))\quad a.e.\ (x,\xi)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}

with all
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
, and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}Q^{+}_{\alpha}(f,f)^{\sharp}(\tau,x,\xi,I)\mathrm{exp}(-(F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(\tau,x,\xi,I)))d\tau

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I)-f_{\alpha,0}(x,\xi,I)\mathrm{exp}(-F^{\sharp}_{\alpha}(t,x,\xi,I))\quad a.e.\ (x,\xi,I)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}

with all
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
. Together with Lemma
2.10
and
5.8
, we see that the
L^{1}
weak limit
f
of
f^{n}
is a mild solution to (
1.1
). At the end, Lemma
2.9
, Lemma
5.12
and Corollary
5.5
show that
f
is actually a renormalized solution of (
1.1
).

7.
Entropy inequality

In this section, we mainly prove the Part (3) of Theorem
1.1
. Namely, we will prove the renormalized solution
f
of (
1.1
) satisfies the entropy inequality (
1.50
), i.e.,

(7.1)

\displaystyle H(f)(t)+\tfrac{1}{4}\sum\limits_{\alpha=1}^{s_{0}}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}e_{\alpha}(\tau,x,\xi)dxd\xi

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{4}\sum\limits_{\alpha=s_{0}+1}^{s}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}e_{\alpha}(\tau,x,\xi,I)dxd\xi dI\leq H(f_{0}),

where
e_{\alpha}
is defined in (
1.51
).

As in (
3.31
) of Lemma
3.11
, the approximated distributional solution
f_{n}
to the approximated problem (
3.1
) admits the entropy identity. Namely, for all
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle H(f^{n}_{0})

\displaystyle=H(f^{n})(t)+\tfrac{1}{4}\sum\limits_{\alpha=1}^{s_{0}}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tilde{e}_{\alpha}^{n}(\tau,x,\xi)dxd\xi

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{4}\sum\limits_{\alpha=s_{0}+1}^{s}\int_{0}^{t}d\tau\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tilde{e}_{\alpha}^{n}(\tau,x,\xi,I)dxd\xi dI,

where
\tilde{e}_{\alpha}^{n}
and
H(f^{n})
are defined in (
3.32
) and (
1.45
), respectively. Note that Lemma
2.6
and Lemma
3.11
imply
\liminf_{n\to\infty}H(f^{n})(t)\geq H(f)(t)
. Moreover, Lemma
3.1
and Lemma
3.2
indicate that
\lim_{n\to+\infty}H(f^{n}_{0})=H(f_{0})
. In order to prove the entropy inequality (
1.50
) (or (
7.1
)), we only need to show that for almost all
(t,x)\in(0,+\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

(7.2)

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tilde{e}_{\alpha}^{n}d\xi

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e_{\alpha}d\xi\quad\qquad\text{ for }\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\},

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tilde{e}_{\alpha}^{n}d\xi dI

\displaystyle\geq\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}e_{\alpha}d\xi dI\quad\,\text{for }\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}.

For notational simplicity, we introduce the following symbols.

For
\alpha,\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, let

\displaystyle E_{R}

\displaystyle=B_{R}\times B_{R}\times[0,1]\times[0,1]\times(0,R)\times(0,R)\times\mathbb{S}^{2},

\displaystyle d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{r}^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{r})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{R})^{(\delta(\alpha)+\delta(\beta))/2-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}d\xi d\xi_{*}d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}dIdI_{*}d\omega,

\displaystyle d\mu_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}\,,d\mu_{\alpha\beta}^{n}=B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta},

where
B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}=B_{2\alpha\beta}\wedge R=\min\{B_{2\alpha\beta},R\}
and
B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}=B_{2\alpha\beta,n}\wedge R=\min\{B_{2\alpha\beta,n},R\}
.

For
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
and
\beta\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
, let

\displaystyle E_{R}

\displaystyle=B_{R}\times B_{R}\times[0,1]\times(0,R)\times\mathbb{S}^{2},

\displaystyle d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=(1-\mathfrak{R})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}d\xi d\xi_{*}d\mathfrak{R}dId\omega,d\mu_{\alpha\beta}=B^{R}_{1\beta\alpha}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}\,,d\mu_{\alpha\beta}^{n}=B^{R}_{1\beta\alpha,n}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta},

where
B^{R}_{1\beta\alpha}=B_{1\beta\alpha}\wedge R=\min\{B_{1\beta\alpha},R\}
and
B^{R}_{1\beta\alpha,n}=B_{1\beta\alpha,n}\wedge R=\min\{B_{1\beta\alpha,n},R\}
.

For
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\},\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
, let

\displaystyle E_{R}

\displaystyle=B_{R}\times B_{R}\times[0,1]\times(0,R)\times\mathbb{S}^{2},

\displaystyle d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=(1-\mathfrak{R})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}d\xi d\xi_{*}d\mathfrak{R}dI_{*}d\omega,d\mu_{\alpha\beta}=B^{R}_{1\alpha\beta}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta},d\mu_{\alpha\beta}^{n}=B^{R}_{1\alpha\beta,n}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta},

where
B^{R}_{1\alpha\beta,n}=B_{1\alpha\beta,n}\wedge R=\min\{B_{1\alpha\beta,n},R\}
and
B^{R}_{1\alpha\beta}=B_{1\alpha\beta}\wedge R=\min\{B_{1\alpha\beta},R\}
.

For
\alpha,\beta\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
, let

\displaystyle E_{R}=B_{R}\times B_{R}\times\mathbb{S}^{2},\ d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}=d\xi d\xi_{*}d\omega,d\mu_{\alpha\beta}=B^{R}_{0\alpha\beta}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta},d\mu_{\alpha\beta}^{n}=B^{R}_{0\alpha\beta,n}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta},

where
B^{R}_{0\alpha\beta}=B_{0\alpha\beta}\wedge R=\min\{B_{0\alpha\beta},R\}
and
B^{R}_{0\alpha\beta,n}=B_{0\alpha\beta,n}\wedge R=\min\{B_{0\alpha\beta,n},R\}
.

Moreover, for
\alpha,\beta\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
and
R>0
, denote by

(7.3)

\displaystyle H^{n,R}_{\alpha\beta}(f^{n})

\displaystyle=\int_{E_{R}}\mathfrak{F}(f_{\alpha}^{n},f_{\beta}^{n})d\mu_{\alpha\beta}^{n},\ H_{\alpha\beta}^{R}(f)=\int_{E_{R}}\mathfrak{F}(f_{\alpha},f_{\beta})d\mu_{\alpha\beta},

\displaystyle H_{\alpha\beta}^{n}(f^{n})

\displaystyle=\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})^{4}}\mathfrak{F}(f_{\alpha}^{n},f_{\beta}^{n})W_{\alpha\beta,n}d\xi d\xi_{*}d\xi^{\prime}d\xi_{*}^{\prime}dIdI_{*}dI^{\prime}dI_{*}^{\prime},

\displaystyle H_{\alpha\beta}(f)

\displaystyle=\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})^{4}}\mathfrak{F}(f_{\alpha},f_{\beta})W_{\alpha\beta}d\xi d\xi_{*}d\xi^{\prime}d\xi_{*}^{\prime}dIdI_{*}dI^{\prime}dI_{*}^{\prime},

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}(f_{\alpha},f_{\beta})=(\tfrac{{f^{\prime}_{\alpha}}{f^{\prime}_{\beta*}}}{(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}-\tfrac{f_{\alpha}f_{\beta*}}{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}})\log(\tfrac{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}{f^{\prime}_{\alpha}}{f^{\prime}_{\beta*}}}{f_{\alpha}f_{\beta*}(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}})\,.

We remark that
\int_{\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha}}\tilde{e}_{\alpha}^{n}d\mathbf{Z}_{\alpha}=N_{n}(f^{n})^{-1}\sum_{\beta=1}^{s}H_{\alpha\beta}^{n}(f^{n})
by (
3.32
), where
N_{n}(f^{n})
is defined in (
3.17
). Here
\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha}=\mathbb{R}^{3}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{\alpha}=\xi
for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
, and
\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha}=\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{\alpha}=(\xi,I)
for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
. Denote by
\tilde{H}_{\alpha\beta}^{n}(f^{n})=N_{n}(f^{n})^{-1}H_{\alpha\beta}^{n}(f^{n})
. In order to show (
7.2
), it suffices to prove that for
\alpha,\beta\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
and for almost all
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

(7.4)

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to+\infty}\tilde{H}_{\alpha\beta}^{n}(f^{n})\geq H_{\alpha\beta}(f)\,.

Without loss of generality, we only prove the cases
\alpha,\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
.

To simplify the notation, we write

\displaystyle A^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}=\int_{[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}\mathfrak{r}^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{r})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{R})^{\delta(\alpha)+\delta(\beta)/2-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega,

\displaystyle A^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}=\int_{[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}\mathfrak{r}^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{r})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{R})^{\delta(\alpha)+\delta(\beta)/2-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega,

\displaystyle L^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})=\iint_{B_{R}\times(0,R)}f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}A^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}d\xi_{*}dI_{*}\,,L^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}(f)=\iint_{B_{R}\times(0,R)}f_{\beta_{*}}A^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}d\xi_{*}dI_{*}.

Step 1:

The sequences
\frac{{f_{\alpha}^{n}}^{\prime}{f_{\beta*}^{n}}^{\prime}}{(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}
and
\frac{f^{n}_{\alpha}f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}}{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}
are relatively weakly compact in
L^{1}(E_{R},d\Theta_{\alpha\beta})
for almost all
(t,x)\in(0,R)\times B_{R}
.

We first let

(7.5)

N(f)=1+\sum_{\alpha=1}^{s_{0}}\int_{B_{R}}f_{\alpha}d\xi+\sum_{\alpha=s_{0}+1}^{s}\iint_{B_{R}\times(0,R)}f_{\alpha}d\xi dI\,,

and

\displaystyle P^{n}_{\alpha\beta}=N(f^{n})^{-1}\tfrac{f^{n}_{\alpha}f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}}{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{P}^{n}_{\alpha\beta}=N(f^{n})^{-1}\tfrac{{f_{\alpha}^{n}}^{\prime}{f_{\beta*}^{n}}^{\prime}}{(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}\,,

\displaystyle P_{\alpha\beta}=N(f)^{-1}\tfrac{f_{\alpha}f_{\beta_{*}}}{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{P}_{\alpha\beta}=N(f)^{-1}\tfrac{{f^{\prime}_{\alpha}}f^{\prime}_{\beta*}}{(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}\,.

Then

\displaystyle\int_{E_{R}}P^{n}_{\alpha\beta}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}=\frac{1}{N(f^{n})}\iint_{B_{R}\times(0,R)}f^{n}_{\alpha}L^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})d\xi dI\,,\int_{E_{R}}P_{\alpha\beta}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}=\frac{1}{N(f)}\iint_{B_{R}\times(0,R)}f_{\alpha}L^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}(f)d\xi dI\,.

Now, we take an increasing function
\Psi\in C(\mathbb{R}_{+})
with

(7.6)

\Psi(t)\rightarrow+\infty\text{ as }t\rightarrow+\infty,\quad\Psi(t)(\log t)^{-1}\rightarrow 0\text{ as }t\rightarrow+\infty.

This means that for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists constant
M
such that
t\geq M
,
\Psi(t)\leq\epsilon\log t.
Observing that

\displaystyle\|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha}\Psi(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha})d\xi dI-\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f_{\alpha}\Psi(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f_{\alpha})d\xi dI\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(f^{n}_{\alpha}\Psi(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha})\mathbf{1}_{I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha}\leq M}-f_{\alpha}\Psi(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f_{\alpha})\mathbf{1}_{I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f_{\alpha}\leq M})d\xi dI\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+\epsilon\sup_{n\geq 1}\|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}(f^{n}_{\alpha}|\log I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha}|+f_{\alpha}|\log I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f_{\alpha}|)d\xi dI\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Since the continuous function
\Psi(t)
is bounded on
[0,M]
, combined with the bound of
f^{n}_{\alpha}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha})
in
L^{\infty}((0,T);L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}))
and Lemma
5.6
, we conclude that the inequality above tends to
0
as
n\to\infty
. This implies that the sequence
\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha}\Psi(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha})d\xi dI
is compact in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Thus, we can derive that

\displaystyle N(f^{n})^{-1}\int_{E_{R}}\tfrac{f_{\alpha}^{n}f_{\beta*}^{n}}{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}

\displaystyle(\Psi(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f_{\alpha}^{n})+\Psi(I_{*}^{1-\delta(\beta)/2}f_{\beta*}^{n}))B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle N(f^{n})^{-1}\int_{E_{R}}\tfrac{{f_{\alpha}^{n}}^{\prime}{f_{\beta*}^{n}}^{\prime}}{(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}

\displaystyle(\Psi((I^{\prime})^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}{f_{\alpha}^{n}}^{\prime})+\Psi((I^{\prime}_{*})^{1-\delta(\beta)/2}{f_{\beta_{*}}^{n}}^{\prime}))B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}

are compact in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
.

We claim that if the sequence
f^{n}(x)\geq 0
is compact in
L^{1}(\Omega)
, where
\Omega
is a bounded set. Then there exists a function
g(x)\in L^{1}(\Omega)
such that
0\leq f^{n}(x)\leq g(x)
a.e.
\Omega.
Specifically, since
f^{n}
is compact in
L^{1}(\Omega)
, then there exists a function
f\in L^{1}(\Omega)
such that
\lim_{n\to+\infty}\|f^{n}-f\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}=0
( up to a subsequence if necessary). It also converges almost everywhere, i.e., for any fixed
x\in\Omega
, there exists
N_{x}\in\mathbb{N},
if
n\geq N_{x},\ f^{n}(x)\leq f(x)+1
. Then it is easy to find that
g(x)=\max\{f(x)+1,f_{1}(x),\cdots,f_{N_{x}}(x)\}\in L^{1}(\Omega)
and
0\leq f^{n}(x)\leq g(x)
a.e.
\Omega.
The claim is therefore holds. As a result, there exist nonnegative functions
\bar{K},\bar{N}\in L^{1}((0,R)\times B_{R})
, independent of
n
, such that

\displaystyle N(f^{n})^{-1}\int_{E_{R}}\tfrac{f_{\alpha}^{n}f_{\beta*}^{n}}{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}(\Psi(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}

\displaystyle f_{\alpha}^{n})+\Psi(I_{*}^{1-\delta(\beta)/2}f_{\beta*}^{n}))B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}\leq\bar{K},

\displaystyle N(f^{n})^{-1}\int_{E_{R}}\tfrac{{f_{\alpha}^{n}}^{\prime}{f_{\beta*}^{n}}^{\prime}}{(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}(\Psi((I^{\prime})^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle f_{\alpha}^{n}}^{\prime})+\Psi((I^{\prime}_{*})^{1-\delta(\beta)/2}{f_{\beta_{*}}^{n}}^{\prime}))B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}\leq\bar{K},

\displaystyle N(f^{n})

\displaystyle\leq\bar{N}.

Besides, for all
M>0
and any Borel subset
A
in
E_{R}
, one knows that for almost all
(t,x)\in(0,R)\times B_{R}
,

\displaystyle\int_{A}\tfrac{f_{\alpha}^{n}f_{\beta*}^{n}}{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle RM^{2}\Theta_{\alpha\beta}(A)+\int_{E_{R}}\tfrac{f_{\alpha}^{n}f_{\beta*}^{n}}{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}(\mathbf{1}_{I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f_{\alpha}^{n}\geq M}+\mathbf{1}_{I_{*}^{1-\delta(\beta)/2}f_{\beta*}^{n}\geq M})B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle RM^{2}\Theta_{\alpha\beta}(A)+\Psi(M)^{-1}\bar{K}\bar{N},

which means that the sequence
\frac{f^{n}_{\alpha}f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}}{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}
is relatively weakly compact in
L^{1}(E_{R},d\Theta_{\alpha\beta})
for almost all
(t,x)\in(0,R)\times B_{R}
. By employing the analogous arguments above, the sequence
\frac{{f_{\alpha}^{n}}^{\prime}{f_{\beta*}^{n}}^{\prime}}{(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}
is also relatively weakly compact in
L^{1}(E_{R},d\Theta_{\alpha\beta})
for almost all
(t,x)\in(0,R)\times B_{R}
.

Step 2:

(7.7)

\int_{E_{R}}P^{n}_{\alpha\beta}\varphi d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}\rightarrow\int_{E_{R}}P_{\alpha\beta}\varphi d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}\quad\text{strongly in }L^{1}((0,R)\times B_{R}),

(7.8)

\int_{E_{R}}\tilde{P}^{n}_{\alpha\beta}\varphi d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}\rightarrow\int_{E_{R}}\tilde{P}_{\alpha\beta}\varphi d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}\quad\text{strongly in }L^{1}((0,R)\times B_{R})

for all
\varphi\in L^{\infty}(E_{R})
.

It is easy to find that
A^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}\in L^{\infty}((0,R)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R);L^{1}(B_{R}\times(0,R)))
, and

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\|A^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}-A^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}\|_{L^{1}(B_{R}\times(0,R))}=0

for almost all
(t,x,\xi_{*},I_{*})\in(0,R)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)
. Following the analogous arguments in Lemma
5.8
, one has

L^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})\rightarrow L^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}(f)\quad\text{strongly in }L^{1}((0,R)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)).

Then the convergence (
5.2
) and Lemma
5.5
indicate that
N(f^{n})^{-1}
strongly converges to
N(f)^{-1}
in
L^{1}((0,R)\times B_{R})
as
n\to+\infty
. Then the Egorov’s theorem indicates that for any
\epsilon>0
there exists a Borel
E\subset\Omega=(0,R)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R)
such that
|E^{c}\cap\Omega|<\epsilon
,
L^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})
uniformly converges to
L^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}(f)
and
N(f^{n})^{-1}
uniformly converges to
N(f)^{-1}
on
E
.

Observe that

\displaystyle f^{n}_{\alpha}L^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})N(f^{n})^{-1}-f_{\alpha}L^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}(f)N(f)^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f^{n}_{\alpha}\{L^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})N(f^{n})^{-1}-L^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}(f)N(f)^{-1}\}+L^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}(f)N(f)^{-1}(f^{n}_{\alpha}-f_{\alpha})\,.

We then obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{R}dt\int_{B_{R}}dx|\int_{E_{R}}P^{n}_{\alpha\beta}\varphi d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}-\int_{E_{R}}P_{\alpha\beta}\varphi d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\|f^{n}_{\alpha}\|_{L^{1}((0,R)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}\sup\limits_{E}|L^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})N(f^{n})^{-1}-L^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}(f)N(f)^{-1}|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(\sup_{n\geq 1}\|A^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|A^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}\|_{L^{\infty}})(\sup_{n\geq 1}\int_{E^{c}}f^{n}_{\alpha}dtdxd\xi dI+\int_{E^{c}}f_{\alpha}dtdxd\xi dI)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{R}dt\int_{B_{R}}dx|\iint_{B_{R}\times(0,R)}f^{n}_{\alpha}\tilde{\varphi}d\xi dI-\iint_{B_{R}\times(0,R)}f_{\alpha}\tilde{\varphi}d\xi dI|,

where
\tilde{\varphi}=\mathbf{1}_{E}L^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}(f)N(f)^{-1}\in L^{\infty}
. Note that
\sup_{n\geq 1}\|f^{n}_{\alpha}\|_{L^{1}((0,R)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))}\leq C_{R}
by Lemma
3.11
, one knows that the first quantity in the right-hand side of above inequality goes to 0 as
n\to+\infty
. Moreover, the convergence (
5.2
) and Lemma
5.5
show that the last inequality in the right-hand side of above inequality goes to 0 as
n\to+\infty
. Furthermore,
\sup_{n\geq 1}\|A^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|A^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq 2R
and
f^{n}_{\alpha}
weakly converges to
f_{\alpha}
in
L^{1}((0,R)\times B_{R}\times B_{R}\times(0,R))
, which imply that the second inequality in the right-hand side of above inequality goes to 0 as
\epsilon\to 0^{+}
. Thus the assertion (
7.7
) holds.

We now prove the convergence (
7.8
). Observe that

\displaystyle\int_{E_{R}}N(f^{n})^{-1}\tilde{P}^{n}_{\alpha\beta}\varphi d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}=N(f^{n})^{-1}\iint_{B_{R}\times(0,R)}Q^{R,+}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n},f^{n})\varphi d\xi dI\,,

\displaystyle\int_{E_{R}}N(f)^{-1}\tilde{P}_{\alpha\beta}\varphi d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}=N(f)^{-1}\iint_{B_{R}\times(0,R)}Q^{R,+}_{\alpha\beta}(f,f)\varphi d\xi dI\,,

where
Q^{R,+}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n},f^{n})
is formed by replacing the collision kernel
B_{2\alpha\beta,n}
of
Q^{+}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n},f^{n})
by
B_{2\alpha\beta,n}^{R}
, and
Q^{R,+}_{\alpha\beta}(f,f)
is defined by replacing the collision kernel
B_{2\alpha\beta}
of
Q^{+}_{\alpha\beta}(f,f)
by
B_{2\alpha\beta}^{R}
. Recalling the identity (
3.18
), one similarly has

\displaystyle\iint_{B_{R}\times(0,R)}Q^{R,+}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n},f^{n})\varphi d\xi dI=

\displaystyle\int_{(B_{R})^{2}\times(0,R)^{2}\times[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}f^{n}_{\alpha}f^{n}_{\beta*}B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}\mathfrak{r}^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}

\displaystyle\times(1-\mathfrak{r})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{R})^{\delta(\alpha)+\delta(\beta)/2-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}\varphi^{\prime}d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega d\xi d\xi_{*}dIdI_{*},

\displaystyle\iint_{B_{R}\times(0,R)}Q^{R,+}_{\alpha\beta}(f,f)\varphi d\xi dI=

\displaystyle\int_{(B_{R})^{2}\times(0,R)^{2}\times[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}f_{\alpha}f_{\beta*}B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}\mathfrak{r}^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}

\displaystyle\times(1-\mathfrak{r})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{R})^{\delta(\alpha)+\delta(\beta)/2-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}\varphi^{\prime}d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega d\xi d\xi_{*}dIdI_{*},

where
\varphi^{\prime}=\varphi(\xi^{\prime},I^{\prime})
. We now introduce

\displaystyle\hat{A}^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}(t,x,\xi,\xi_{*},I,I_{*})=\int_{[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}\mathfrak{r}^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{r})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{R})^{\delta(\alpha)+\delta(\beta)/2-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}\varphi^{\prime}d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega,

\displaystyle\hat{A}^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}(t,x,\xi,\xi_{*},I,I_{*})=\int_{[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}\mathfrak{r}^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{r})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}(1-\mathfrak{R})^{\delta(\alpha)+\delta(\beta)/2-1}\mathfrak{R}^{1/2}\varphi^{\prime}d\mathfrak{R}d\mathfrak{r}d\omega,

\displaystyle\bar{L}^{R}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})(t,x,\xi,I)=\iint_{B_{R}\times(0,R)}\hat{A}^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}f^{n}_{\beta*}d\xi_{*}dI_{*}\,,

\displaystyle\bar{L}^{R}_{\alpha\beta}(f)(t,x,\xi,I)=\iint_{B_{R}\times(0,R)}\hat{A}^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}f_{\beta*}d\xi_{*}dI_{*}.

Then

\displaystyle\int_{E_{R}}\tilde{P}^{n}_{\alpha\beta}\varphi d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}=N(f^{n})^{-1}\iint_{B_{R}\times(0,R)}f^{n}_{\alpha}\bar{L}^{R}_{\alpha\beta,n}(f^{n})d\xi dI\,,

\displaystyle\int_{E_{R}}\tilde{P}_{\alpha\beta}\varphi d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}=N(f)^{-1}\iint_{B_{R}\times(0,R)}f_{\alpha}\bar{L}^{R}_{\alpha\beta}(f)d\xi dI\,.

Then, by following the analogous arguments of proving the convergence (
7.7
), one can infer that the convergence (
7.8
) holds.

Step 3:

For almost all
(t,x)\in(0,R)\times B_{R}
,

(7.9)

\displaystyle N_{n}(f^{n})^{-1}\tfrac{f^{n}_{\alpha}f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}}{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\tfrac{f_{\alpha}f_{\beta_{*}}}{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}\,,

\displaystyle N_{n}(f^{n})^{-1}\tfrac{{f_{\alpha}^{n}}^{\prime}{f_{\beta*}^{n}}^{\prime}}{(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\tfrac{{f^{\prime}_{\alpha}}f^{\prime}_{\beta*}}{(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta}\,

weakly in
L^{1}(E_{R},d\Theta_{\alpha\beta})
.

The strong convergence of
\int_{E_{R}}P^{n}_{\alpha\beta}\varphi d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}
implies that there exists a subsequence converging pointwise a.e. in
(t,x)
to
\int_{E_{R}}P_{\alpha\beta}\varphi d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}
. However, this subsequence may depend on
\varphi
.

In fact, for any Borel subset
A
of
E_{R}
, there exists a sequence of measurable subsets
\{A_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
such that
\mathbf{1}_{A_{k}}\to\mathbf{1}_{A}
in
L^{1}(E_{R})
. Letting
\varphi=\mathbf{1}_{A_{k}}
and applying to the strong convergence of
\int_{E_{R}}P^{n}_{\alpha\beta}\mathbf{1}_{A_{k}}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}
, we can extract a subsequence (still denoted by
\int_{E_{R}}P^{n}_{\alpha\beta}\mathbf{1}_{A_{k}}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}
) such that

\int_{E_{R}}P^{n}_{\alpha\beta}\mathbf{1}_{A_{k}}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}\to\int_{E_{R}}P_{\alpha\beta}\mathbf{1}_{A_{k}}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}\quad\text{a.e.}\ (t,x)\in(0,R)\times B_{R}.

Then for any Borel subset
A
of
E_{R}
and any constant
M>0
,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{E_{R}}P^{n}_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{1}_{A_{k}}-\mathbf{1}_{A})d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M^{2}\int_{E_{R}}|\mathbf{1}_{A_{k}}-\mathbf{1}_{A}|d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}+\int_{E_{R}}f^{n}_{\alpha}f^{n}_{\beta_{*}}(\mathbf{1}_{f^{n}_{\alpha}\geq M}+\mathbf{1}_{f_{\beta_{*}}^{n}\geq M})B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M^{2}\int_{E_{R}}|\mathbf{1}_{A_{k}}-\mathbf{1}_{A}|d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}+\Psi(M)^{-1}\bar{K}\to 0\quad(k\to\infty,\ M\to\infty).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{E_{R}}P^{n}_{\alpha\beta}\mathbf{1}_{A}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}-\int_{E_{R}}P_{\alpha\beta}\mathbf{1}_{A}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\int_{E_{R}}P^{n}_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{1}_{A_{k}}-\mathbf{1}_{A})d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}\right|+\left|\int_{E_{R}}(P^{n}_{\alpha\beta}-P_{\alpha\beta})\mathbf{1}_{A_{k}}d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}\right|+\left|\int_{E_{R}}P_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{1}_{A_{k}}-\mathbf{1}_{A})d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}\right|

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle 0\quad(k\to\infty,\ n\to\infty).

Since the above holds for all measurable subset
A
, then for almost every
(t,x)\in(0,R)\times B_{R}
and for all
\varphi\in L^{\infty}(E_{R})
,

\displaystyle P^{n}_{\alpha\beta}\rightarrow P_{\alpha\beta},\quad\tilde{P}^{n}_{\alpha\beta}\rightarrow\tilde{P}_{\alpha\beta},

weakly in
L^{1}(E_{R},d\Theta_{\alpha\beta})
. Besides, using the facts that
N(f^{n})(t,x)
and
\left(1+\frac{1}{n}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}f^{n}_{\alpha}d\xi dI\right)^{-1}
converge a.e. to
N(f)
and
1
, respectively (see Lemma
5.6
), one easily knows that (
7.9
) holds.

Step 4:

The convergence (
7.4
) holds for
\alpha,\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
.

We first define

j(a,b)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{rl}(a-b)\log\frac{a}{b},&\quad\text{for }a,b>0\,,\\
+\infty,&\quad\text{for }a\text{ or }b\leq 0\,,\end{array}\right.

whose the Hessian matrix

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{ab}\begin{pmatrix}b+\frac{b^{2}}{a}&-(a+b)\\
-(a+b)&a+\frac{a^{2}}{b}\end{pmatrix},\quad a,b>0

is nonnegative. We then know that
j(a,b)
is convex. We further consider the integral

(7.10)

J(F,G)=\int_{E_{R}}j(F,G)d\Theta_{\alpha\beta},\quad F,G\in L^{1}(E_{R},d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}).

The convexity of
j(a,b)
shows that

\displaystyle j(F^{n},G^{n})\geq j(a,b)+(\log\frac{a}{b}+\frac{a-b}{b})(F^{n}-a)+(\log\frac{b}{a}+\frac{b-a}{b})(G^{n}-b)

for
F^{n},G^{n},a,b>0
. We assume that
F^{n}
and
G^{n}
respectively weakly converge to
F\geq 0
and
G\geq 0
in
L^{1}(E_{R},d\Theta_{\alpha\beta})
. For any
\epsilon>0
and
M>0
, we take
a=\epsilon+F\wedge M,b=\epsilon+G\wedge M
. It then infers that

\displaystyle\liminf\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}J(F^{n},G^{n})

\displaystyle\geq\int_{E_{R}}j(a,b)+(\log\tfrac{a}{b}+\tfrac{a-b}{b})(F-a)+(\log\tfrac{b}{a}+\tfrac{b-a}{b})(G-b)d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{E_{R}}j(a,b)+(\log\tfrac{a}{b}+\tfrac{a-b}{b})(F\wedge M-a)+(\log\tfrac{b}{a}+\tfrac{b-a}{b})(G\wedge M-b)d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=\int_{E_{R}}j(a,b)d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}-\epsilon\int_{E_{R}}(a-b)(\tfrac{1}{a}-\tfrac{1}{b})d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}\geq\int_{E_{R}}j(a,b)d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}.

Hence, for any
\epsilon>0
and
M>0
,

\liminf\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{E_{R}}j(F^{n},G^{n})d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}\geq\int_{E_{R}}j(\epsilon+F\wedge M,\epsilon+G\wedge M)d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}.

By letting
\epsilon\to 0^{+}
and
M\to+\infty
, Fatou’s lemma concludes that

(7.11)

\liminf\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{E_{R}}j(F^{n},G^{n})d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}\geq\int_{E_{R}}j(F,G)d\Theta_{\alpha\beta}.

By taking
F^{n}=N_{n}(f^{n})^{-1}\frac{{f_{\alpha}^{n}}^{\prime}{f_{\beta*}^{n}}^{\prime}}{(I^{\prime})^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}(I^{\prime}_{*})^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}
,
G^{n}=N_{n}(f^{n})^{-1}\frac{f_{\alpha}^{n}f_{\beta*}^{n}}{I^{\delta(\alpha)/2-1}I_{*}^{\delta(\beta)/2-1}}B^{R}_{2\alpha\beta,n}
, one has

\liminf\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}[N_{n}(f^{n})^{-1}H^{n,R}_{\alpha\beta}(f^{n})]\geq H^{R}_{\alpha\beta}(f)\quad a.e.\kern 5.0pt(t,x)\in(0,R)\times B_{R}.

Note that

(7.12)

\displaystyle N_{n}(f^{n})^{-1}H^{n,R}_{\alpha\beta}(f^{n})\leq\tilde{H}^{n}_{\alpha\beta}(f^{n})\,,\ H_{\alpha\beta}(f^{n})\geq H^{R}_{\alpha\beta}(f^{n})\quad\text{for }R>0\,,

\displaystyle H^{R}_{\alpha\beta}(f)\uparrow H_{\alpha\beta}(f)\quad\text{as }R\uparrow+\infty.

By letting
R\to+\infty
, the convergence (
7.4
) holds for
\alpha,\beta\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
.

By the analogous arguments above, one can prove that the convergence (
7.4
) holds for
\alpha,\beta\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
. Hence the entropy inequality (
1.50
) holds. The proof of Theorem
1.1
is therefore finished.

8.
Weak lower semicontinuity of entropy: Proof of Lemma
2.6

In this section, we will prove the weak lower semicontinuity of the entropy
H(f^{n})
defined in (
1.45
), i.e., justifying Lemma
2.6
.

Proof of Lemma
2.6
.

First, we claim that for any
r>1
,

(8.1)

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha=1}^{s_{0}}\iint_{B_{r}\times B_{r}}f_{\alpha}\log f_{\alpha}dxd\xi+\sum_{\alpha=s_{0}+1}^{s}\iiint_{B_{r}\times B_{r}\times(r^{-1},r)}f_{\alpha}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f_{\alpha})dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\liminf\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\Big\{\sum_{\alpha=1}^{s_{0}}\iint_{B_{r}\times B_{r}}f^{n}_{\alpha}\log f^{n}_{\alpha}dxd\xi+\sum_{\alpha=s_{0}+1}^{s}\iiint_{B_{r}\times B_{r}\times(r^{-1},r)}f^{n}_{\alpha}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha})dxd\xi dI\Big\}.

Indeed, denote by
F_{m}(a,b)=\{(x,\xi)\in B_{r}\times B_{r}:a\leq f_{\alpha}(x,\xi)<b\}
for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
and
F_{p}(a,b)=\{(x,\xi,I)\in B_{r}\times B_{r}\times(r^{-1},r):a\leq f_{\alpha}(x,\xi,I)<b\}
for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
. Moreover, for any
\eta>0
, we divide the interval
[0,b]
into subintervals with
0=y_{0}<y_{1}<\cdots<y_{m}=b
such that for
1\leq i\leq m-1
,

(8.2)

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{y\in[y_{i-1},y_{i+1})}|y\log y-y_{i+1}\log y_{i+1}|<\tfrac{\eta}{4\mathrm{Vol}(B_{r}\times B_{r})},

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{y\in[y_{i-1},y_{i+1})}|y\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}y)-y_{i+1}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}y_{i+1})|<\tfrac{\eta}{4\mathrm{Vol}(B_{r}\times B_{r}\times(r^{-1},r))}\,.

Note that
f_{\alpha}\log f_{\alpha}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
and
f_{\alpha}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f_{\alpha})\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
. Then

(8.3)

\displaystyle|\iint_{F_{m}(0,b)}f_{\alpha}\log f_{\alpha}dxd\xi-\sum_{i=0}^{m-1}y_{i+1}\log y_{i+1}\mathrm{Vol}(F(y_{i},y_{i+1}))|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{m-1}\sup\limits_{(x,\xi)\in F_{m}(y_{i},y_{i+1})}|f_{\alpha}\log f_{\alpha}-y_{i+1}\log y_{i+1}|\mathrm{Vol}(F_{m}(y_{i},y_{i+1}))<\tfrac{\eta}{4},

and

(8.4)

\displaystyle|\iint_{F_{p}(0,b)}f_{\alpha}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f_{\alpha})dxd\xi-\sum_{i=0}^{m-1}y_{i+1}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}y_{i+1})\mathrm{Vol}(F_{p}(y_{i},y_{i+1}))|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{m-1}\sup\limits_{(x,\xi,I)\in F_{p}(y_{i},y_{i+1})}|f_{\alpha}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f_{\alpha})-y_{i+1}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}y_{i+1})|\mathrm{Vol}(F_{p}(y_{i},y_{i+1}))<\tfrac{\eta}{4}.

Furthermore,
f_{\alpha}^{n}
are
L^{1}
weakly convergent to
f_{\alpha}
for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s\}
. It therefore infers that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\iint_{F_{m}(y_{j},y_{j+1})}f_{\alpha}^{n}dxd\xi=\iint_{F_{m}(y_{j},y_{j+1})}f_{\alpha}dxd\xi\,,

\displaystyle\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}\,,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\iiint_{F_{p}(y_{j},y_{j+1})}f_{\alpha}^{n}dxd\xi dI=\iiint_{F_{p}(y_{j},y_{j+1})}f_{\alpha}dxd\xi dI\,,

\displaystyle\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}\,.

Observe that

\displaystyle y_{i}\mathrm{Vol}(F_{m}(y_{i},y_{i+1}))\leq\iint_{F_{m}(y_{j},y_{j+1})}f_{\alpha}dxd\xi<y_{j+1}\mathrm{Vol}(F_{m}(y_{j},y_{j+1})),

\displaystyle\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}\,,

\displaystyle y_{i}\mathrm{Vol}(F_{p}(y_{i},y_{i+1}))\leq\iiint_{F_{p}(y_{j},y_{j+1})}f_{\alpha}dxd\xi dI<y_{j+1}\mathrm{Vol}(F_{p}(y_{j},y_{j+1})),

\displaystyle\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}.

Consequently, for any
\delta>0
there is
n_{\delta}
such that for any
n>n_{\delta}
and
0\leq i\leq m-1
,

(8.5)

\displaystyle y_{i}-\delta\leq\tfrac{1}{\mathrm{Vol}(F_{m}(y_{i},y_{i+1}))}\iint_{F_{m}(y_{i},y_{i+1})}f^{n}_{\alpha}dxd\xi\leq y_{i+1}+\delta,

\displaystyle y_{i}-\delta\leq\tfrac{1}{\mathrm{Vol}(F_{p}(y_{i},y_{i+1}))}\iiint_{F_{p}(y_{i},y_{i+1})}f^{n}_{\alpha}dxd\xi dI\leq y_{i+1}+\delta.

By employing the Jensen’s inequality on
F_{m}(y_{j},y_{j+1})
for the convex function
\varphi(y)=y\log y,\ y=f^{n}_{\alpha}
, we get that for
n>n_{\delta}
,

\displaystyle\iint_{F_{m}(y_{i},y_{i+1})}f^{n}_{\alpha}\log f^{n}_{\alpha}\tfrac{dxd\xi}{\mathrm{Vol}(F_{m}(y_{i},y_{i+1}))}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(\iint_{F_{m}(y_{i},y_{i+1})}\tfrac{f^{n}_{\alpha}}{\mathrm{Vol}(F_{m}(y_{i},y_{i+1}))}dxd\xi)\log(\iint_{F_{m}(y_{i},y_{i+1})}\tfrac{f^{n}_{\alpha}}{\mathrm{Vol}(F_{m}(y_{i},y_{i+1}))}dxd\xi)\geq(y_{i}-\delta)\log(y_{i}-\delta)\,.

Taking
0<\delta<\min\limits_{0\leq i\leq m-1}|y_{i-1}-y_{i}|
, one follows from (
8.2
) that for
n>n_{\delta}
and
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
,

(8.6)

\displaystyle\iint_{F_{m}(y_{i},y_{i+1})}f^{n}_{\alpha}\log f^{n}_{\alpha}dxd\xi

\displaystyle\geq(y_{i+1}\log y_{i+1}-\tfrac{\eta}{4\mathrm{Vol}(B_{r}\times B_{r})})\mathrm{Vol}(F_{m}(y_{i},y_{i+1})).

Similarly, for
n>n_{\delta}
and
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
,

(8.7)

\displaystyle\iiint_{F_{p}(y_{i},y_{i+1})}f^{n}_{\alpha}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha})dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\geq(y_{i+1}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}y_{i+1})-\tfrac{\eta}{4\mathrm{Vol}(B_{r}\times B_{r}\times(r^{-1},r))})\mathrm{Vol}(F_{p}(y_{i},y_{i+1})).

Combining the estimates (
8.3
), (
8.4
), (
8.6
) and (
8.7
), one obtains that for
n>n_{\delta}
,
\eta>0
and large enough
b>0
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha=1}^{s_{0}}\iint_{F_{m}(0,b)}f^{n}_{\alpha}\log f^{n}_{\alpha}dxd\xi+\sum_{\alpha=s_{0}+1}^{s}\iiint_{F_{p}(0,b)}f^{n}_{\alpha}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha})dxd\xi dI

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha=1}^{s_{0}}\iint_{F_{m}(0,b)}f_{\alpha}\log f_{\alpha}dxd\xi+\sum_{\alpha=s_{0}+1}^{s}\iiint_{F_{p}(0,b)}f_{\alpha}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f_{\alpha})dxd\xi dI-\tfrac{s}{2}\eta\,,

which implies the claim (
8.1
) by letting
b\to+\infty
,
n\to+\infty
and
\eta\to 0^{+}
.

Denote by

\displaystyle a(r)=

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha=1}^{s_{0}}\iint_{B_{r}\times B_{r}}f_{\alpha}\log f_{\alpha}dxd\xi+\sum_{\alpha=s_{0}+1}^{s}\iiint_{B_{r}\times B_{r}\times(r^{-1},r)}f_{\alpha}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f_{\alpha})dxd\xi dI\,,

\displaystyle b_{n}(r)=

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha=1}^{s_{0}}\iint_{B_{r}\times B_{r}}f^{n}_{\alpha}\log f^{n}_{\alpha}dxd\xi+\sum_{\alpha=s_{0}+1}^{s}\iiint_{B_{r}\times B_{r}\times(r^{-1},r)}f^{n}_{\alpha}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha})dxd\xi dI\,.

Then the claim (
8.1
) indicates that
a(r)\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}b_{n}(r)
for any fixed
r>1
. Taking a subsequence
\{b_{n_{k}}(r)\}
of
\{b_{n}(r)\}
such that
\liminf_{n\to\infty}b_{n}(r)=\lim_{k\to\infty}b_{n_{k}}(r)
. Then for any
\epsilon>0
, there is a
K=K(\epsilon,r)>0
such that for all
k>K
,

\displaystyle a(r)-\epsilon<b_{n_{k}}(r)\,.

Due to
f^{n}_{\alpha}\log f^{n}_{\alpha}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
for
\alpha\in\{1,\cdots,s_{0}\}
and
f^{n}_{\alpha}\log(I^{1-\delta(\alpha)/2}f^{n}_{\alpha})\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
for
\alpha\in\{s_{0}+1,\cdots,s\}
,
\lim_{r\to+\infty}b_{n_{k}}(r)=b_{n_{k}}(\infty)
exists. Consequently, by letting
r\to+\infty
,
k\to\infty
and
\epsilon\to 0^{+}
, one has
a(\infty)\leq\lim_{k\to\infty}b_{n_{k}}(\infty)=\liminf_{n\to\infty}b_{n}(\infty)
. The proof of Lemma
2.6
is finished.
∎
